## Rulesets

`tests/test_rulesets.py` holds RS1 to RS4 and `tests/test_pack_rulesets.py` holds RS5 and RS6. RS7 to RS9 are held by `tests/test_console_api_rules.py` (the editor's routes, its refusals with their locations and the approver's 403), `tests/test_rulesets.py` (the sentences, the inert note and `policy.ruleset_compiled`), `tests/test_console_api_policy.py` (the rule behind each preview change) and `tests/test_browser_console_policy.py` (the editor and a bundle's rules in Chromium). RS11, about the hosted sandbox's welcome page, is kept with the hosted service's inventory. RS12 is held by `tests/test_console_api_agents.py`, `tests/test_browser_console_directory.py` and the console's `components/directory/agents.test.tsx`.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RS1 | One ruleset compiled twice, and once with its rules in another order | Identical bytes for all five components; validated by the real Cedar engine, never a stub |
| RS2 | A condition naming an unknown fact or argument, a value outside the declared domain, an ordered operator on text, a ninth condition, a quorum on a rule that is not REVIEW, an action no mapping serves | Refused with the field's location; the model refuses what it cannot mean before the compiler is reached |
| RS3 | A bundle carrying a ruleset, with each of the five components edited on its own while staying well-formed | Validation refuses `RULESET_MISMATCH` naming the component; a bundle without a ruleset serialises with no `ruleset` key and validates as before |
| RS4 | A REVIEW rule with a quorum beside one without | The quorum names the positional obligation the engine actually raises for that rule, asked of the engine rather than restated |
| RS5 | The endpoint, identity and SOC packs as rulesets, every combination of classification, environment, incident status and severity, criticality and argument value | Effect, reasons and whether review is owed agree with the hand-written pack on every input; every reachable effect is reached |
| RS6 | Each pack's ruleset builder, recompiled from its own ruleset | Equal to the bundle it built, and the compiler's raw limits already in the stored order; the hand-written builders stay the default fixtures derive variants from |
| RS7 | An ADMIN writes three rules (allow a workstation with an open critical incident, deny a domain controller, review production with a quorum of two), a review of open incidents and a budget, in the console's rules editor | Each rule reads as one sentence and the compiled Cedar is shown read only; the draft is created through the compiler and the store and its bundle page shows the rules, the note as text and the Cedar; validation succeeds; the preview names the rule behind each changed decision and marks a review candidate unresolved; activation succeeds; `policy.ruleset_compiled` is audited with counts |
| RS8 | A condition naming an unknown fact, a text value where a number is declared, and a note containing markup | Refused at the form with the field's location; the note renders as text and appears in no compiled component |
| RS9 | An approver requests the rules editor | 403 |
| RS10 | Every fixture host the seeder writes, read by `project()` over its facts; every input of every pack (`tests/test_pack_rulesets.py`); and every input of rules written with `is_not`, `at_least` and `at_most` at every value of every field an action's rules may name, a Long argument through the synthetic probe action, and the Defender scenario's committed rules with every value of `compromise` (`tests/test_rule_reading_operators.py`) | The reading equals the pack's stated effect on every host and names a rule for every reading that is not the default denial; over the whole input space the reading equals the kernel's policy decision, a reading owing review names a review rule, and no reading is made with a fact missing; for the other operators the review rules the reading names are exactly the obligations Cedar raised, each operator in every effect and in conjunction reads as the kernel decides, and the Defender scenario reads as allowed without review only for a compromised host under an open incident that is not a domain controller; a host with no open incident yields the names of the facts it lacked |
| RS12 | The agent page with a ruleset bundle active, and with a hand-written one | A Rules column names the rules about each governed action in the compiler's order, labelled as the reading and not the decision; the hand-written bundle adds no column and no claim |

## Approval deadline

`tests/test_approval_deadline.py` holds AD1 to AD6, AD8, AD10 and AD11, `tests/test_client_package.py` and `tests/test_examples.py` hold AD7.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AD1 | A fresh tenant | `approval_deadline_seconds` is 120 and an approval expires at `min(valid_until, created + 120 s)`; every earlier 120-second test passes unchanged |
| AD2 | Deadline 900, facts valid for an hour; decision at +500 s, retry at +800 s | The approval carries `created + 900 s`; the retry executes exactly once; a further retry returns the same execution |
| AD3 | Deadline off | `expires_at` equals the plan's `valid_until`; a decision after it is `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` with `approval_state: EXPIRED` and no mutation; a decision before it executes |
| AD4 | `--set` 119, 3601, -1, 0, a string, a float or a boolean; the current value; a HUMAN_APPROVER credential; a blank reason | `INVALID_DEADLINE`, `INVALID_STATE`, `INVALID_IDENTITY`, `INVALID_REASON`; nothing written; each success is audited as `tenant.approval_deadline_changed` with previous and new values, the reason, principal and credential ids |
| AD5 | Deadline changed while an approval is pending | The pending approval keeps its expiry; the next approval uses the new value |
| AD6 | Export after a decision made at +600 s under 900 | Replay verifies complete with no issues and reproduces every recorded effect, the review decision included |
| AD7 | Deadline 900; the sync client, the async client and the MCP example wait through an expired continuation | Each resends the same intent, keeps polling, and returns `EXECUTED` after the later decision with one mutation |
| AD8 | Console under 900 and under off | The approval page and listing say `(approval deadline)` or `(context validity)`; a decision submitted four minutes after the page rendered succeeds and executes once |
| AD10 | Sessions | A load at +8 minutes extends the session to twelve hours after that load and marks it renewed, at +5 it does not; renewal never passes the absolute cap or the credential's expiry; a revoked session is not renewed; the console re-issues both cookies with the new age and twelve quiet hours still end the session |
| AD11 | Migration and runtime catalog | Head `0027_approval_deadline`; the column is updatable and the sessions expiry is not; `renew_human_session` is the only new definer function; downgrade to 0026 and back returns every tenant to 120, passing through `0100_longer_sessions`' downgrade (sessions longer than eight hours are revoked and cut back) and upgrade |

## Approval expiry

An approval past its deadline that nothing touches again would stay `PENDING`, and its execution `PENDING_APPROVAL`, indefinitely; the recovery run expires it. `tests/test_approval_lapse.py` holds AX1 to AX10, and `console/src/routes/-home.test.tsx` and `tests/test_browser_console.py` hold the console clauses of AX9.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AX1 | A pending approval thirty-six hours past a 600-second deadline | No `execution_impact` row, no `resource_claims` row, both demo rules projecting zero, and no `approval.transition` event: the row reserves nothing and claims nothing, so nothing is lost to capacity and the defect is a lifecycle that never terminates |
| AX2 | One recovery run over it | `expired` is 1; the approval is `EXPIRED` from `PENDING` and the execution `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` from `PENDING_APPROVAL`, bound to the same approval id and plan hash; no impact row, no claim and no provider mutation appear |
| AX3 | Two further runs | `expired` is 0 each time, the chain is byte-identical to after the first run, the states are unchanged and the selection is empty |
| AX4 | An approval inside its deadline, then past it but inside the run's grace | Selected by neither run; the approval stays `PENDING` and the execution `PENDING_APPROVAL` |
| AX5 | An approved-and-consumed execution and a denied one, both past the deadline | `expired` is 0; `EXECUTED`/`CONSUMED` and `DENIED`/`DENIED` are untouched and the single mutation stands |
| AX6 | An approval a human approved and the agent never came back to consume | Swept on the same terms: `EXPIRED` from `APPROVED`, execution `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`, no mutation |
| AX7 | A decision, then an agent retry, in the window after the deadline and before any run | Both answer `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` with `approval_state: EXPIRED` and no mutation; a second decision is `INVALID_STATE` |
| AX8 | A decision and a recovery run racing on two threads past the deadline | Exactly one `approval.transition` to `EXPIRED` and one `execution.transition` to `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`; the loser adds nothing and holds nothing |
| AX8b | The same race inside the deadline | The sweep always refuses under the fence and the decision always lands: `APPROVED`, execution `PENDING_APPROVAL`, no `EXPIRED` transition |
| AX9 | The pending view, the lapsed view and the overview count, before and after a run | Before: out of pending, in lapsed, overview `pending` 0 and `lapsed` 1, because the overview classifies an approval past its deadline on the server's clock. After: still out of pending, **still in lapsed**, overview `pending` 0, `lapsed` 0 and one `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`; an approval that ran out of time never disappears from the one view that says so |
| AX10 | Export and replay after a run | The verified export replays complete with no issues |

## Teammate invitations (TI1 to TI45)

Expiring, single-use links that add a workspace membership, migration `0059_workspace_invitations`. The pages are `/join`, `/join/accept` and `/join/confirm`; `/invite` belongs to the customer-directory invitation code (T4.29) and is a different feature. Real PostgreSQL throughout; TI20, TI21 and the real-Chromium Origin check below run in `tests/test_browser_invitations.py`; TI4, TI9, TI13d, TI14b, TI14c and TI14d run in the hosted service's own tests, which are not published, and TI18w and TI45, which concern the hosted plugin, are kept with the hosted service's inventory; everything else is in `tests/test_teammate_invitations.py`. The Case column names the signup design's acceptance case each row evidences (CS04, CS05, CS09, CS11 or CS12); the design is not published.

| ID | Scenario | Expected | Case |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TI1 | Migration and runtime catalog | Head `0059_workspace_invitations`; both tables forced-RLS with the runtime holding no INSERT/DELETE and UPDATE only on the messages table's seven delivery columns; the seven functions granted EXECUTE only; the drain's `owner_drain` SELECT policy names only the migration's own role, never `execbound_app`; `check_runtime` green; under another tenant's fence the runtime reads zero rows of either table while `workspace_invitation_due` still lists the message | CS05 |
| TI1a | Malformed input, and a duplicate address | Each bad shape (unknown role, empty or oversized grant list, an administrator grant scoped to a Space, an unknown Space, a duplicate grant pair) is refused before a row exists; a second invitation for a still-pending address is `INVITATION_PENDING`; an address an active principal already holds is `ALREADY_MEMBER`; a principal of another workspace is `FORBIDDEN` | CS05 |
| TI1b | A new address accepts | One membership, one managed `HUMAN_APPROVER` holding exactly the invitation's grants, and no personal workspace or Community allocation for the account the link created | CS05 |
| TI1c | A digest mismatch, five times | Each wrong guess commits its own failure count on both `check` and `accept`; the fifth locks even the real token out and nothing is created; a fresh rekey resets the count to zero and the superseded token stays dead | CS05 |
| TI1d | A revoked invitation | Acceptance is refused `INVITATION_REVOKED`; `check` reports state `REVOKED`; a second revoke is `INVALID_STATE`, an unknown invitation is `NOT_FOUND`, and a rekey of the revoked row is `INVALID_STATE`; no principal is created | CS05 |
| TI1e | The inviter loses `access.manage` | Acceptance, resend and revoke are all refused `FORBIDDEN`; nothing is created and the invitation stays `PENDING` | CS05 |
| TI1f | An existing account | A password is refused `ACCOUNT_EXISTS`; a different signed-in account is `ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`; both proofs or neither is `INVALID_REQUEST`; the real acceptance keeps the home membership and adds a second, the new principal's email left `NULL` with its label the address; a retry is `ALREADY`; a second invitation to the address is `ALREADY_MEMBER` | CS05 |
| TI1g | Resend at the SQL layer | The first send still in flight refuses `RESEND_LIMIT`; past the two-minute spacing it succeeds, returns the message to `PENDING` with a fresh `next_attempt_at` and zeroed `attempts`, and bumps `sends`; a sixth send is refused; the runtime can write only the messages table's delivery columns | CS05 |
| TI1h | The drain, and acceptance, as an owner that does not bypass row security | `workspace_invitation_due` lists a message across tenants only because `owner_drain` admits that specific role; `_check_drain_policy` reads that same policy and passes under this restricted posture too; the runtime role's own fence sees nothing outside its tenant; downgrade with a populated table is refused "lossy downgrade refused"; a new address, an existing account whose address a principal elsewhere holds, and a removed-and-reinvited member all accept correctly under this restricted posture | CS05 |
| TI1i | A removed member is invited back | While the old principal is active a second invitation is `ALREADY_MEMBER`; once it is deactivated a new invitation and acceptance re-point the account's one membership at the new principal (`active=true`, `version+1`), leaving the old principal deactivated with its own grant history intact | CS09 |
| TI1j | A grant's `space_id` is canonical or refused | Every shape but absent, `null`, or an 8-4-4-4-12 UUID string in any case is `INVALID_SPACE`; two cased spellings of one Space in one request are `INVALID_ROLES`; a shouted-case id is stored and later reported lowercase, and the accepted grant carries exactly that id | CS05 |
| TI2 | `create_invitation` writes a row and a chain event | One `PENDING` row and message; `invitation.created` names no address, only the domain; a non-administrator is `FORBIDDEN`; a second pending address is `INVITATION_PENDING`; an active member's address is `ALREADY_MEMBER`; a grant naming an archived Space is `INVALID_SPACE`; every refusal is logged by code alone | CS05 |
| TI2b | An expired pending invitation | `create_invitation` revokes the expired row (`invitation.revoked`, reason `EXPIRED`) and creates the new one in the same transaction; an unexpired pending row still refuses `INVITATION_PENDING` | CS04 |
| TI2c | Python-side grant validation matches SQL | `_validate_grants` normalizes every UUID spelling to the canonical lowercase form before a database round trip and refuses the same bad shapes and Space ids SQL's own `GRANT_CHECK` refuses | CS05 |
| TI2d | `list_invitations` requires `access.manage` | An administrator's own list shows the address, state, sends and delivery outcome; a member with no `access.manage` is `FORBIDDEN` | CS05 |
| TI3 | `deliver_invitations` sends one message; the email for a workspace and inviter named in a non-Latin script; names holding a web address, a dotted host-like word, a colon or a slash, an inviter named by an address, and names ending a word with a full stop or holding an underscore or hyphen | The link is `<origin>/join#ebt1.<tenant>.<invitation>.<secret>`; the stored digest matches and `generation` is 1; the subject and body name the workspace and inviter, or say "a workspace" and "An administrator" where nothing of a name survives or its label holds anything a mail client could link, never "unknown", so the invitation's own link is the only one; a name no mail client would link is kept; no address or token reaches a log line | CS04 |
| TI3b | `deliver_one`, the fast first send | Delivers the just-created message and marks it `DELIVERED`; a message that is not due, or does not exist, is a quiet `False`, never a raise | CS04 |
| TI4 | The drain reaches a Community-only tenant | A workspace made only through `community_provision`, which no jobs token or slots file names, is drained exactly like any other tenant *Evidence not published.* | CS04 |
| TI5 | Resend is bounded by spacing and by five | In flight, or under two minutes since delivery, is `RESEND_LIMIT`; past the spacing it sends again with a new digest and one `invitation.resent` event; the sixth send is refused | CS04 |
| TI6 | Revoke stops delivery | The invitation and its pending message both settle at once (`REVOKED` / `FAILED` `REVOKED`); the drain sends nothing for it; a second revoke is `INVALID_STATE` and an unknown invitation is `NOT_FOUND` | CS04 |
| TI7 | A transient failure stays pending; an invalid state settles failed | A relay refusal leaves the message `PENDING` with the attempt counted and the real cause recorded; an invitation that moved out from under an already-leased message (accepted or revoked mid-flight) settles `FAILED` `INVALID_STATE` on its first attempt, not spent through retries | CS04 |
| TI8 | A new address accepts with a password and signs in once | One verified account with no allocation; one managed `HUMAN_APPROVER` holding exactly the invitation's grants; one membership; the chain holds `principal.created`, `access.set_user_roles` (inviter as actor) and `invitation.accepted`, with no address, token or digest in any payload; `sign_in_invited_account` yields a session in that tenant; a retry is `ALREADY` and mints nothing | CS05 |
| TI8b | Acceptance writes every row without bypass | The same new-address path, `inspect_invitation`, and an `ACCOUNT_EXISTS` only SQL's own cross-tenant check can raise, all run and read back fenced as a NOBYPASSRLS owner | CS05 |
| TI9 | A Community workspace owner joins a second workspace | Signs in normally and accepts; two active memberships, each with its own local roles (Administrator at home, Viewer here); the home workspace and its allocation are unchanged; the new principal's email is `NULL` and its label is the address *Evidence not published.* | CS05 |
| TI10 | The wrong account, a pending session, and a held address | A password for an address an account or an active legacy principal holds is `ACCOUNT_EXISTS`; a different signed-in account is `ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`; a session missing its own account cookie is `ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`; both proofs or neither is `INVALID_REQUEST`; a pending-MFA session is not signed in yet and is `ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`; a holder who appears between inspection and acceptance is still caught by SQL's own recheck | CS05 |
| TI11 | Revoked, expired, forged and superseded links | Revoked is `INVITATION_REVOKED`; expired (seven days from creation regardless of resends) is `INVALID_TOKEN`; a forged guess counts on both `check` and `accept` and five lock the real link out too; a malformed code never reaches the database; a resend's new link works and the superseded one is `INVALID_TOKEN` | CS05 |
| TI12 | An inviter who lost authority admits nobody | Demoted to Viewer by another administrator, or the inviter's own principal deactivated: acceptance is `FORBIDDEN` either way, and the chain gains neither `principal.created` nor `invitation.accepted` | CS11 |
| TI12b | A Space archived after the invitation | `access_change` rechecks every Space at acceptance, so a grant naming a now-archived Space is `INVALID_SPACE` on both the new-address and the existing-account path, and nothing is made | CS11 |
| TI13a | Two acceptances of one link at once | One `ACCEPTED` and one `ALREADY`; exactly one account and one membership result | CS05 |
| TI13b | One account accepting twice at once | The account lock orders them; one `ACCEPTED` and one `ALREADY`; exactly one membership | CS05 |
| TI13c | Acceptance against a concurrent revoke | Exactly one of `{ACCEPTED, INVALID_STATE}` or `{INVITATION_REVOKED, REVOKED}` results, and the invitation's own state matches whichever won | CS05 |
| TI13d | Acceptance against a concurrent verified signup | The same new address racing an invitation and a Community signup produces exactly one account; the loser gets `ACCOUNT_EXISTS`, and whichever lost leaves its own request untouched (the signup stays `VERIFYING` or the invitation stays `PENDING`) *Evidence not published.* | CS05 |
| TI14a | The inviting administrator cannot reach the member's account | The workspace's own fenced reads see one membership row and zero rows of every account table; the runtime cannot write a password or factor table directly; the administrator's own password-reset and set-email doors both refuse `ACCOUNT_OWNER_REQUIRED` | CS09 |
| TI14b | Removal ends the invited workspace, leaves the member's own | Deactivating the member's principal ends its sessions and drops it from the switcher and from switching; their own workspace, and password sign-in to it, are untouched; an account whose only membership was removed cannot sign in at all *Evidence not published.* | CS09 |
| TI14c | Account-wide revocation ends both workspaces | "Sign out other devices" reaches the invited workspace's session too; suspending the account ends both workspaces' sessions and every new sign-in *Evidence not published.* | CS09 |
| TI14d | A removed member invited back rejoins under a new principal | The account's one membership here re-points at the new principal (`version` incremented); the old principal stays deactivated with its own grant history; the home workspace is untouched; the next switch and session land on the new principal with its new grants *Evidence not published.* | CS09 |
| TI15 | An administrator invitation | `access_change`'s promotion handling runs for the new principal; it holds `access.manage` and role `administrator`, and no credential exists to revoke, because this call just made the principal | CS11 |
| TI16 | A scanner, and a query string | Repeated `GET /join` changes nothing (state and failure count unchanged); a query-string token is refused (400) before the database | CS04 |
| TI17 | The new-address flow over HTTP | Signs the person in once (303, a minted session cookie); reopening the same link afterward shows "You have joined." with no session cookie, and the invitation stays `ACCEPTED` | CS04 |
| TI18 | The existing-account flow over HTTP | Joins (200, "You have joined `<workspace>`. Choose it from the workspace menu."), and never says the deployment cannot switch between workspaces, because switching is core; a pending-MFA session is refused at `/join/confirm` and redirected to `/mfa` without provisioning anything; a different signed-in account gets `mismatch` (403) | CS05 |
| TI19 | Each result kind carries its own status | `invalid` 403, `revoked` 403, `ready` 200, `sign_in_first` 200, `mismatch` 403, `joined` 200, `retry` 503, matching `RESULT_STATUS` exactly | CS04 |
| TI20 | The fragment, over real Chromium | Leaves the address bar in one `replaceState`; the token reaches no URL and no Referer, appears in exactly one request body (the exchange POST), and touches no storage | CS04 |
| TI21 | The invite pages at 390 pixels | Each page's `<h1>` is visible and nothing overflows the viewport | CS12 |
| TI22 | The console API as an administrator | Create, list, resend and revoke all succeed and agree with the row underneath; a second revoke of an already-revoked invitation is a typed `CONFLICT` (409), not a 500; a Viewer with no `access.manage` is refused `FORBIDDEN` on every route and changes nothing | CS05 |
| TI23 | Create schedules exactly one best-effort send | The console's create route runs `deliver_one` as a background task after commit, using the same production call shape `invitations-run` uses; the message is `DELIVERED` with one attempt by the time the response returns | CS04 |
| TI24 | Every new console code has a browser sentence | `INVITATION_PENDING`, `ALREADY_MEMBER` and `RESEND_LIMIT` are declared in `console_api/errors.py`, every code `teammate_invitations.REFUSALS` can answer with is one of them, and each has its own sentence in the console's `COPY` table | CS12 |
| TI25 | The console mutations require the same recent MFA | With the session's MFA assurance aged past thirty minutes, create, resend and revoke all refuse `MFA_REQUIRED` (401) and change nothing; a list read is unaffected; a fresh factor lets create succeed again | CS11 |
| TI26 | The list window is a pure thirty-day cutoff | A resolved row just past thirty days is dropped, a pending row well inside it is kept, and a pending-but-expired row past the cutoff is dropped too: `created_at` alone decides, never `state` | CS05 |
| TI27 | An address SQL accepts but `is_address` refuses | `create_invitation` refuses it `INVALID_EMAIL` before a row exists, for shapes SQL's own looser regex would have accepted; the normal address still normalizes (strip, lowercase) and is stored | CS04 |
| TI28 | A bad stored address never stalls another tenant | A row `create_invitation` would now refuse, inserted directly outside it, settles `FAILED` `INVALID_ADDRESS` on its own first attempt instead of aborting the whole drain; a valid tenant's message in the same pass still delivers | CS04 |
| TI29 | `deliver_one` logs and returns `False` on an unexpected failure | An unrelated fault is caught, logged once as `invitation.refused` naming the tenant and code with no exception text, and answered `False` rather than raised or swallowed silently | CS04 |
| TI30 | A settle fault counts failed and does not end the run | A guarded `_settle` (`SQLAlchemyError`, `UnsafeDatabaseRole`, `ValueError`, the same trio `_lease` and `_send` already catch) counts its message failed and leaves the row exactly as `_lease` set it, `IN_FLIGHT` with one attempt spent, while a second tenant's due message in the same pass still delivers; the CLI's own `invitations-run` exit-2 mapping answers a `SQLAlchemyError` with a fixed sentence rather than a traceback | CS04 |
| TI31 | The exchange answers `retry` for our own fault | `POST /join` maps `INVITATION_UNAVAILABLE` to `retry` (503), the same recoverable page `/join/accept` and `/join/confirm` already show, rather than telling the person a still-good link is `invalid` | CS04 |
| TI32 | A fault signing in after acceptance still says joined | `sign_in_invited_account` is guarded inside the same `except (SessionError, InvitationError, SQLAlchemyError)` `issue_session` already is; a fault there, after the acceptance already committed, answers `result("ready")` ("You have joined. Sign in.") rather than a bare 500 | CS04 |
| TI33 | Resend gets the same fast first send as create | `resend_invitation` returns the message id read in the same transaction, and the resend route schedules `deliver_one` as a background task exactly as create's route does, only when `runtime.mail` is set | CS04 |
| TI34 | `invitation.sent` logs the state actually written | A message that settles `FAILED` on a terminal race is logged `state=FAILED`, not derived from `result.delivered` alone (which would misreport it `PENDING`) | CS04 |
| TI35 | `list_invitations` filters and bounds in SQL | The thirty-day cutoff and a `LIMIT` are applied in the query `list_invitations` itself runs, not filtered afterward in the console route; a direct service call already excludes a backdated row | CS05 |
| TI36 | The drain refuses the run when `owner_drain` is missing | `_check_drain_policy` reads `pg_policy` for `owner_drain` on the messages table before the batch drain lists anything due, passes on the migrated database, and refuses the whole run with a fixed code, leaving the due row untouched, when the policy is gone | CS05 |
| TI37 | A re-invitation marks the principal it replaced | Migration `0061_replaced_principals`: a removed member's second acceptance sets `replaced_by` on the old principal to the new one; the new principal is active and replaces nobody, the old stays inactive, nobody else in the workspace is marked, and a first acceptance replaces nobody | CS09 |
| TI38 | A replaced principal is never reactivated | The old principal held Administrator; `admin_reactivate_principal` refuses it `PRINCIPAL_REPLACED`; the two principals, the membership and the grants are exactly as before; `access_has_permission` still answers false for it and the workspace's active managers are unchanged; deactivating the workspace's own administrator is still `LAST_ADMINISTRATOR` | CS11 |
| TI39 | Every reactivation surface refuses and shows the replacement | `people_pages.reactivate` (the function the console route wraps) raises `PRINCIPAL_REPLACED` and appends no `principal.reactivated`; `POST /api/console/v1/people/{id}/reactivate` answers 409 with that code and its sentence; the person and the list both carry `replaced_by`; `errors.ts` holds the same sentence and a bare 409 never reads as this code | CS12 |
| TI40 | The check refuses every write that would revive one | Even the owner inside the fence cannot make a replaced principal active, name a principal as its own replacement, or mark an active principal replaced (`principals_replaced_retired`, 23514); a replacement in another workspace fails the foreign key (`principals_replaced_by`, 23503) | CS11 |
| TI41 | A reactivation racing the re-invitation | The People function and the member's acceptance, started together on real threads and connections inside their open transactions: exactly `{ACCEPTED, PRINCIPAL_REPLACED}` or `{ALREADY_MEMBER, REACTIVATED}`, never a raw constraint violation reported as unavailability, and the rows and the chain match whichever won | CS11 |
| TI42 | The lock order, in both queue orders | The two definer functions called directly while a third connection holds the tenant fence; whichever queued first commits and the other refuses with its own code (`ALREADY_MEMBER` or `PRINCIPAL_REPLACED`); taking only the row lock before the fence instead deadlocks when the reactivation queued first | CS11 |
| TI43 | The migration walks both ways | Upgrade patches exactly its three fragments and keeps each function's owner, grants and definer; a membership re-pointed at 0059 needs no backfill; the downgrade refuses "lossy downgrade refused" while any principal names its replacement and leaves the head at 0061; with none, it restores both function bodies byte for byte and drops the column, both constraints and the covering index | CS05 |
| TI44 | The same walk without bypass | As a NOBYPASSRLS owner: the acceptance writes `replaced_by` through the tenant arm, reactivation refuses, the check binds this owner, and the downgrade sees a retained replacement although this owner sees no principal unfenced, with `FORCE` back on afterwards | CS05 |

Two further tests carry no TI number. `test_ti_invitation_token_round_trips_and_refuses_a_bad_code` is a pure unit test of the `ebt1.<tenant>.<invitation>.<secret>` format and its `INVALID_TOKEN` refusal, with no scenario of its own. `test_the_accept_post_passes_the_strict_origin_check_in_real_chromium` drives `POST /join/accept` end to end and asserts it is never refused with 403 under the site's `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, the same evidence T4.31's real-Chromium regressions record for the signup pages (CS04, CS12). The console side of TI39 is `console/src/components/directory/people.test.tsx`: a replaced entry reads "Rejoined as a new entry" in the list and on its page, which links to the new entry and offers no Reactivate, whatever roles the old entry held.

## Arcade logic extension

On real PostgreSQL, with a fixture Arcade caller and the protected mocks standing in for the vendor. The guide is [Arcade logic extension](ARCADE.md). Rows live in `tests/test_arcade.py` (AL1 to AL25 and AL29 to AL34), `tests/test_arcade_schema.py` (AL26), `tests/test_arcade_workflow.py` (AL27) and `tests/test_browser_surfaces.py` (AL28 and AL30's page clause), with shared fixtures in `tests/arcade_fixtures.py`; those four prefixes are group `AL` in `ACCEPTANCE_PREFIXES`. AL32's relation, the deadline the deployment file declares against the slowest grant measured, is `tests/test_arcade_deadline.py`: it takes the machine's timing lock and is marked `timed`, so a run that selects `not timed` leaves it out while AL32's grants still run. Supporting evidence lives in `tests/test_checkpoint.py`, `tests/test_replay_scenarios.py`, `tests/test_replay_verify.py`, `tests/test_recovery_run.py`, `tests/test_route_authority.py` and `tests/test_surface_configuration.py`.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AL1 | A governed tool's pre hook on a mapping with `external_executor`, in a tenant with no connector for the account and an unrevoked executor key | `{"code": "OK"}` and nothing else; one `DISPATCH_COMMITTED` execution, one `UNRESOLVED` impact row, one canonical claim, one correlation row holding the Arcade id and the secret; no dispatch ticket and no provider call |
| AL2 | The same pre hook delivered three times under one Arcade execution id, and once more after the frozen plan expires | One execution, one reservation, one claim, one correlation row and one secret; the earlier repeats answer `OK` from the re-grant and charge nothing; the post-expiry repeat is `CHECK_FAILED` with the reservation and claim still standing |
| AL3 | Two Arcade execution ids for one intent, concurrently and then sequentially while the first is granted and unsettled; then the same intent again after the first settles | The second is `CHECK_FAILED` and ExecBound holds one execution, one reservation and one claim; the post-settlement repeat is a fresh decision that resolves, evaluates and counts again, and is `CHECK_FAILED` on `RESOURCE_BUSY` until the run releases the claim |
| AL4 | A second Arcade execution id for an intent whose first pre hook was abandoned at the extension's deadline while admission committed, and again for one whose correlation row was written without a secret | Both are `CHECK_FAILED` because neither row holds the grant's secret; ExecBound holds one execution, one reservation and one claim; neither is answered `OK` from a re-grant, and the abandoned grant is visible in the uncertain queue |
| AL5 | A denial, a tenant emergency stop, a ninth outstanding grant, a claimed target and a tenant whose key ring cannot attest | Each `CHECK_FAILED` carrying only a stable code and the execution id where one exists; no input value, vendor string, rule id or secret in `error_message`; nothing reserved by any of them |
| AL6 | The runtime capacity bound full and the surface rate limiter tripped; separately, a shared hard ceiling reached | The first two answer `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` with no execution row; the ceiling answers `CHECK_FAILED`, and no rule identifier reaches the caller |
| AL7 | The application's own refusals reaching the boundary on each route: the capacity limiter exhausted, and a handler-level 503, on `POST /pre` and then on `POST /post` | On the pre path both are 200 with a body of exactly `{"code": ...}` — `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` for the 429 and `CHECK_FAILED` for the 503 — and neither carries `detail`; on the post path both are passed through with their original status and body; a 401 is passed through on both |
| AL8 | A tool outside the map; a governed tool at an undeclared version; a tool of an ungoverned toolkit; a tool whose reported `behavior` contradicts the map's `expect` | Denied, denied, `OK`, denied; nothing reserved in any case. The ungoverned pass is asserted against the tenant's chain: the audit head's sequence and hash are read before and after and are **unchanged**, and no `executions` row exists for it |
| AL9 | `inputs` missing the declared target key, carrying it as a non-string, and carrying an undeclared key; then, against a declaration naming an `incident` key, `inputs` missing it, carrying an explicit null, carrying a string no UUID reading admits, and carrying a number, and a hook that omits it while the reported `metadata` names one | Each refused before any kernel call; nothing resolved, reserved or claimed, and the refusal names no key value. The incident refusals answer `INVALID_INCIDENT` rather than the kernel's opaque code, the tenant's audit head is unmoved across all of them, and the reported id fills nothing |
| AL10 | `inputs` carrying a second target-shaped key beside the declared one, and an identity tool targeted by hostname | The first is `OK` on the declared key alone with the other ignored for selection and refused as undeclared where it is undeclared; the second reaches the kernel and the kernel refuses it, which is where that refusal belongs |
| AL11 | An approval-requiring tool: the first hook, a human decision, a retry under a new Arcade id inside the deadline; and a second walk where the deadline passes first | The first is `CHECK_FAILED` carrying the approvals page and the execution id; the retry adopts the same execution, is answered the execution's **first** grant with its own secret, consumes one approval exactly once and answers `OK`; the expired walk reaches `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` and its next attempt is a new decision and a new approval |
| AL12 | Post hooks carrying `success: true`, and `success: true` beside an `execution_error` | Both settle `EXECUTED` at tier `ASSERTED` with impact `CONFIRMED`, the claim still held until the run after the act window; the self-contradiction is retained on the correlation row, appends no audit event and changes no settlement; the settled rows' correlation secrets are null; both answer `OK` |
| AL13 | A post hook carrying `success: false` with and without `execution_code`, against a `DISPATCH_COMMITTED` row and against one already `INDETERMINATE` | Both settle `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` at tier `ASSERTED` with impact `CONFIRMED` and `confirmed_at` stamped, the claim held; `outstanding_grants` falls by one; the adoption query returns nothing for that intent; the transition payload carries the whole `AssertedOutcome` with reason `REPORTED_FAILURE` and `tier: ASSERTED` beside it; the platform's codes are retained as inert text; both answer `OK` |
| AL14 | A post hook with no `success` at all; one whose body exceeds `post_bytes`; one whose body will not parse | All three answer `OK`, settle nothing and leave the row where they found it with impact charged and the claim held; the oversized one is recorded as an unreadable report and no part of `output` is retained or logged; none of the three is a 4xx |
| AL15 | A post hook with `content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8`, one with `application/json`, one with `Application/JSON`, one with two `content-type` headers and one with `content-encoding` | The first three are accepted and settle identically; the last two are refused, exactly as the monitoring intake refuses them |
| AL16 | A post hook delivered twice; a second `UNKNOWN` against an `INDETERMINATE` row; a post hook arriving before its own pre hook; a post hook whose correlation row is a denial | The first two append and retain nothing and answer `OK`; the early arrival is the orphan case, recorded bounded and answered `OK`, and its later pre hook still decides normally; the denial case appends exactly one `execution.control_point_contradiction` to the tenant's chain, a second delivery of it appends none, and both answer `OK` |
| AL17 | A post hook carrying `inputs` that differ from the frozen ones, and one carrying none | The difference appends one `execution.control_point_contradiction` and is retained as inert text; the absent case appends nothing and claims nothing; neither changes the settlement; a tenant holding both kinds still exports and replays with no `UNKNOWN_EVENT_SEMANTICS` |
| AL18 | A correlation row's report secret read out of the table by a reader with no credential, then presented on the outcome route as `EXECUTED`; then a recovery run after the act window | The row settles and the run releases the canonical claim, so the theft **does** free the target — asserted as the guide's stated bound, not as a refusal; the impact stays `CONFIRMED` and no scope is refunded; the genuine post hook afterwards is answered with the settled view and appends nothing |
| AL19 | A governed pre hook and an ordinary agent dispatch against one ceiling with a total and an autonomous rule; and a group ceiling reached across two Arcade projects of one tenant | Both paths charge the same counters in every scope and the ceiling is reached once across them; an approved candidate adds zero autonomous units on both; the project past the hard ceiling is `CHECK_FAILED` |
| AL20 | A grant that never receives a post hook, past its report window, then a recovery run, then the console settlement with an ADMIN-signed record; and eight silent uncertain rows followed by a ninth pre hook, followed by one reported failure | Swept to `INDETERMINATE` with impact charged and the claim held and listed in the uncertain queue; only the signed settlement releases them; the ninth pre hook is `CHECK_FAILED` with `TOO_MANY_GRANTS`; the reported failure settles one row terminally and the next pre hook is then answered `OK` with a grant, which is what says the cap drains on settlement |
| AL21 | The extension's deadline expiring while admission is still in flight | The hook answers `CHECK_FAILED` within the configured budget; whatever admission commits is retained, visible in the uncertain queue and never refunded; no second request is sent |
| AL22 | A tenant in `OBSERVE` through both hooks | `OK` with an `OBSERVED` row and no reservation, claim or grant; the post hook is recorded and settles nothing and answers `OK` |
| AL23 | An ADMIN and an approver bearer; unknown, expired and revoked bearers; another tenant's bearer; a credential expiring before the report window | The ADMIN and approver bearers are refused as non-AGENT and audited; the unknown, expired and revoked bearers are refused as unauthenticated and counted; no hook decision is reached in any of those; the foreign bearer reaches only its own tenant's objects; the short credential shortens the report window to its own expiry and its post hook afterwards is refused on identity rather than on the deadline |
| AL24 | Configuration validation: an `operation_ref` outside `ActionRequest.operation_ref`'s own constraint, a toolkit named as both governed and ungoverned, a `target.input` outside its own `inputs` list, an `incident.input` outside it, a duplicate tool declaration, a deployment with an `arcade` block and no connectors, and a key reload whose file changes the `arcade` block | Each invalid file is refused at load as the same opaque invalid-configuration answer every invalid file gets; the connector-free deployment loads; the reload is `CONFIGURATION_CHANGED` and leaves the running keys and the running tool map untouched |
| AL25 | The `create_arcade` boundary, `GET /health`, and the route inventory with and without an `arcade` block | Every method and raw path but the three answers 404 and a websocket is closed; `GET /health` is served by the application's readiness route, answers 200 with the database up and 503 with it down, is unauthenticated and names no tenant; `tests/test_route_authority.py` asserts the exact served set in both constructions and the two new routes carry AGENT authority |
| AL26 | Migration round trip with correlation rows of each kind and an execution in each settled state retained, **including a corrected row whose own state is `FAILED_NOT_EXECUTED` and whose events name the new state**; `check_runtime`; the runtime role against `arcade_hooks`; a replayed history containing a settled `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` and a corrected one | Downgrade refuses while any of the three constrained columns holds the new state anywhere — asserted with the corrected row as the only holder, which a check of `executions.state` alone would let through — and succeeds once none does; `check_runtime` passes before and after; the runtime cannot `DELETE`; the export recomputes every decision, reconstructs the impact as `CONFIRMED` for the asserted failure and `NONEXECUTION` for the corrected one and reports both as settled rather than unresolved; `Ledger.correct` is asserted as the narrow method it is; the frozen contract fixtures and the context parity golden stay byte-identical |
| AL27 | The fixture Arcade caller over the real HTTP surface, driving every branch and asserting its recorded shapes against `execbound_harness.fake_arcade.HOOK_CONTRACT` | The walk covers governed permit, denial, approval wait and retry, ungoverned pass, duplicate pre and post delivery, reported success, reported failure, absent `success`, an oversized body and a missing post hook; the contract dictionary is a module-level constant of the harness rather than a literal inside the test, so the guide, the driver and this walk assert one object and a change to any shape fails in one place |
| AL28 | The record and approval pages for an Arcade-carried execution, with real Chromium | Tool identity, Arcade user id, reported codes and metadata render as inert text with no control that dispatches; the tier is shown with what it rests on; a settled assertion says what still holds its claim and what releases it; a settled `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` row says the effect was never established and that the impact is charged |
| AL29 | The settlement vocabulary, both halves. Every `(outcome, reason)` spelling on the outcome route, signed and unsigned; `TerminalEvidence`, `ExecutorRecord` and `JournalRecord` asked for `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` and `REPORTED_FAILURE`; a verifier double returning the new pair, through `record_outcome` against a dispatched row and through the report route against a grant | Exactly five pairings validate and exactly ten `(tier, outcome, reason)` combinations settle — four verified at `ATTESTED`, the same four at no tier on the dispatched path, and two asserted — enumerated in the test rather than sampled; every other spelling is `INVALID_REQUEST` from `parse_report` with no execution row read, no fence taken and no secret compared; the three evidence models raise for both new members; a report presenting a signed `record` beside `REPORTED_FAILURE` is refused by `OutcomeReport`'s own clause, asserted as that refusal; the verifier double is refused by `_proof` in both call paths; `test_the_terminal_aliases_are_the_evidence_literals` passes **unchanged** |
| AL30 | A row settled `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` at tier `ASSERTED`, then an ADMIN-signed `CANCELLED_NOT_EXECUTED` through `reconcile`; the same walk with an attested `VERSION_MISMATCH`; a third where the recovery run released the claim before the correction; and, on a corrected row, the correcting record replayed on the report route | Each settles at the verified evidence's own state — `FAILED_NOT_EXECUTED`, then `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` — at tier `ATTESTED`, with the impact rewritten to `NONEXECUTION` and `confirmed_at` null; the claim is released, and in the third walk `released_at` and the earlier `execution.claim_released` event are **unchanged**; the chain holds both transitions with both statements retained and the administrator's reason on the second; an ADMIN drives one correction of each direction from the record page's own control, in Chromium, and the page then shows the corrected row and the statement it corrected; a redelivery of the original post hook afterwards is `INVALID_STATE` in the kernel, answered `OK` to the platform and settles nothing, while the correcting record on the same route is the restatement it is. The export clause is **parametrized over both correction directions**: for each, the ledger recomputes with no `INVALID_IMPACT_HISTORY` and no `INCOMPLETE_HISTORY`, `ReplayReport.complete` is true, and the exported row's `settlement_tier` and terminal time are the **correction's** |
| AL31 | Everything the correction refuses: an ADMIN-signed `EXECUTED` against a correctable row; an ADMIN-signed non-execution against an `EXECUTED` row at tier `ASSERTED` and at tier `ATTESTED`; against an already-corrected row; against a row ExecBound dispatched; a second correction; a report carrying a record presented against an **uncorrected** correctable row; and a row whose retained state, tier or reason is not the correctable trio, each of the three varied on its own | Every one is `INVALID_STATE`, audited by `_rejection` and leaving the row, its impact, its claim and its tier untouched; the promotion the checkpoint guide's note refused is still refused with the same code; the report route produces no correction, so a held report secret corrects nothing. Four refusals are asserted **individually**, because a mutation of any one alone fails nothing: `_correctable`'s state fact and its tier fact, each varied while the other two hold, and each of the two direction guards removed by name |
| AL32 | The granting-path latency measurement: the eligible pre hook end to end against the fixture caller and the protected mocks, at one, four and sixteen concurrent callers of one tenant, with the reservation, the canonical claim, an approval consumption, the executor key ring load, the closing attestation check and the secret mint inside the timed region | Medians and a maximum reported beside `scripts/admission_benchmark.py`'s denied-path figures for the same concurrencies; the `arcade` block's required `deadline_seconds` is defended against the measured maximum rather than against a default, and a deployment file omitting it is refused at load |
| AL33 | A tenant whose only executor key is revoked as compromised, holding a live grant issued before the revocation: an attested report, then a bare `success: false`, then a recovery run past the act window, then an attempted correction with a newly registered key | The attested report is refused and settles nothing; the bare word settles `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` at tier `ASSERTED` with the impact confirmed; the run releases the canonical claim; the correction is refused because no key is usable at that grant instant, and a key registered after it is refused too — asserted as the guide's stated bound, not as a refusal the guide claims to make |
| AL34 | A governed tool declaring an `incident` key and mapped to `endpoint.isolate` under the released `endpoint-isolation.v1`, driven end to end: a hook claiming the target's own open critical incident; then hooks claiming an id naming nothing, an incident of another resource of the same account, and an incident of another tenant; then one claiming a second incident of this tenant standing against the same target | The first is `{"code": "OK"}` with one `DISPATCH_COMMITTED` row and one `UNRESOLVED` impact, and the frozen plan carries the claimed id with the contract's incident facts naming it as their source object and nothing set in the claim beside it; the three misdirected claims are each `CHECK_FAILED` with nothing resolved, reserved or claimed; the second incident of the same target is granted, which is the guide's stated bound — the kernel binds a claim to the target and not to relevance — asserted rather than implied |
| AL35 | A governed call the tool map refuses, delivered three ways: an undeclared tool with a usable Arcade execution id, an undeclared input with another, and an undeclared tool whose execution id falls outside the kernel's idempotency class; then a legitimate call reusing the first refusal's id | Each of the first two leaves a correlation row carrying the toolkit, the tool and the version refused, with `decision` `CHECK_FAILED`, no execution and no report secret; the unkeyable one is answered and recorded nowhere; the tenant's audit head is unmoved across all three; and the reused id grants, so a refusal row is not a tombstone |

`GC7`, `GC8`, `GC10`, `GC12` and `GC13` are re-run as part of this group's evidence, and what they establish is that the checkpoint's own behaviour still holds; `GC14` and `GC17` are **extended** above, because without a `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` or corrected row neither could detect a defect in that state. Provider-specific proof, the live half of the hook contract and every claim about how a real Arcade deployment behaves are **not** covered by these rows; the [18 September 2026 trial](trials/2026-09-18-arcade-logic-extension.md) records what a real Arcade deployment was seen to do.

## Assistant

The engine's rows are `tests/test_assistant_client.py`, `tests/test_assistant_turns.py`, `tests/test_assistant_tools.py` and `tests/test_assistant_loop.py`; CI never calls a model, a scripted transport replays every provider answer.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AS1 | A private configuration without an `assistant` block; with one; with the key in the environment, in the block, in both, in neither; a tenant with the switch off | Without the block `runtime.assistant` is None and `assistant_enabled` is false; with it the caps and model load and the key appears in no repr; boot merges the key from `EXECBOUND_ASSISTANT_API_KEY` when the block carries none, refuses both and neither naming the variable and never the value, and scrubs it; a turn for a tenant whose switch is off is refused `ASSISTANT_OFF`; an ADMIN sets the switch with a reason and it is audited, a no-op and an approver are refused |
| AS2 | The runtime role against `assistant_turns`; a person's turns read by another person and by an agent principal | INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE are refused; every write goes through a definer function that checks the actor owns the turn; another person reads nothing and cannot step the turn; an agent can own no turn |
| AS3 | An approver with one scoped pending request and one execution outside the scope; the administrator | The approver's list holds the scoped request only; its detail carries the frozen intent, the facts with sources, the approval with its deadline and the kernel's decision events; the other execution is `found: false`; the administrator lists both |
| AS4 | Every tool called with an unknown name, a missing or extra argument, a wrong type, a bad uuid or a value outside its bounds | Refused as `UNKNOWN_TOOL` or `INVALID_ARGUMENTS` before any read; every tool's wire schema is strict with no additional properties |
| AS5 | An agent label carrying instructions; a scripted answer carrying a Markdown image, a link and an id no tool returned | The label reaches the model inside the `untrusted` envelope only; the answer is stored as text and cites only the id a tool returned |
| AS6 | A credential-shaped string in a tenant label read by a tool | The turn fails `REFUSED_SECRET`, is audited, and the transport recorded nothing carrying the string |
| AS7 | An explanation of an approval whose scripted answer recommends a decision; one that explains | The first is `FAILED`/`RECOMMENDATION` with no answer stored; the second completes citing the approval; an approval the person cannot read is refused before any model call |
| AS8 | A completed turn read back, retired at 29 days 23 hours and at 30 days | Steps, answer, cited ids, tools and token counts are whole; retirement nulls the text at thirty days, keeps the counts, and is an ADMIN's call; `assistant.asked` carries the turn id, tools, counts and model and no question or answer |
| AS9 | Nine tool calls; output past the token ceiling; a provider slower than the turn's limit; a rate limit then success; a bad request; a turn left running for ninety seconds; the hourly and daily caps | `TOOL_LIMIT`, `TOKEN_LIMIT`, `TIME_LIMIT` each once with nothing retried past the bound; one retry for the rate limit and none for the bad request; `ABANDONED` persisted on the first read; `PERSON_LIMIT` and `TENANT_LIMIT` counted in the creating transaction |
| AS10 | A console with no `assistant` block; with one and the tenant switch off; with one and the switch on | Without the block every assistant address answers 404; with it and the switch off a turn is refused `ASSISTANT_OFF` and the list answers empty; with the switch on a turn is created and answered |
| AS11 | Every assistant route with a bearer credential, with no session, and with another person's turn id | 403 and audited for the credential; a typed 401 in JSON for no session, even to a browser; 404 for another person's turn on the read, the stream and the opened mark |
| AS12 | The four constructions of the application: plain, with an `arcade` block, with the console, with the console and an `assistant` block | Each serves exactly its inventory; the assistant block adds its seven routes and nothing else, and all seven are session routes |
| AS13 | A turn watched as it gains steps, one already finished, and one still running at the stream's deadline | One `step` event per step, each once, then one `done` event carrying the ending; a finished turn is one `done` and no wait; the deadline closes the stream saying what the row says |
| AS14 | Every packaged help text against the document it summarises | Each phrase that carries a claim appears in the text and in that document; each text is one bounded paragraph and carries no advice about a decision |
| AS15 | The `recover` sweep over a tenants file | Each tenant's report carries `assistant_turns_retired` beside its settlements, because the administrator and tenants-file runs retire through `retire_turns`, while on the schedule retirement rides on `recover_scheduled` (SJ2) |
| AS16 | A draft written as rules, and a scripted proposal the compiler accepts | The answer is the rule as the form takes it, its sentence, and its conditions in the words the editor parses, which parse back to the same conditions; no bundle is created or changed; the turn is the asker's own, completed, carrying the rule's sentence |
| AS17 | Proposals naming an unknown fact, a value outside its domain, an action the draft does not map, an id the draft already uses, an impossible effect or quorum, and answers that are not a rule at all | Each is refused `INVALID_PROPOSAL` with the field that caused it, the turn reads failed, and nothing is written |
| AS18 | The route as an approver; for a bundle written as a document; for a validated bundle; for a bundle of another tenant; with a bad body | 403 for the approver; the store's own refusal for a bundle with no rules to add to and for one that is not there, neither reaching the model; 400 for a body the model of the request refuses |

## Spaces (SP1 to SP22)

Separation of ownership and view inside one tenant. **A space is not isolation**:
forced row-level security is a second line of defence for tenants and there is no second
line for spaces, so SP1 is the whole of the mitigation for a forgotten predicate and takes
its parameter list from the migration's own column list.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SP1 | Two spaces with rows in every space-carrying table; a member of one reads every list, count, chart, search and detail | Parametrized over every table the migration touches: nothing of the other space is returned, on any surface, in either role |
| SP2 | "All spaces" | A tenant administrator sees both; a member of two spaces sees the union of theirs and nothing else; a member of one is offered no "All spaces" entry |
| SP3 | Membership restricts | A tenant administrator given a membership is narrowed at once; the ADMIN space role is refused for a `HUMAN_APPROVER` principal; the last tenant administrator cannot be narrowed away |
| SP4 | The current space is a preference | Removing a member while their session holds the space refuses the next read; `session_set_space` refuses another tenant's, an archived-but-unseen and an unknown space alike |
| SP5 | Sharing | An offered account and an offered bundle are usable by another space and editable, retirable and un-offerable only by the owner; an unoffered one is invisible and a mapping on it is refused at admission |
| SP6 | Per-space policy selection | Two spaces select two bundles; each agent is admitted under its own; a bundle no space selects retires; a bundle a space selects cannot be retired; `ACTIVE` equals "named by a selection row" after every transition |
| SP7 | Limits | A `tenant` rule counts across spaces; a `space` rule counts only its own retained rows; an approved candidate is exempt from autonomous and charges total in a space scope as in every other; a missing or doubled qualifier fails validation with its field location |
| SP8 | The tenant-wide claim | Space A's unresolved claim refuses space B's execution on the same canonical target with today's conflict and nothing about A on the wire or the page |
| SP9 | The archive race | An archive and a decision run concurrently on real PostgreSQL; exactly one commits; an archived space refuses new work with `SPACE_ARCHIVED` at the stop's check point; a `DISPATCH_COMMITTED` execution is not cancelled by an archive |
| SP10 | Migration round trip | The default space holds everything; every column is backfilled and every CHECK holds; approvers gain memberships and administrators do not; the selection moves off the control row; downgrade refuses with two spaces and otherwise restores the column, the grant and the index; `check_runtime` passes; the endpoint and identity contract goldens and every frozen fixture are byte-identical |
| SP11 | Console API | The harness's two sweeps on every route; a member is 403 on every administration route; a cross-tenant or unknown space is 404; `/session/space` answers the fresh session view; the secret check over every fixture response |
| SP12 | The console in Chromium | The switcher in both schemes at 1280 and 390 px, axe clean with zero CSP or Trusted Types violations; the popover reachable and dismissable by keyboard alone; a switch clearing the cache and re-reading every count; an approval link, typed or followed from the Activity drawer while another space is open, opening in the space that owns it; a space named with markup rendered as text |
| SP13 | Replay | A `space` candidate rule reconstructs from captures; captures with no recorded space report `UNKNOWN_SPACE` and stay `UNRESOLVED` while the baseline still matches; `report.json` stays deterministic |
| SP14 | Audit | Every administration act appends its event under the tenant's one chain and head; the chain is not split; a space switch appends nothing; an owner command and the console write the same event with the same payload, except that the console's also names the credential that acted (`actor_credential_id`, a browser session's own or a personal access token's backing credential) and an owner command, which acts over the owner connection with no credential, names none |
| SP15 | Keys of a space's agents | An administrator seated as `ADMIN` in one space issues, revokes and deletes the keys of an agent of another space, also when seated there as an approver, through the pages' functions and the console API; then the same for an agent of their own space; a workspace administrator in both; a person seated only in the other space; the credentials page and `GET /credentials` read by each; migration 0071 applied and rolled back | Every write on the other space's agent is refused with `INVALID_PRINCIPAL` or `INVALID_CREDENTIAL`, the code an unknown principal or credential gets, and writes nothing; their own space's and the workspace administrator's writes succeed; the person's credential is issued and revoked as before 0071; the space administrator's page lists every person and no agent or key of the other space, the workspace administrator's lists both spaces and follows the space they are looking at; the upgrade adds only the check and keeps owner, grants, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`, and the downgrade restores each function byte for byte | `tests/test_spaces_credentials.py` |
| SP16 | Another space's agents and scopes | An administrator seated as `ADMIN` in one space labels, deactivates, reactivates, binds and unbinds an agent of another space, also when seated there as an approver, through the definer functions and the console API; binds their own agent to a discovered agent of another space's connection; withdraws an approver scope of another space and assigns the same approver, target and action in their own; then each for their own space's agent and scope; a workspace administrator in both; migration 0072 applied and rolled back | Every write on the other space is refused with the code a target that does not exist gets (`INVALID_PRINCIPAL`, `INVALID_AGENT`, `INVALID_STATE`, `INVALID_SCOPE`; taking over a live scope `INVALID_SPACE`) and writes nothing, and the console answers 404 before the write runs; their own space's and the workspace administrator's writes succeed; the upgrade adds only the checks to eighteen functions and keeps owner, grants, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`, and the downgrade restores each function byte for byte | `tests/test_spaces_principals.py` |
| SP17 | People outside their spaces | The same administrator issues and revokes a credential, labels, deactivates and reactivates, sets the sign-in address, mints a set-password code, invites, links and unlinks a directory account, revokes a directory invitation and ends the sessions of a person seated only in the other space, of one seated in both, of one seated nowhere, of one managed by the access model and of a member of an access group; revokes and deletes a workspace administrator's personal access token; the console's People routes for the first; then each for a person seated in their space alone, their own token and another of their space's administrators'; a workspace administrator on each refused person | Every write on a refused person is refused with the code an unknown target gets and writes nothing, and ending sessions ends none; the console answers 404; seated as `ADMIN` in both spaces they administer the person seated in both, and as an approver in the other space only those seated in theirs; the writes on their own space's person and tokens succeed, and the workspace administrator's on every person | `tests/test_spaces_principals.py`, `tests/test_spaces_credentials.py` |
| SP18 | What a space administrator is shown | The People list, a person's page, the credentials page and the workspace's token list, read by the space administrator and by a workspace administrator looking at all spaces and at one | The space administrator is shown only the people they may administer and the tokens of those people and their own, and a person outside them is 404; the workspace administrator is shown every person whichever space they look at | `tests/test_spaces_principals.py` |
| SP19 | Adding a person | The space administrator creates an approver with `New approver` and with the credentials page's `new:HUMAN_APPROVER`, then labels them, sets their address, mints a code, issues and revokes a credential, and deactivates and reactivates them; then, seated as `ADMIN` in two spaces and looking at both, creates another | Each new person is seated as an approver in the space the administrator writes in and every later write on them succeeds; looking at two spaces the creation is refused `INVALID_SPACE` rather than guessed | `tests/test_spaces_principals.py` |
| SP20 | The tenant's own identity provider | An administrator seated as `ADMIN` in one space registers a provider, enables a verified one and disables an enabled one under which a person holds a directory session, through the store, the definer functions and the console API, and names a provider that does not exist; signs in through a draft and through a verified provider by the store and by the self-link route, and through a draft by `directory_link_self` itself, as do, through a draft by `directory_link_self`, an approver seated in a space and one seated nowhere; links their own account through the enabled provider; then a workspace administrator each; migration 0073 applied, rolled back and applied again | Registering and every state change are refused with `INVALID_ACTOR`, the code an actor who is not an administrator gets, whatever the provider, and nothing is written: no provider row, no state change, no audit event, and the person's session stands; the console answers 403 before a discovery document is fetched or the provider named is read, and still lets them read the provider; a sign-in through a provider that is not enabled is refused `INVALID_PROVIDER` for all three and links nobody, and the self-link route answers 404 and sets no flow cookie; through the enabled provider they link their own account; the workspace administrator registers, verifies, enables and disables as before; the upgrade adds only the checks to three functions and keeps owner, grants, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`, and the downgrade restores each function byte for byte | `tests/test_spaces_directory.py` |
| SP21 | The workspace-wide settings | An administrator seated as `ADMIN` in one space changes the enforcement mode, the approval deadline and the emergency stop through the store functions, resumes a stop a workspace administrator activated, turns the assistant on and off, and sets the organization's MFA requirement through `mfa.manage_security` and through `account_security_change` and `user_security_change` themselves with a factor verified on that session; the same through the console API, the command line and Account security; then reads each setting; then a workspace administrator each; migration 0074 applied, rolled back and applied again | Every change is refused and writes nothing: the control row, the stop and the requirement are unchanged and no change event is appended; the kernel functions and the switch refuse `INVALID_IDENTITY`, the code an approver gets, and the kernel functions audit the refusal as `execution.rejected`; the two definer functions refuse `RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED`; the console answers 403 `FORBIDDEN`, the command line exits 2 with the code, and Account security offers no requirement form and answers its address 403; the administrator still reads the mode, the deadline, the stop in force and the assistant switch on the console and with `tenant-status`; the workspace administrator changes each as before; the upgrade adds only the check to the two functions and keeps owner, grants, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`, and the downgrade restores each function byte for byte | `tests/test_spaces_workspace_settings.py` |
| SP22 | The workspace's requests and reports | An administrator seated as `ADMIN` in one space reads the data and closure requests queue, which holds a request each from an approver seated only in another space and one from a workspace administrator, and records a response to each of the three kinds, a workspace closure included, through the store, through `account_change` itself and through the console API, reading the queue by a session and by a personal access token; runs `observe-report` and `rotation-readiness` through the functions and the command line; files and reads their own requests; then a workspace administrator each; migration 0075 applied, rolled back and applied again | The queue read is refused `FORBIDDEN` before any request is read, and the console answers 403, to the session and to the token alike, with no request id, name or requester id in the body; every response is refused `FORBIDDEN` by the store, by `account_change` and by the console with 403, and writes nothing: each request stays pending with no closer and no `account.resolve` event is appended; both reports are refused `INVALID_IDENTITY`, the code an approver gets, and the command line exits 2 with the code and prints nothing; the administrator's own requests are filed and read as before, and a workspace administrator's answer reaches them; the workspace administrator reads the whole queue, the other space's requester by name included, answers a workspace closure, and reads both reports as before; Workspace settings shows a narrowed administrator a note in place of the queue and does not read it; the upgrade adds only the check to `account_change` and keeps owner, grants, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`, and the downgrade restores the function byte for byte | `tests/test_spaces_workspace_requests.py`, `console/src/components/settings/WorkspaceService.test.tsx` |

SP12 is `tests/test_browser_console_spaces.py`, in real Chromium, beside
`console/src/shell/switcher.test.tsx` and `console/src/components/spaces/spaces.test.tsx`
for the components' own behaviour.

## Guided console setup

| ID | Scenario | Required result |
|---|---|---|
| UX1 | Run all three safe examples | Cedar evaluates synthetic facts; allow, deny and approval appear; no execution is created; ADMIN session, Origin and CSRF checks run before evaluation |
| UX2 | Connect and verify a named agent | Credential appears once, public host configuration contains no secret, verification reads actual scoped request history, and Activity opens with the selected agent filter |
| UX3 | Empty Space and paginated discovery | Setup uses registered agents in the current view, includes bound agents, and exposes continuation instead of inferring tenant-wide completion |
| UX4 | Set up a reviewer | Named account and normal sign-in setup precede explicit review permissions; legacy credentials remain inspectable but are not normal issuance choices; disabled directory providers do not imply usable sign-in |
| UX5 | Read stale or uncertain execution | Policy cause, execution evidence and next action remain separate; recorded specific causes survive generic codes; uncertain effects never advise blind retry |
| UX6 | Start and edit policy | Named starts preserve denials, mappings, context and limits; unsupported conversion is refused; creation requires selected-Space ADMIN access and available accounts/source bundles; drafts retain that Space; validation, preview and activation remain separate; activation leaves other Spaces' selections unchanged |
| UX7 | Connect a monitoring source | Tines and Entra connect from the console keep a provider key out of every place but the sealing request; the operator path keeps it outside the browser. Registration requires ADMIN access to the target Space; verification distinguishes awaiting sync, reporting, stale/error and revoked states from persisted scoped evidence |
| UX8 | Navigate and cancel setup | Both themes and phone layouts pass accessibility/CSP checks; phone navigation reaches setup pages; a form with no available choices can be closed; unavailable clipboard access gives manual-copy guidance |
| UX9 | Read setup progress for a Space | An administrator of that Space reads five milestones, each from a record with its identifier; another Space's records tick nothing; an approver, an agent credential and a reviewer-role administrator are refused; the read writes nothing |
| UX10 | Return to Getting started | It opens on the first unfinished step; the step survives a reload and the browser's Back; done steps show a tick and a time; without the read it opens as before |
| UX11 | The sidebar card at 1280 by 800 | It says how many required steps are done and which is next, collapses to a row when all are done or when hidden in this browser, and Sign out stays in view without scrolling |
| UX12 | An empty Space with no connection | Home, Connect an agent and Policies say requests are refused until a connection and a policy exist, offer samples only in the default Space to a workspace-wide administrator, say elsewhere that samples go to the default Space, and otherwise give the operator's commands (self-hosted) or the service operator's address (hosted) |
| UX13 | Wait for an agent's first request | The panel asks only while the tab is visible and stops ten minutes of wall-clock time after it started, offers Resume, names no cause, and on arrival shows the state, the reference and a link to the record |
| UX14 | Connect a Tines story | The two downloads are the committed files byte for byte; the steps name the credentials and resources the stories reference; no key, origin or tenant id is in a file |
| UX15 | Pilot in Observe only | The card says a policy is still required, that the mode is workspace-wide and that samples need Enforcing; the change goes through the reasoned dialog; observed decisions are counted apart and linked |
| UX16 | Empty pages | Agents with no agents and Activity with no requests say so and name the next action; filtered views keep their filtered wording, and so does a Space whose own agents are all asleep, which the default State hides, while a Space with no agents still says so when another Space's agents sleep |
| UX17 | Caveats | Each caveat is one disclosure away; the stale and lapsed notices still show whenever their condition holds |
| UX18 | Start by watching | `/monitor?setup=` opens setup only for the two providers; the watch-first line shows on Home and under Connect an agent in this edition, and the Discover card links Monitoring setup |
| UX19 | Which actions nobody can review | With `access.manage`, the review card lists each mapped action the decision coverage read reports with no eligible reviewer, by action and never by target; each fix opens the existing prefilled assignment dialog and sends nothing until confirmed; a custom policy's unknown coverage reads "Coverage unknown for this policy" with no fix; without `access.manage`, no call is made |

Evidence lives in the focused console component tests, `tests/test_console_learning.py`,
`tests/test_console_api_agents.py`,
`tests/test_console_policy_usability.py`, `tests/test_console_policy_spaces.py`,

`tests/test_browser_console_directory.py`, `tests/test_browser_console_policy.py`,

UX9 to UX18: `tests/test_setup_progress.py` and `tests/test_console_api_setup.py` for the
setup read on real PostgreSQL, with their Space boundaries mutation-checked;

for the Tines stories and their license; `tests/test_browser_console_setup.py` and

`TinesStory.test.tsx`, `PilotCard.test.tsx`, `SampleChecks.test.tsx`), `console/src/shell/`
