## Console: residual acceptance

Console requirements an audit on 23 September 2026 found only partly tested, each now held by a test. CR4 found one real failure, since fixed: the review-workspace page's "Stay in the current workspace" link sat under its button below the 24 px minimum target (WCAG 2.2, 2.5.8).

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CR1 | Every console API route in the route inventory, with an agent's bearer credential and with no session | Refused 401 or 403 with `FORBIDDEN` or `SESSION_REQUIRED` and audited once as that agent; without a session, 401. One application walks all of them, so a route added later is held to the same answers |
| CR2 | Every console API GET, as an administrator and as an approver, with a held request in the tenant | No answer carries a secret or sets a cookie, and none is a server error; the board's stream is left to its own tests |
| CR3 | Another tenant's approver, holding the real continuation of a held request | The queue and the decided list are empty, the detail is 404, the decision is refused as `INVALID_CONTINUATION`, the approval is still held and nothing reached the provider |
| CR4 | Every navigation entry at 390 px in both schemes; Ask, a turn and a monitored agent's page in dark and at 390 px; the workspace review route at 1280 px and in dark at 390 px; the phone's navigation sheet, the bell's panel and the keyboard list in both schemes at 1280 and 390 px | axe-core finds no WCAG 2.2 AA violation, the browser reports no policy or Trusted Types violation, and nothing scrolls sideways |
| CR5 | An agent labelled with markup, on the Agents page | The label is the characters it is, no element it describes exists, and nothing it names ran |
| CR6 | The command palette's arrow keys | The selection moves down and up over the entries a keyboard can land on, skipping disabled lines, and Enter opens the one it rests on |
| CR7 | The copy the reconcile dialog and the workspace switcher show for a refusal | The client's own sentence per code, a fixed line for an unknown code, never the server's detail, and a failure to reach the server for anything without a string code |
| CR8 | A page component that throws, on an approval route and elsewhere | The page's error boundary inside an intact shell; the basic approval page link only on an approval route; Retry renders the page once it no longer throws |
| CR9 | A response through the generated client carrying a newer release than the build's | The banner appears in the shell, the page still has its data and the navigation still works; the same release shows nothing |
| CR10 | The countdown ring and the sparkline | The ring's arc is the fraction left, turns to the deny colour at a quarter and clamps at both ends over a token-coloured track; each sparkline point sits by its value between the line's own low and high |

Tests live in tests/test_console_api_walk.py (CR1, CR2), test_console_api_approvals.py (CR3), test_browser_console.py, test_browser_console_monitor.py, test_browser_console_shell.py and the hosted service's own tests (CR4, CR5), and in the console's own `CommandPalette.test.tsx` (CR6), `lib/errors.test.ts` (CR7), `RouteError.test.tsx` (CR8), `shell/release-in-shell.test.tsx` (CR9) and `charts/charts.test.tsx` (CR10).

## Sample targets (SM1 to SM11)

One click in guided setup gives an empty workspace a real first governed action: a sample connection to the endpoint mock, three sample targets and the starter policy, so the person's own agent sees an allowed request execute, a denied one stop and one wait for their approval and then execute. The administrator's demo configuration for the samples lets `execbound-demo` run its boundary and approval beats on them from the administrator's own machine. Real PostgreSQL throughout; SM1 runs under an owner with the hosted owner's attributes, SM5 and SM11 against the endpoint mock as a process of its own and SM7 in real Chromium.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SM1 | Install as a workspace's administrator, under an owner that is NOSUPERUSER and NOBYPASSRLS with forced row security; install again | Exactly one sample connector account with `sample` set, its sources, the `endpoint.isolate` mapping, three canonical targets with resources, identities, two aliases each, an open critical incident each and the installer's review scope, and the starter bundle created, validated and activated, with `samples.installed` and the bundle's lifecycle events naming the administrator; the second install changes no row, appends nothing and logs nothing; replay reads the history as complete |
| SM2 | Install for a workspace with a selected policy, with an active connector account, with someone else's `endpoint.isolate` mapping; as an approver; with a malformed target set; on a deployment without a sample key; for a workspace the gateway's configuration holds a connector for; by an administrator switched off while the call waits on the tenant fence | Each refused with its code (`SAMPLES_NOT_OFFERED` in the console, `SAMPLES_UNAVAILABLE` without a key or beside a configured connector, `FORBIDDEN` for anyone but an administrator) and no row written; a sample account in a tenant holding a configured connector gets no derived connection, so its request is refused at admission and nothing is sent; an approver still sees the samples, their requests and their badges |
| SM3 | Derive the dispatch token, the control token and the journal key | Fixed known answers for a fixed key, tenant and account; a value derived for one tenant or account differs for any other; the three labels never give the same value; the tokens take the form a generated token takes; a sample key is 32 to 128 bytes of lower-case hex and anything else is refused |
| SM4 | The endpoint mock holding a sample key | A derived sample account executes and its journal verifies with the derived key under `sample-v1`; a mismatched tenant, account, key or role answers 401; a sample account's target is created on first sight at `sample-resource-v1` and a configured account's never is; configured accounts behave exactly as before; an unusable key stops the mock at start |
| SM5 | A workspace made through the owner commands, its administrator signed in with a password, two agents over HTTP, the mock as a real process | The development workstation executes and the provider's signed journal verifies with the derived key, with no settlement tier; the domain controller is denied with nothing sent; the production workstation waits, is approved by the person and executes; the shared limits count the sample requests across agents; replay reproduces every decision and touches neither the mock nor its journal; a lost response settles from the mock's journal by reconciliation |
| SM6 | Remove with an executed and a denied request on record; with a request attempted, pending approval, reserved, dispatch committed or indeterminate; after the person activated a policy of their own; with no samples | Every sample row deactivated and none deleted, the history kept, the unchanged starter bundle deselected and retired, `samples.removed` logged once, and install offered again; the status with nothing written and nothing logged without samples; `SAMPLES_BUSY` with nothing changed while a request is unresolved; a policy the person activated stays and install is not offered again |
| SM7 | In real Chromium at 1280 and 390 px: add samples from Home's guided setup, refresh them, have an agent make a request, open Verify protection and Activity, remove | The card lists what it adds, then the three targets with a Sample badge and the starter policy's reading; after the refresh the card shows the three targets again, read afresh, and a notice that each has a new incident; the request executes; Verify protection shows it as the first request and suggests the other two targets, the production workstation as the request to relay with its operation, hostname and incident and with the workspace's approval deadline, and the domain controller with what the agent is told of a denial; Activity shows it with a Sample badge; removal asks first and offers the samples again; axe finds no violation, the browser reports no policy violation, nothing scrolls sideways and no secret reaches the page |
| SM8 | Migration `0060_sample_targets` up, down and up again; the runtime catalog | `connector_accounts.sample` and the two definer functions, EXECUTE only for the runtime role and no new table write; the downgrade refuses while any sample account exists; `check_runtime` passes |
| SM9 | Refresh as a second administrator with a denied request and another reviewer's scope on record; with a request attempted, pending approval, reserved, dispatch committed or indeterminate; with an approval that lapsed and was not yet ended, and after it is ended; while a person's copy of the starter exists, as a draft, validated and then active, and when the person creates one while the refresh waits on the tenant fence; while a second space selects the starter; after the person added a connection of their own; with no samples; without a sample key; as an approver, an administrator seated in a space and a personal access token; with a held request on the tenant fence, in either order; on stale facts | One transaction removes and installs: the same three hostnames with new canonical ids and incidents and facts recorded now, the old starter retired and a new one active, review returned to the refreshing administrator alone, the first request counted afresh and every request kept, the removal's three audit events and the install's five naming the refreshing administrator and their credential, `samples.refreshed` logged and replay complete; `SAMPLES_BUSY` with nothing changed while a request is unresolved, a lapsed approval included until it is ended, and a refresh once it is; `POLICY_PRESENT` (`SAMPLES_NOT_OFFERED` in the console) before anything is removed while a person's copy names the sample account, which stays usable, or while a second space selects the starter, which both spaces still select; `ACCOUNT_PRESENT` with the removal rolled back; the status with nothing written and nothing logged without samples; `SAMPLES_UNAVAILABLE` without a key; `FORBIDDEN` and `TOKEN_NOT_ALLOWED` with nothing changed, the seated administrator refused before the refresh reads for a person's policy; the route refuses a missing CSRF token, another origin and an unknown field; request first, it is held and the refresh refused `SAMPLES_BUSY`, refresh first, the request is refused `EXECUTION_UNAVAILABLE`, and neither leaves an unresolved request on a switched-off account; a request on stale facts is refused `EXECUTION_UNAVAILABLE` with no execution until a refresh, after which the new incident is admitted and the replaced one refused |
| SM10 | Guided setup and the connection card with samples installed: the request to relay, with and without the operation; a mapping the starter names that is not live; stale facts, including a page left open as they go stale; the approval deadline on and off, and an agent key that expires before the facts; a pending approval on the held target; a lapsed one before and after the recovery run ends it; a lapse followed by a second agent's newer pending request, and by a newer request that executed; the domain controller's denial; the need for a new `reference`; the Sample targets card before and at `stale_at`, with the browser's clock wrong either way, refreshing, a refresh that finds the samples removed, and a refused refresh | `GET /api/console/v1/connection` names the `operation_ref` read from the live mapping row the starter bundle names for the samples, never assumed from the catalog action (for the samples both are `endpoint.isolate`), and `null` without samples or while that mapping is not live, and no module of guided setup spells the operation; each target's request reads "call `execute` with `operation_ref` …, target `hostname` … and `claimed_context.incident_id` …", and names the target and its incident alone until the operation has arrived; stale facts are said to refuse every request until refreshed, within half a minute of `stale_at` on a page left open, with the way past a policy made from the starter (retire it in Policies, activating the starter again first where it is the active policy); the deadline is the tenant's `deadline.seconds`, `null` when off where the session reads 120, and a request never waits past `stale_at`: with the deadline off an approval ends at `stale_at`, or at the agent key's expiry where that comes first; the newest pending approval on the held target made since the facts were recorded is linked at its own address with its closing time, else a lapsed one that nothing has ended yet, with when it lapsed, that the agent that made it ends it by sending the same request again, after which a new request with a new `reference` waits for a new approval while the facts are current, and that otherwise the recovery run ends it; an ended one is never named, nor a lapse once a newer approval is pending; the domain controller's line carries the bare-denial sentence; a line under the targets says each new request needs a new `reference`, a recorded one bringing back the request recorded under it whatever incident it names, with `reference` as code; the Sample targets card shows a Stale badge from the moment the server's clock reaches `stale_at`, and within half a minute on a page left open, offers Refresh sample facts with what a refresh re-creates said beside it, the new `reference` the refreshed targets need among it, holds both buttons while a sample request waits and until the samples have been read again after a change, shows the samples as read again rather than the refresh's own answer, says there was nothing to refresh where the samples were removed first, and words `SAMPLES_BUSY` and `SAMPLES_NOT_OFFERED` from a refresh as a refresh that changed nothing, the second with the way past a policy made from the starter |
| SM11 | The demo configuration for a workspace's samples: download it as their administrator before adding them, with the tab's session generation and another session's, with a query key it does not take and after removing them; as an administrator signed in to an account, with no generation, another session's and the session's own; as an approver, with an agent's key, with no session and with the administrator's personal access token; on a deployment that holds no sample key, and on one that holds a configured connector for the workspace; with the starter's isolation mapping switched off or renamed, with the denied or the allowed target's incident closed, with a policy a person activated selected in the starter's place and with the workspace observing; under the workspace's emergency stop and after its release; after the person's agent has had the domain controller denied, the production workstation held, then approved and executed, and the development workstation executed, and after a refresh; once the sample facts have gone stale, and after a refresh; on a deployment whose endpoint mock is published, beside a loopback and a public console origin; with a document the secret check refuses; and the configuration built from a status for each of those, for one with a request unresolved, for one with any named target changed at the provider, for one read with a request on the sample workstation left in each execution state and for one read just before and at the instant its facts go stale, and beside a public and a loopback console origin, for a public and a loopback endpoint mock. Then, the console served over real HTTP, download it signed in, add one agent's key file and the mock this machine reaches, and run `execbound-demo` `all`, then `budget`, then read the status and the download, then a second `decide`, then `decide` again after a refresh and a fresh download; run `all` on a samples file naming a mock, or a gateway, this machine cannot reach; run `decide` on a samples and on a fixture file whose first request is refused for unavailable context, for a key the gateway does not accept, under an emergency stop, for a space with no policy and as busy; and load a samples file naming another hostname, a target by another kind, an `admin` key file, no `agent_a` file or an unknown `workspace`, one naming `agent_b` beside `agent_a`, a fixture file naming `agent_a` alone, and a samples or fixture file naming a loopback mock, beside a loopback gateway and beside one published elsewhere, whether the file or `EXECBOUND_URL` names it | `GET /api/console/v1/samples/demo-configuration` answers 404 `NOT_FOUND` without samples and after their removal, and with samples whenever the demo could not run on them: on either deployment, which also refuses their refresh `SAMPLES_UNAVAILABLE`, while the mapping is not live, while a named target has no open incident, while a person's policy is selected in the starter's place or the workspace observes, though the hosts still carry their open incidents and the starter's effects, and while the emergency stop is active, when admission refuses the workstation isolation `TENANT_STOPPED`, each with `GET /samples` answering `demo_available` false and `demo_needs_refresh` false, and once the stop is released the file is handed out again; after the agent's denial both stay as they were, while its production request waits both are false, and once that request and the workstation's have executed `demo_available` is false and `demo_needs_refresh` true until a refresh, after which the file is handed out again; from the instant the facts go stale, when admission refuses the workstation isolation `EXECUTION_UNAVAILABLE`, `demo_available` is false and `demo_needs_refresh` true until a refresh, after which the file is handed out again; a request on the sample workstation left `EXECUTED`, `DISPATCH_COMMITTED`, `INDETERMINATE` or `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` counts that target as changed and one in any other state counts none, so a settled one of those four holds the file back with a refresh to offer, an unresolved one holds it back with none, and any other leaves it offered; otherwise, with `demo_available` true, an attachment named `sandbox.json` under `Cache-Control: no-store` holding no credential and no secret: `"workspace": "samples"`, the console's origin, the tenant, the endpoint mock's origin unless it is a loopback address, whatever the console's origin, and never the console's, and the allowed, denied and held sample targets by hostname with their open incidents, with an empty pool; another session's generation answers 409 `WORKSPACE_CHANGED`, and so does an account session's download without its own, which with it gets the file, an unknown query key 400, an approver 403 `FORBIDDEN`, an agent's key 403 audited, no session 401, and the token the same document its owner gets; a document the secret check refuses answers 503 `UNAVAILABLE` with nothing of it in the answer, logged once as `dependency.unavailable` with the code `PRIVATE_DATA` and nothing of what it found. The demo loads that file as samples, never as synthetic fixtures, with agent A's key alone. `all` runs `boundary` and `decide` and says that `budget` was not run: the agent's key opens the agent routes and nothing else and the endpoint mock refuses it; the workstation executes once and its retry returns the same execution, the domain controller is denied, a claim of the domain controller's incident on the workstation is refused `EXECUTION_UNAVAILABLE`, the agent's own approval is refused, and the production isolation waits, is approved by the administrator who added the samples and executes; the mock records two calls, every intent the run puts on the wire, refused ones included, names a sample hostname and none carries `synthetic_demo`, and no key is printed or recorded. `budget` exits 2 with nothing written; the status then says `demo_needs_refresh` and the download answers 404, the second `decide` exits 2 saying the workstation isolation ended `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` because a sample target has been isolated since its facts were recorded, and to refresh the sample facts and download again, and after that it runs. A refused first `decide` request exits 2 on either kind of file, recorded with its status and code and naming them, and says to refresh the facts only for `EXECUTION_UNAVAILABLE`: a key the gateway does not accept says to issue a new one, the emergency stop that it refuses every new request until an administrator lifts it, a space with no policy that it has none, and on samples to use an agent in the default space, and any other code points at the agent API reference. A mock or a gateway this machine cannot reach fails `boundary` with exit 2 and no traceback, the beat recorded as failed with the unreachable request and naming what could not be reached, and `all` stops there. Each such file is refused before anything is sent, the samples file naming `agent_b` too loads and the fixture file naming `agent_a` alone is refused, a loopback mock loads beside a loopback gateway and is refused beside any other, and the recording driver, which needs an administrator's credential, refuses a samples file |

Tests live in `tests/test_samples.py` (SM1, SM2, SM6, SM9, and SM11's configuration built from a status and the status read with a sample request in each state), `tests/test_samples_schema.py` (SM1, SM2, SM6, SM8), `tests/test_sample_accounts.py` (SM3), `tests/test_sample_mock.py` (SM4), `tests/test_samples_end_to_end.py` (SM5), `tests/test_console_api_samples.py` (SM2, SM6, SM9, SM10 and SM11 through core's console API with no plugin loaded, reading the status from `GET /api/console/v1/samples`), `tests/test_console_api_connection.py` (SM10), `tests/test_demo_command.py` (SM11, the demo run against a served gateway and the samples mock) and `tests/test_browser_console_samples.py` (SM7, against a gateway that loads no plugin), with `tests/test_sample_runtime.py` for the gateway's configuration and connection resolution, and the console's `WelcomePage.test.tsx` (SM10, and SM11's link on the Sample targets card, shown only with `demo_available` while the facts are current by the card's clock and naming the tab's session generation, and its refresh advice in the link's place with `demo_needs_refresh` or once the facts go stale, the page left open included), `SampleChecks.test.tsx` and `RequestToRelay.test.tsx` (SM10), `RecordTable.test.tsx`, `policy.test.tsx` and `agents.test.tsx` for the components.

SM9 is held by `tests/test_samples.py::test_sm9_refresh_records_the_samples_again_in_one_transaction_with_both_audits`, `::test_sm9b_refresh_is_refused_while_a_sample_request_is_unresolved`, `::test_sm9c_a_refresh_is_refused_rather_than_break_a_persons_policy_or_connection`, `::test_sm9d_a_lapsed_approval_holds_the_refresh_until_it_is_ended`, `::test_sm9e_a_refresh_and_a_held_request_on_the_fence_never_strand_the_request`, `::test_sm9f_stale_sample_facts_are_refused_before_any_plan_until_a_refresh`, `::test_sm9g_refresh_refuses_whom_install_refuses_and_writes_nothing_without_samples`, `::test_sm9h_a_starter_copy_created_while_a_refresh_waits_on_the_fence_holds_it_off` and `::test_sm9i_a_refresh_is_refused_while_a_second_space_selects_the_starter`; by `tests/test_console_api_samples.py::test_sm9_api_an_administrator_refreshes_the_sample_facts`, `::test_sm9_api_refresh_without_samples_or_a_sample_key_adds_nothing`, `::test_sm9_api_refresh_is_refused_while_a_persons_copy_of_the_starter_names_the_samples`, `::test_sm9_api_an_administrator_seated_in_a_space_may_not_refresh_the_samples`, `::test_sm9_api_a_token_may_not_refresh_the_samples`, `::test_sm9_api_refresh_waits_for_a_held_request_and_for_its_lapsed_approval_to_end`, `::test_sm2_api_an_approver_sees_the_samples_but_cannot_add_or_remove_them` and `::test_sm_api_the_routes_take_the_guards_every_mutation_takes`. SM10 is held by `tests/test_console_api_samples.py::test_sm10_api_guided_setup_finds_the_held_request_under_the_workspaces_deadline`, `::test_sm10_api_with_the_deadline_off_the_held_request_ends_no_later_than_the_facts` and `::test_sm10_api_a_short_lived_agent_key_ends_the_held_request_before_the_facts_go_stale`; by `tests/test_console_api_connection.py::test_the_operation_ref_leaves_with_the_samples`, `::test_the_operation_ref_is_the_samples_mapping_row_never_the_catalog_action` and `::test_the_operation_ref_is_null_while_the_starters_own_mapping_is_not_live`, which the validation evidence collects under SM by their own prefix; and by the console's `SampleChecks.test.tsx`, `RequestToRelay.test.tsx`, `WelcomePage.test.tsx` and `agents.test.tsx`. SM11 is held by every `tests/test_samples.py::test_sm11_`, `tests/test_console_api_samples.py::test_sm11_api_` and `tests/test_demo_command.py::test_sm11_` test, and by `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_a_token_downloads_the_samples_demo_configuration_its_owner_downloads`, which the validation evidence collects under SM by its own prefix.

## People numbers (PN1 to PN16)

A person reads "User 3" where the console printed "unlabelled" and the first characters of a UUID, the way migration `0058_api_key_numbers` gave agents "Agent 3". Migration `0062_people_numbers` numbers administrators and approvers from one in each workspace, in creation order, on a counter separate from agents'; the console names a person by the label, else the email, else "User N", and the numbers are display handles only. The group also covers the People page's pending invitations card, which leaves out accepted, revoked and expired invitations, and the workspace switcher, which no longer cuts the workspace name through. Real PostgreSQL throughout: PN3 runs on six real connections at once, PN7 under an owner with the hosted owner's attributes, and PN8 and PN16 in real Chromium.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PN1 | An administrator, an agent, an approver, an agent and an approver inserted in that order into a fresh workspace | The people read 1, 2 and 3 and the agents 1 and 2: each class counts from one in creation order on its own counter, and other workspaces' people change nothing |
| PN2 | An approver made an administrator and back, then made an agent; an agent made an approver | The number stays across the two person kinds; the person who becomes an agent takes the next agent number and the agent who becomes a person the next person number, and neither keeps the other class's number |
| PN3 | Six writers on six connections in one workspace, four people and two agents, released together and each holding its transaction open | The people get 2 to 5 and the agents 1 and 2, all distinct: the advisory lock keyed on the tenant and the class makes each insert wait for the one before it, and none stops at a unique index |
| PN4 | A removed member rejoins through a teammate invitation | The old entry, now replaced, keeps its number, and the new entry takes the next person number |
| PN5 | As an administrator: the People list and a person, access, the approvers' assignments and options, a space's members and the credentials list; then a new agent | Each carries the person's number, and the People list, the approvers' assignments and the credentials list carry the email the console names a person by; the new agent takes the next agent number, whatever the people's numbers are |
| PN6 | A space's member list in a workspace where every person has an email, read by a tenant administrator, by the approver seated in the space, and by two teammates managed by access control with one grant at Workspace scope: a Viewer seated nowhere and an Approver seated in the space | The administrator reads the member's email and number; the approver and both teammates read every member's number and no member's email, their own included. The teammates open every space and People refuses them: workspace-wide read access is not administration, and the gate is the session identity's effective kind, the check People makes |
| PN7 | Migration `0062_people_numbers` under an owner that is NOSUPERUSER and NOBYPASSRLS with forced row security, over two workspaces holding a person from before creation times were kept, people created out of their id order and at the same moment, and agents numbered by 0058; new rows after it; then the downgrade | People numbered per workspace by `created_at`, a row without one first, then by principal id, and the agents' numbers unchanged; new rows continue both counts inside the tenant fence; the column is `NOT NULL` and the functions are `number_agent_key` and `number_principal`; the downgrade clears people's numbers and restores 0058's function with its grants, its index, check and trigger exactly, and 0058's own count carries on from the agents' numbers |
| PN8 | In real Chromium at 1280 and 390 px, in light and dark: a workspace named "contoso" and one named "Northwind security operations, second shift" | The switcher chip's accessible name is "Workspace" and the name; both lines sit inside the chip and the chip inside the 56 px top bar; the name is one whole line tall and not cut through, and the long name ends in an ellipsis on that line; axe finds no violation, the browser reports no policy violation and nothing scrolls sideways |
| PN9 | The People list with a person who has an email and no label, and one with neither | The first is named by the email with "User 3" beside it and the second by "User 5" once, each a link to the person; no id prefix and no "unlabelled"; the button reads "Edit roles for" the email and opens that person's roles |
| PN10 | A person's page for someone with no label | The heading is the email, and "User 3" stands where the id prefix was; no "unlabelled" |
| PN11 | The approvers list and its assign form, with approvers who have no label | Each is named by the email, else "User 3", as a link to the person; the form's option reads "dana@example.com · User 3"; no "unlabelled" |
| PN12 | A space's members with no label, one with an email and one without | "dana@example.com User 2" and "User 1"; no id prefix and no "unlabelled"; each remove button names the member the same way |
| PN13 | A space's assign form offering an administrator with neither a label nor an email | The option reads "User 1 · Space administrator" |
| PN14 | The invitations card given a sent, a failed, a sending, an expired, a revoked and an accepted invitation | The sent, failed and sending ones are listed, each with its delivery state and its Resend and Revoke, and the expired one as Expired with only Invite again (AR7); the accepted and revoked ones are not |
| PN15 | The invitations card given only an accepted invitation | It reads "No open invitations." and shows no address |
| PN16 | In real Chromium at 360, 390 and 430 px, in light and dark, for a deployment with Ask and one without: a tenant administrator of a workspace named "Northwind security operations, second shift" who belongs to a second one, so the chip is there | No control in the top bar, nor the breadcrumb, paints over another or into either 16 px gutter, measured from each one's own box and every visible part of its content; the compact Space switcher shows its mark and chevron, whole from 390 px and at 360 px painting nothing outside its own box, and is still named "Space <name> in <workspace>; change space", the mode shows its dot and is still named by its word, and the Ask button is left out while Menu still offers Ask; axe finds no violation, the browser reports no policy violation and nothing scrolls sideways |

Tests live in `tests/test_people_numbers.py` (PN1 to PN7), `tests/test_browser_workspaces.py` (PN8, PN16), and the console's `directory/people.test.tsx` (PN9, PN10), `directory/access.test.tsx` (PN11), `spaces/spaces.test.tsx` (PN12, PN13) and `directory/invitations.test.tsx` (PN14, PN15). `tests/test_api_key_numbers.py` keeps 0058's own proofs under the new revision: a person's credential still has no number, and a principal whose kind changes takes the next number of its class.

## Personal access tokens (PT1 to PT13)

An administrator's own bearer token for the console API, `ebp1.<tenant>.<token>.<secret>`, migration `0063_personal_access_tokens`. A token carries its owner's authority except for the named exceptions (approvals and settlement, loosening enforcement, people/access/sign-in, and tokens and the owner's own account), each answering 403 `TOKEN_NOT_ALLOWED` and audited. Real PostgreSQL throughout, in `tests/test_personal_tokens.py` (the schema, the store and the owner rules), `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py` (the routes and the exceptions through the application), `tests/test_route_authority.py` (the closed route classification) and `tests/test_console_api_base.py` (the published reference), named in full below since each is one file among several; the console cards are `console/src/components/settings/PersonalTokens.tsx` and `console/src/components/tenant/WorkspaceTokens.tsx`, held by their own component tests rather than a PT row.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PT1 | An administrator creates a token; a person who is not an administrator tries to create one | The token is returned once, under `Cache-Control: no-store`, and never again from the list. If the administrator has enrolled a second factor, an assurance older than thirty minutes is refused `MFA_REQUIRED` and creates nothing, and a fresh one lets it succeed again (`require_recent_factor` is a no-op with none enrolled, so an administrator with no factor is not stepped up at all). A non-administrator is refused `FORBIDDEN`, leaving no token and no credential | `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_an_administrator_creates_lists_revokes_and_deletes_own_tokens`, `::test_creating_a_token_needs_a_recently_verified_factor_only_when_one_is_enrolled`, `::test_a_person_who_is_not_an_administrator_cannot_create_a_token` |
| PT2 | A token calls an allowed read and an allowed write as its owner | Both succeed as the owner, carry no workspace-generation header, and the write's audit event names the owner as `actor_id` and the token's backing credential as `actor_credential_id`, as every write a token may make does; the parametrized case list is checked against `TOKEN_ALLOWED` itself, so a route added later without evidence fails closed | `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_a_token_reads_and_writes_as_its_owner`, `::test_every_write_a_token_makes_names_its_backing_credential`, `::test_every_token_allowed_post_route_has_attribution_evidence` |
| PT3 | Every route in `token_rules.TOKEN_REFUSED`, and every hosted route in the plugin's own refused set, is called by a token, with its path parameters filled in | Each answers 403 `TOKEN_NOT_ALLOWED` and is audited as a refusal; switching to enforcing is allowed but switching to observe-only is not, and issuing a credential is allowed only for an agent | `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_every_refused_route_answers_token_not_allowed_through_the_application`, `::test_a_token_may_switch_to_enforcing_and_not_to_observe_only`, `::test_a_token_issues_a_key_only_for_an_agent`; for the hosted plugin's own routes, the hosted service's own tests |
| PT4 | A token tries an approval decision and a reconcile directly, and every write `TOKEN_REFUSED` carries under `/people`, `/access` and `/tenant/sign-in` | The decision and reconcile routes are refused before a continuation is ever offered; every people, access and sign-in write is refused, closing approval by proxy through a person the token could otherwise create and sign in as | `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_a_token_is_never_offered_an_approval_continuation`, `::test_a_token_is_never_offered_a_reconcile`, `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_token_allowed_writes_never_touch_people_access_or_sign_in` |
| PT5 | The owner's administrator role is removed; the owner is deactivated, through `people_pages.deactivate` (which revokes the backing credential, the product's own removal path) or directly; the owner's `workspace_memberships` row is marked inactive, distinct from both | Each ends the token on its next request with `SessionError`, whether or not anyone revoked it | `tests/test_personal_tokens.py::test_an_owner_demoted_from_administrator_loses_the_token`, `::test_an_owner_who_stops_being_an_administrator_loses_the_token`, `::test_a_token_revoked_through_its_backing_credential_can_be_deleted` (the `people_pages.deactivate` case asserts the refusal directly), `::test_removal_from_the_workspace_ends_the_token_distinct_from_deactivation_or_demotion` |

| PT8 | A request carries a session cookie and a bearer token together | Refused rather than one being preferred | `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py::test_a_cookie_and_a_token_together_are_refused` |
| PT9 | Every served `/api/console/v1` route: core's `CONSOLE`, `AUTHORITY` and `ASSISTANT` collections, the sample plugin's `PLUGINS` and the hosted plugin's `HOSTED` | Each of core's routes appears in exactly one of `TOKEN_ALLOWED` or `TOKEN_REFUSED`, which name nothing else; each of a plugin's appears in exactly one of the two sets that plugin registers with `token_routes`, which refuses any method of a path whose shape core classifies; a token calls a route a plugin allows and is refused one it refuses or leaves out; an unclassified route is refused | `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_every_console_api_route_says_whether_a_token_may_call_it`, `tests/test_plugins.py::test_token_routes_are_the_plugins_own_console_routes_and_methods`, `tests/test_plugins.py::test_a_plugin_cannot_allow_any_method_of_a_core_path`, `tests/test_plugin_surfaces.py::test_a_token_calls_the_route_a_plugin_allows_and_no_other`; for the hosted plugin's `HOSTED`, the hosted service's own tests |

## Scheduled jobs for every workspace

The scheduled `recover` and `notify` passes reach every workspace, one made by verified Community signup included, with no workspace credential: `recover --all-tenants` walks the directory of tenants (`job_tenants`, migration `0064_scheduled_jobs`) and acts in each active workspace as that workspace's scheduler principal, and `notify --all-tenants` drains every workspace a configured destination names. The tests are in `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_schema.py` (the schema), `tests/test_scheduled_actor.py` (the actor, SJ8 at the application and the SJ9 pin), `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py` (recovery), `tests/test_scheduled_notify.py` (notifications) and `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py` (the walk and the CLI), named in full below. Every test that touches a database runs on real PostgreSQL, and the `restricted` and `stack` fixtures build a database whose owner has the hosted owner's attributes, neither superuser nor BYPASSRLS. Where a row's tests cover less than its scenario, the row says how.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SJ1 | Migrate a database that already holds a tenant, then create tenants by a direct insert into `tenants` (as `seeded` does; sandbox seeding, `seed-demo`, monitoring `init-demo`, `gate_demo` and `validation_demo` insert the same way), by `administration.create_tenant` (the owner CLI's `tenant-create` and `quickstart`), by `community_provision`, and under an owner without bypass; then widen `owner_list`, narrow it to `USING (false)`, or add a restrictive policy | Every tenant is in `job_tenants` exactly once: the migration's backfill lists the tenant made before it, and the `tenants_enroll` trigger each one made after, by whichever path. `job_tenants_page` lists them in id order, 1 to 500 a page, and answers nothing for 501. The runtime role's direct read of the table is refused for want of a privilege while `check_runtime` still passes. The walk, enlistment and retirement work under the owner without bypass. Each changed policy stops the pass with `UNSAFE_JOB_POLICY` before any workspace is visited, and once it is restored the pass visits every workspace again | `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_schema.py::test_sj1_every_tenant_is_in_the_directory_exactly_once`, `::test_sj1_the_page_is_ordered_and_bounded`, `::test_sj1_the_runtime_role_cannot_read_the_directory`, `::test_sj1_the_backfill_and_the_list_work_for_an_owner_without_bypass`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_a_changed_directory_policy_stops_the_pass`, `::test_the_walk_and_the_scheduler_work_for_an_owner_without_bypass`; for a tenant made by `community_provision`, the hosted service's own tests |
| SJ2 | A workspace made only by `community_provision`, with no jobs token, holding a lapsed untouched approval on its sample production workstation, a lost response on a sample account and a 31-day-old assistant turn. A checkpoint grant past its report window and a settled assertion's claim past the act window, on the Gate test world instead, because a workspace made by signup cannot hold a Gate grant. A workspace with nothing due. `execbound recover --all-tenants` over a private configuration | With the workspace's `ScheduledActor` and the sample runtime, `recover` expires the approval to `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`, its `approval.transition` naming the `SYSTEM` principal, after which the samples can be removed; it settles the lost response `EXECUTED` from the sample mock's journal with `skipped_no_connector` 0, its `execution.reconciliation_requested` naming the `SYSTEM` principal. `recover_scheduled` retires the turn and, writing no event, makes no scheduler principal. On the Gate world the grant is swept `INDETERMINATE` and the claim released, each event naming the `SYSTEM` principal. The workspace with nothing due gains no scheduler principal. The CLI gives each workspace's pass the runtime, which derives a sample account's connection, and the configuration's whole list of destinations. Under the owner without bypass these steps rest on the directory, enlistment, retirement and guard tests (SJ1, SJ8): recovery reads nothing across tenants and calls no new definer function beyond those. What this row asserts of the workspace made by `community_provision` (the approval's expiry to `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` and the samples' removal after it, the lost response's `EXECUTED` settlement from the sample mock's journal, and the turn's retirement) rests on the hosted service's own tests, which are not published | `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py::test_sj2_grants_sweep_and_claims_release_under_the_scheduler`, `::test_sj2_a_settled_assertion_s_claim_is_released_under_the_scheduler`, `::test_a_workspace_with_nothing_due_gains_no_scheduler_principal`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_the_scheduled_recovery_is_given_the_runtime_and_its_destinations`; for the workspace made by `community_provision`, the hosted service's own tests |
| SJ3 | A workspace with a configured email destination and a pending approval, drained with no administrator; a workspace made by signup whose sample production workstation asks for approval, with an email destination for it; a queued row whose approval then closes, and one whose destination the pass's configuration no longer names; a row under a live lease; a deactivated workspace; `execbound notify --all-tenants` | `notify_scheduled` delivers one message through the TLS relay fixture in both workspaces, the first row `DELIVERED` after one attempt. `destination_tenants` names only the workspaces an email, Slack, Teams or webhook destination whose classes include approvals names, so a workspace whose last destination is removed is no longer drained, and the CLI drains each named workspace as its `ScheduledActor` and reports its counts. The recovery pass settles the closed approval's row `APPROVAL_CLOSED` without sending, and a second pass settles nothing. A pending row is kept while the pass's configuration names its destination and, once it does not, until the row is older than `ORPHAN_AFTER` (one day), and is then settled `DESTINATION_UNKNOWN`. A row under a live lease is left to its drain until the lease runs out. Settling refuses a deactivated workspace with `INVALID_IDENTITY` and leaves the row. With no plugin loaded the scheduled drain's mail links to `/approvals/{approval_id}`; with the hosted plugin loaded, given the plugin's approval path as `notify --all-tenants` gives it, to `/workspaces/{tenant}/approvals/{approval_id}` and its `/classic` page. Under the owner without bypass: as SJ2; the drain calls no new definer function | `tests/test_scheduled_notify.py::test_sj3_destination_tenants_are_the_approval_kinds_only`, `::test_sj3_the_scheduled_drain_delivers_with_no_administrator`, `::test_sj3_recovery_settles_a_closed_approval_s_row_no_drain_will_reach`, `::test_sj3_recovery_settles_against_the_destinations_it_is_given`, `::test_sj3_a_pending_row_whose_destination_is_gone_is_settled_unknown`, `::test_sj3_a_row_under_a_live_lease_is_left_to_its_drain`, `::test_sj3_settling_refuses_a_deactivated_workspace`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_sj3_the_notify_command_drains_every_workspace_a_destination_names`; for the workspace made by signup and the hosted plugin's approval links, the hosted service's own tests |
| SJ4 | A mapped workspace with a configured connector and a lost response (RC1's setup), reconciled by a `ScheduledActor` before and after enlistment, recovered by one, and recovered by `execbound recover --all-tenants` with that connector in the private configuration and no credential | Before enlistment `reconcile_operation` and `reconcile` each refuse the actor with `INVALID_IDENTITY`, settle nothing and retain no refusal, because there is no principal to name. Enlisted, the actor settles the operation `EXECUTED` from the journal with one provider mutation in total, the request and the settlement naming the `SYSTEM` principal, and one scheduler principal exists. `recover` with a `ScheduledActor` settles it the same way (examined 1, executed 1, unresolved 0) and records `principal.created`. The CLI run reports it executed in that workspace, with no workspace failed | `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py::test_sj4_a_scheduled_actor_settles_a_lost_response_from_the_journal`, `::test_sj4_a_mapped_workspace_settles_under_the_scheduler`, `::test_reconcile_refuses_a_scheduled_actor_with_nothing_to_attribute`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_the_cli_recovers_every_workspace_with_no_credential` |
| SJ5 | Two real workspaces with due work, walked by `every_workspace`: the first's connection lookup refuses and its assistant retirement fails. A step that refuses in one workspace. The token-map walk, `for_each_tenant`, with an `AssistantError` in one tenant (defect 1). A sweep that fails after `recover` expired an approval. `UnsafeDatabaseRole` from a sweep | The second workspace is still recovered (`executed` 1), and the first is failed with `ASSISTANT_UNAVAILABLE`, keeping its partial counts (examined 1, unresolved 1); every transaction either workspace's pass opens is fenced to that workspace. A refusing step is its own workspace's outcome on both walks, and the next workspace still runs. A failed sweep raises `SweepFailed` carrying the expiry and the retirement that still ran, and the walk reports them beside the failure code. `UnsafeDatabaseRole` is never one sweep's failure: it stops the run. That the next workspace is still notified rests on the notify walk being the same `every_workspace`; no test drains two workspaces where one fails | `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_sj5_one_workspace_s_failure_is_its_own_outcome`, `::test_a_step_s_refusal_is_contained_to_its_own_workspace`, `::test_defect_1_the_token_map_run_contains_an_assistant_failure`, `::test_a_failed_sweep_reports_what_its_run_had_already_committed`, `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py::test_a_failed_sweep_keeps_what_the_pass_had_already_done`, `::test_an_unsafe_database_role_is_never_one_sweep_s_failure` |
| SJ6 | A deactivated workspace in the walk; a workspace whose activity cannot be read; a scheduled run whose workspace is deactivated inside its first operation, and one deactivated between its first and second; an administrator's run whose credential is revoked between two operations, two expiries or two claim releases (defect 2) | The deactivated workspace is counted `inactive`, neither visited nor logged; the unreadable one is visited and failed with `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; a stopped workspace reports what it did beside its code. Deactivated inside an operation, the scheduled run stops there with `RecoveryStopped` (`INVALID_IDENTITY`), that operation counted `unresolved` and no second lookup made; deactivated between operations, it stops at the next recheck and the first settlement stands. The revoked administrator's run stops at the next recheck with `RecoveryStopped`, which carries its counts; an operation in flight at the revocation costs that one operation, and the later rows are untouched | `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_an_inactive_workspace_is_counted_and_a_stopped_one_reports_what_it_did`, `::test_a_workspace_whose_activity_cannot_be_read_is_visited_and_failed`, `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py::test_sj6_a_workspace_deactivated_inside_an_operation_stops_the_run_there`, `::test_sj6_a_workspace_deactivated_between_two_operations_stops_at_the_next`, `::test_defect_2_an_admin_revoked_between_two_operations_stops_the_run`, `::test_defect_2_an_admin_revoked_between_two_expiries_stops_the_run`, `::test_defect_2_an_admin_revoked_between_two_claim_releases_stops_the_run` |
| SJ7 | `recover` with a `ScheduledActor` and `cancel_unattempted`; `reconcile_operation` with an enlisted `ScheduledActor` and `cancel_if_missing` on a never-attempted operation; `execbound recover --all-tenants --cancel-unattempted`, and the other flag combinations the modes exclude | `recover` refuses with `INVALID_IDENTITY` before any selection reads. `reconcile_operation` refuses with `INVALID_IDENTITY` before any journal lookup or cancellation: the operation stays `DISPATCH_COMMITTED`, no reconciliation request is recorded, and the refusal is retained as `execution.rejected` naming the `SYSTEM` principal and no credential. The CLI refuses the combination and, beside `--all-tenants`, every flag another mode reads, with exit 2 before anything runs. No tombstone is requested | `tests/test_scheduled_recovery.py::test_sj7_a_scheduled_actor_never_asks_for_a_tombstone`, `::test_sj7_the_scheduled_run_refuses_cancellation_before_reading_anything`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_the_modes_are_exclusive_and_cancellation_needs_an_administrator` |
| SJ8 | The scheduler principal: made by `scheduler_actor` and by enlistment, twice each; named through each guarded route; changed; granted the administrator role past its guard; present while every console list is read | It is made once, with the derived id, kind `SYSTEM`, label "ExecBound scheduler", number 1 and no space, and `principal.created` is recorded once. `scheduler_actor` and enlistment refuse an unfenced or inactive tenant and record nothing, enlistment mapping the function's own `INVALID_TENANT` to `INVALID_IDENTITY`. `access_effective_kind` answers `SYSTEM` and `access_has_permission` false, even with an administrator grant written past the guard. No change to it is accepted (deactivation, a relabel, a change of kind) and no principal becomes one; `principals_system_id` refuses a `SYSTEM` principal under another id and a person under the derived id. The guard refuses it in `credentials` (fenced by tenant), in `user_passkeys` written under an account fence with no tenant fence by an owner without bypass, and as a `replaced_by`, and migration 0064's downgrade refuses while one exists. `admin_issue_credential`, `admin_label_principal`, `admin_deactivate_principal` and `admin_reactivate_principal` refuse it with `SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL`; the owner CLI's `issue_credential` and the console's `POST /credentials/issue` refuse it with `INVALID_PRINCIPAL` and leave no credential and no file. Every console list core's published console API document names answers below 500 with it present and never shows its id or label, and so does every one the hosted areas' own document names, with the hosted plugin loaded; the four in each that refuse this client do so for reasons of their own, and each set is pinned. The scheduled check admits only the workspace's own active scheduler, only in a transaction fenced to that workspace, and nothing once the workspace is deactivated. Two concurrent passes making one is SJ13. | SJ9 | The code and the catalog | Every function in `src/` that names `ScheduledActor`, `RecoveryActor`, `actor_check` or `scheduled_check` is one the pin lists, and no import aliases one of those names; `store.identity_check` refuses a `ScheduledActor` with `INVALID_IDENTITY`, a lookalike whose kind is ADMIN included. The catalog test enumerates every foreign key to `principals`, every uuid column named for a principal and the listed columns whose names do not say so, and fails on a column `execbound.principal_columns` classifies as neither `GUARDED` nor `UNGUARDED` with a reason, or on a classified column that no longer exists. Every guarded column carries `system_guard_<column>` `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF` it, and migration 0064's copy of `GUARDED`, together with the `GUARDED_ADDED` of every later migration that guards a column of its own, equals the application's, no column guarded twice. The pin is a review tripwire that reads names; what refuses a scheduled actor anywhere else is `store.identity_check` | `tests/test_scheduled_actor.py::test_sj9_only_the_pinned_functions_accept_a_scheduled_actor`, `::test_identity_check_refuses_a_scheduled_actor`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_schema.py::test_sj9_every_principal_column_is_classified`, `::test_sj9_every_guarded_column_has_the_guard_and_the_migration_agrees` |
