| SJ15 | `scheduler_actor` and `assistant_turn_retire_due`, unfenced, fenced to an inactive tenant, and over turns completed 29 and 31 days ago by the database's clock | Neither takes an argument; each refuses an unfenced or inactive tenant with `INVALID_TENANT`; retirement keeps the 29-day turn, retires the 31-day one, and retires nothing on a second call | `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_schema.py::test_sj15_retirement_due_takes_no_time_and_needs_an_active_fenced_tenant`, `::test_sj15_retirement_due_keeps_29_days_and_retires_31`, `::test_sj8_the_scheduler_principal_refuses_an_unfenced_or_inactive_tenant` |
| SJ16 | Five workspaces walked from an injected start; the same walk with a budget spent after the first workspace; the next run from another start, cut short the same way | The walk begins just after the start, wraps round to it and visits all five; cut short, it visits one and counts four `not_reached`; the next run reaches a workspace the first did not | `tests/test_scheduled_jobs_cli.py::test_sj16_the_walk_starts_where_it_is_told_wraps_and_counts_what_the_budget_left` |

## Dormant catalog (DC1 to DC24)

The tables and functions only a plugin uses hold no runtime grant on any install unless a loaded plugin claims them, and the plugin's own upgrade command grants its claims in the transaction that applies the migrations (migration `0065_dormant_grants`; [Data model](DATA_MODEL.md#roles-and-connection-behavior)). Real PostgreSQL for every test that reads a grant. The core tests run with no plugin's grants, as a core install does; the hosted service's own tests run with its plugin loaded and its claims granted, as its deployment does. ES13 and ES14 carry the emergency stop's numbering because they extend its file.

| ID | Scenario | Expected | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DC1 | The dormant catalog | `DORMANT_TABLES` and `DORMANT_FUNCTIONS` sit inside `TABLES` and `FUNCTIONS`, ten tables and twenty-seven functions each named once and pinned by name, 37 names in `DORMANT`; `account_switch`, the teammate invitation, sample target, people number, personal access token and scheduled jobs objects are not dormant | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc1_the_catalog_marks_the_dormant_objects_inside_the_core_catalog` |
| DC2 | `Registry.claim` and the loader: a valid claim; claims outside the catalog or the grammar; a second claim by one plugin; two plugins claiming one object; a conflict beside an unknown plugin; a second load that adds, leaves one out, or differs; a load refused after a claim; `honour_claims` called directly with an invalid or a conflicting claim | The valid claim is on the loaded plugins and honoured, and `NO_PLUGINS` claims nothing. A core table or function (`account_switch` included), an unknown or non-string name, an empty, string, bytes, non-iterable or unhashable set, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `MAINTAIN`, `EXECUTE` on a table, a lowercase privilege, an uppercase or injected column, a whole-table and a column claim for one privilege, `SELECT` on a function, and a `str` subclass that compares equal to anything or renders as another name are each `INVALID_CLAIM`; a refused name and a refused privilege are logged as `plugins.refused` with the code and the plugin and without the claimed text; a second claim by one plugin is `INVALID_CLAIM`. Two plugins claiming one object are `PLUGIN_CONFLICT`, an unknown plugin beside them refuses `UNKNOWN_PLUGIN` first, and neither honours anything. A later load adds to what is honoured, one that leaves a claim out takes nothing away, and one that differs is `PLUGIN_CONFLICT` and changes nothing. A load refused after a claim (a duplicate console path, an unknown second plugin, a `register` that raises) honours nothing. `honour_claims` raises `ValueError` for an invalid claim and `ClaimConflict` for a conflicting one, and records nothing | `tests/test_plugins.py::test_dc2_a_plugin_claims_dormant_objects_and_the_process_honours_them`, `::test_dc2_a_claim_outside_the_catalog_or_its_grammar_is_refused`, `::test_dc2_a_refused_claim_is_logged_by_code_and_plugin_and_never_echoed`, `::test_dc2_a_plugin_claims_an_object_once`, `::test_dc2_two_plugins_claiming_one_object_conflict_and_nothing_is_honoured`, `::test_dc2_a_claim_conflict_is_found_after_every_plugin_resolves`, `::test_dc2_a_later_load_adds_to_what_is_honoured_and_never_changes_it`, `::test_dc2_a_refused_load_honours_nothing`, `::test_dc2_honouring_an_invalid_or_conflicting_claim_changes_nothing` |
| DC3 | A core test database with no plugin; then five grant states: nothing, what the runtime held before 0065, claims beside grants to PUBLIC, claims beside extra grants, and an overload of a dormant function's name granted to the runtime | With no plugin the runtime holds nothing on any dormant object, `check_runtime` passes, and the owner's `check_dormant` with no claims passes. In every state `check_runtime`'s own reading of the grants, from the rows of its functions query, equals `dormant_grants`' | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc3_with_no_plugin_the_runtime_holds_nothing_on_any_dormant_object`, `::test_dc3_the_runtime_check_reads_the_grants_dormant_grants_reads` |
| DC4 | With no claim honoured, one grant at a time: `SELECT` on each dormant table and `EXECUTE` on each dormant function to the runtime role; a `SELECT` and an `EXECUTE` to PUBLIC; and grants only the dormant reading refuses, `TRIGGER`, `REFERENCES` and `MAINTAIN` on a whole dormant table and `INSERT` and `REFERENCES` on one column (`onboarding_invitations.note`) | `check_runtime` raises `DormantGrantsRefused` for every one, and so does `check_dormant` with no claims | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc4_a_dormant_object_nobody_claims_refuses_any_grant` |
| DC5 | A claim of one table, whole and on one column, and one function: honoured and not granted; granted; then each of an unclaimed column, `INSERT`, another table's `SELECT` and PUBLIC's `EXECUTE` on an unclaimed function added; then a claimed column grant taken away | Claimed and not granted is refused; granted exactly, `check_runtime` and `check_dormant` pass; every extra grant, and the missing column, is refused with `DormantGrantsRefused` | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc5_a_claimed_object_holds_exactly_its_claim` |
| DC6 | A claimed dormant table also granted `DELETE`, or `TRUNCATE`, to the runtime role | `check_runtime` refuses it as unsafe table privileges, whatever the claim | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc6_no_claim_lets_the_runtime_delete_or_truncate_a_dormant_table` |
| DC7 | `grant_dormant` over stray grants to the runtime role and to PUBLIC; `grant_dormant` given a claim no plugin could make; a claimed privilege also granted to PUBLIC or held with a grant option; an overload of a dormant function's name granted to the runtime role | `grant_dormant` leaves exactly the claim, which `check_dormant` accepts, and with no claims leaves nothing; a core table, `SELECT` on a function, `DELETE`, a whole-table and a column claim for one privilege, and an injected column are refused with `ValueError`. Where the runtime's reach equals the claim but PUBLIC holds it too, a grant option is held, or the overload is granted, `check_dormant` still refuses, and `grant_dormant` converges the first two again | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc7_grant_dormant_sets_exactly_the_claim_whatever_the_database_held`, `::test_dc7_grant_dormant_refuses_what_no_plugin_could_claim`, `::test_dc7_check_dormant_reads_every_acl_entry_not_only_what_the_runtime_can_do`, `::test_dc7_check_dormant_refuses_a_grant_on_another_function_of_a_dormant_name` |
| DC8 | Down to `0064_scheduled_jobs`, then up to `0065_dormant_grants` | After 0065's downgrade to 0064 the runtime holds exactly what it held before 0065 (the hosted plugin's claims), and PUBLIC executes no dormant function, because the downgrade does not give back PUBLIC's `EXECUTE` on `onboarding_invitation_revoke`; at 0065 it holds nothing and `check_dormant` with no claims passes | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc8_0065_takes_the_grants_away_and_its_downgrade_gives_back_the_runtimes` |
| DC9 | A caller's owner connection, inside a transaction it began, passed to Alembic in `config.attributes["connection"]`, upgraded from 0064 to the head and then rolled back | The upgrade reaches `SCHEMA_REVISION` inside the caller's transaction, and after the rollback the database is at 0064 | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc9_a_callers_connection_owns_the_migrations_transaction` |
| DC17 | `python -m execbound.monitoring.commands` with `EXECBOUND_PLUGINS` naming an unknown plugin, then a plugin that claims a dormant table; then a command refused `DormantGrantsRefused` with no claim honoured and with one | An unknown plugin exits 2 with `Plugin refused: UNKNOWN_PLUGIN.`; the named plugin's claim is honoured before the command runs; the refusal exits 2 with the sentence naming `EXECBOUND_PLUGINS` with no claim and the one naming the plugin's upgrade command with one, and the database is closed | `tests/test_monitoring_commands.py::test_dc17_the_commands_load_the_plugins_the_environment_names`, `::test_dc17_a_named_plugins_claims_are_honoured_before_any_command_runs`, `::test_dc17_grants_present_or_missing_are_each_refused_with_their_own_sentence` |
| DC18 | `UPDATE_COLUMNS` | It no longer names `signup_messages`: the outbox's seven columns are the hosted plugin's claim | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc18_the_outbox_columns_are_the_plugins_claim_now` |
| DC19 | Every source under `src` but `db.py` and a plugin's own package | None names a dormant object as `execbound.<name>` | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc19_no_core_source_reaches_a_dormant_object` |
| DC21 | A stand-in for a Supabase platform role holding a dormant table's `SELECT` and a dormant function's `EXECUTE`; then granted a claimed function after the claims; and a role holding a grant option that grants a claimed function to PUBLIC | `grant_dormant` takes the platform role's grants away; granted after the claims, the runtime's reach is still the claim and `check_dormant` refuses until `grant_dormant` runs again. The other grantor's grant survives `grant_dormant`, and `check_dormant` refuses it. Each is skipped where the test owner cannot create a role | `tests/test_dormant_catalog.py::test_dc21_grant_dormant_revokes_what_a_platform_role_holds`, `::test_dc21_a_grant_by_another_grantor_survives_grant_dormant_and_is_refused` |
| ES13 | `execbound tenant-stop` with no plugin loaded against a database holding a claimed object's grant; then with the claim honoured and the grant missing; then with the two matching | Exit 2 with "Database refused: it holds grants for a plugin this process has not loaded. Set EXECBOUND_PLUGINS in the real environment to the plugins the deployment runs, and run the command again." and no stop; then exit 2 with "Database refused: its grants do not match what the plugins this process has loaded claim. Restore them with the upgrade command of the plugin that claims them, then run the command again." and no stop; never a traceback; then the tenant is stopped | `tests/test_emergency_stop.py::test_es13_the_kill_switch_names_the_fix_for_either_grant_mismatch` |
| ES14 | Every command on the runtime connection, in the same two mismatches: the emergency stop and its seven siblings, the six `bundle-*` commands, `audit-export`, `recover` and `notify` in all three modes, `reconcile`, `notifications`, the three export commands and `invitations-run` | Each exits 2 with the sentence for the mismatch, prints nothing on standard output and no traceback, and changes nothing: no stop, no audited event, no export | `tests/test_emergency_stop.py::test_es14_every_command_on_the_runtime_connection_names_the_fix` |

Tests with no DC number cover the rest of the refusals. A plugin command refused by the runtime check prints the same sentences as core's commands (`tests/test_plugin_surfaces.py::test_a_plugin_command_that_fails_says_only_how`), quickstart keeps its own refusal and never advises running it again (`tests/test_quickstart.py::test_a_runtime_check_refusal_stops_quickstart_without_advice_to_run_it_again`), and a run over a tenants file skips every tenant at another schema revision but stops at the first refused for its grants (`tests/test_jobs.py::test_a_revision_mismatch_skips_every_tenant`, `::test_a_refusal_for_the_dormant_grants_stops_the_run`).

## Agent capacity (AC1 to AC13)

The agent limit is the workspace's own `tenants.max_agents`, null for uncapped, since migration `0066_max_agents` ([Data model](DATA_MODEL.md#agent-limit)). The core tests set the limit directly, as an operator would, with no plan involved; the hosted service proves the same cap through its plans in its own inventory, which also keeps AC11, its plan view. Real PostgreSQL throughout.

| ID | Scenario | Expected | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC1 | Six agents created in a workspace with no limit | All six succeed | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac1_no_limit_is_uncapped` |
| AC2 | Workspaces with limits of 0, 1, 3 and 5, filled to the limit and then asked for one more | Every agent up to the limit succeeds and the next is refused `PLAN_LIMIT` | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac2_a_limit_refuses_exactly_the_agent_past_it` |
| AC3 | A limit of three, filled; one agent put to sleep, then woken; one deactivated by hand, a replacement created and the first reactivated; a person created; then the limit lowered to one | A sleeping agent still counts and waking it is never refused; a hand deactivation frees its allowance, and reactivating it at the limit is refused `PLAN_LIMIT`; a person is never counted; lowering the limit leaves all three agents held and refuses the next creation | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac3_what_the_limit_counts` |
| AC4 | Two creations racing a limit of three's last allowance, both held at the fence and the tenant-row lock and released together | Exactly one succeeds and the other is refused `PLAN_LIMIT`; the workspace holds three | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac4_two_creations_race_the_last_allowance_and_one_wins` |
| AC5 | A limit of -1 | Refused by the check `tenants_max_agents` | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac5_a_negative_limit_is_refused_by_the_schema` |
| AC6 | At `0065_dormant_grants`, one script of four creations, a hand deactivation, a creation, the hand reactivation, a sleep, a creation and the wake, run on a `sandbox`, a `free` and a `team` workspace; then the upgrade to `0066_max_agents` and the same script on a second workspace of each plan | The backfill gives `free` 3 and the others null, and every decision after the upgrade is the one the plan cap made before it: `free` refuses three times, `sandbox` and `team` never | `tests/test_max_agents_migration.py::test_ac6_the_limit_reproduces_every_decision_the_plan_cap_made` |
| AC7 | Up from 0065 and back, once with a `team` workspace's limit set to 7 by hand and once with it cleared | The patched functions keep their grants, the two core ones the runtime's `EXECUTE` and the three dormant ones none. The downgrade refuses "An agent limit differs from its plan's; downgrade refused" while the hand-set limit is there; once it is cleared, the downgrade restores the five function bodies byte for byte with the same grants and drops the column | `tests/test_max_agents_migration.py::test_ac7_the_downgrade_refuses_a_hand_set_limit_and_otherwise_restores_everything` |
| AC8 | The upgrade and the downgrade as an owner without `BYPASSRLS`, over a `sandbox`, a `free` and a `team` tenant, with the `team` limit then set to 10 and cleared | The backfill sees every tenant (`free` 3, the others null) and puts `FORCE` back; the downgrade refuses the hand-set limit although this owner sees no tenant unfenced, and once it is cleared commits with the column gone and `FORCE` on | `tests/test_max_agents_migration.py::test_ac8_the_backfill_and_the_downgrade_see_every_tenant_as_the_hosted_owner` |
| AC9 | The source of the modules a request passes through: `execbound.requests`, `identity`, `runtime`, the HTTP, MCP and human surfaces, the console's approval routes, and every module of `execbound.execution`, `execbound.policy`, `execbound.resolution`, `execbound.arcade` and `execbound.provider` | None names `max_agents`, `agent_capacity` or `execbound.capacity` | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac9_nothing_on_the_request_path_reads_the_agent_limit` |
| AC10 | `execbound.capacity.agent_capacity` on a workspace with a limit of two, after one creation, then after a hand deactivation and a sleep | It reads the limit and counts (2, 1, 0), then counts exactly what the cap counts, one held and one asleep; `execbound.plans` cannot be imported and `execbound.administration` has no `plan_summary` | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac10_core_reads_the_limit_and_no_plan` |
| AC12 | The runtime role and `tenants.max_agents` | Under `check_runtime` the runtime reads the limit through the table-wide `SELECT`; it holds `SELECT` on the column and no `INSERT`, `UPDATE` or `REFERENCES`, `tenants` carries no column-level grant, and the runtime's `UPDATE` of the limit is refused | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac12_the_runtime_reads_the_limit_and_holds_nothing_that_writes_it` |
| AC13 | A workspace with a limit of one and its scheduler principal, made by `scheduler_actor` | The scheduler principal is not counted: the capacity reads (1, 0, 0), one agent is still created, and the next is refused `PLAN_LIMIT` | `tests/test_agent_capacity.py::test_ac13_the_scheduler_principal_is_never_counted` |
| HF50 | The Agents page's line on a workspace with no limit, then after its `max_agents` is set to 3 with one of three agents asleep | The line carries no `plan` in either state; `agent_limit` reads null, then 3; `agents_held` 1, then 3, and `agents_dormant` 0, then 1: the sleeping agent still counts as held | `tests/test_console_api_agents.py` |
| HF51 | A workspace at its agent limit of three (`max_agents` 3) with one agent asleep, read through a real browser at 1280 and 390 px in both colour schemes | The line reads "Agents: 3 of 3.", then the limit sentence, "This workspace has reached its agent limit. Every agent you have keeps running." (the refusal toast's own words), then the dormancy sentence; the sleeping row carries an "Asleep" badge; every page is axe-clean | `tests/test_browser_console_directory.py` |
| HF52 | A sleeping agent and a hand-deactivated agent, in the row list and in each one's detail page | The row and the detail both carry a `dormant` field distinct from `active`, so asleep and deactivated read differently rather than both as merely inactive | `tests/test_console_api_agents.py` |

The Agents page's line is held by HF50 and HF51 above. The console's own tests hold the line's text (`directory/agents.test.tsx`: "Agents: 5." with no limit, "Agents: 1 of 3." under it, and the limit sentence at or over it). Core's Workspace settings ask for no plan and show no plan card (`settings/WorkspaceService.test.tsx`).

## Denial notice (DN1 to DN10)

An agent is told that a request was denied, the request's reference and one of three categories, and never the reason itself: a mistaken, prompt-injected or compromised agent gets no map of the policy from its denials, and a reviewer's words stay with people. DN1 to DN3 cover the notice itself, which discloses nothing new; DN4 to DN10 cover the `busy` and `limit` categories and the audit's specific causes for a refusal before any plan, with the readers those causes reach (threat row T1.16). Real PostgreSQL, the live endpoint provider, HTTP through the application and MCP over a real Streamable HTTP server.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DN1 | A domain controller's isolation over MCP, which the endpoint pack's deny rule refuses (`NOT_AUTHORIZED`); then one isolation under a principal budget of one that denies, and a second over HTTP (`HARD_LIMIT:isolation-budget`), each re-sent and read back on both surfaces; a third through the Python client's `execbound_action`; an administrator searching the console for each reference; a pending approval refused by a reviewer with a distinctive comment, then re-sent and read over HTTP and MCP; a `DENIED` view built without a reference | Each denial answers 200 `DENIED` with exactly the view's members, `denial` and `notice`: the deny rule's and the reviewer's `withheld` with "Denied. The reason is recorded for a person, who can find it by searching the console for EB-n. Re-sending this request returns this same answer.", the budget's `limit` with its own sentence (DN4), each naming its own reference; no member carries the recorded reason, the rule id, the fact or the words `NOT_AUTHORIZED`, `HARD_LIMIT` or `HUMAN_DENIED`; the MCP text content is the structured content and `isError` is false; re-sends and status reads answer the same bytes; the client's summary carries the same category and sentence and nothing of why; the console search for `EB-n` finds the denied execution and its record carries the reason; the refused request shows `approval_state` `DENIED`, the disclosure the view already made, while the comment, recorded in the audit chain, appears in no answer; the view without a reference gets the sentence without one | `tests/test_denial_notice.py::test_dn1_a_denied_answer_names_its_reference_and_nothing_of_why`, `::test_dn1_a_reviewers_refusal_shows_as_the_approval_state_and_never_as_the_comment`, `::test_dn1_a_denied_view_without_a_reference_still_says_nothing_of_why` |
| DN2 | A pending approval over HTTP, read over MCP; the same request re-sent after its approval deadline; the target made autonomous and executed over MCP; an observing tenant's domain controller over HTTP; then every execution state through the presenter with no keys and no database | `PENDING_APPROVAL`, `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`, `EXECUTED` and `OBSERVED` carry no `notice`; the observed would-be denial keeps `observed.reasons` with `NOT_AUTHORIZED`, the deliberate exception; through the presenter, `DENIED` with no recorded category gains exactly `denial` `withheld` and the withheld notice, and every other state, with neither member, is answered exactly as the view serializes | `tests/test_denial_notice.py::test_dn2_no_other_state_carries_a_notice`, `::test_dn2_the_presenter_adds_a_notice_to_a_denial_and_to_no_other_state` |
| DN3 | The `execute` tool's description, the agent guide and its OpenAPI schema, every agent instruction shipped (the OpenAI Agents recipe and both client tool examples, one of which the Claude Agent SDK recipe names), and every Python source under `src/` | The description tells the model to call `list_actions` for the operation_ref values its space offers (LA8), that a denial's reason is kept for a person and to relay the `request_ref` rather than guess, in under 800 characters; the guide states the rule and its reason, shows the notice, promises only that re-sending the same request returns the same answer, states the `approval_state` disclosure, covers an observe-only mapping as well as a workspace, answers a busy target as a denial, lists exactly the reasons a denial records (with `HUMAN_DENIED`, without `APPROVAL_REQUIRED` or `INVALID_APPROVAL`, which never end a request as denied) and lists no `RESOURCE_BUSY` error; the schema describes `notice` for `DENIED` alone, with the withheld sentence among its examples; each instruction tells the agent to give the person the `request_ref` on a denial; no source raises `RESOURCE_BUSY` as an error or maps it to a status, and `ERROR_STATUS` has no entry for it | `tests/test_denial_notice.py::test_dn3_the_execute_tool_says_where_actions_come_from_and_to_relay_the_reference`, `::test_dn3_the_guide_withholds_the_reason_and_answers_a_busy_target_as_a_denial`, `::test_dn3_every_agent_instruction_shipped_tells_it_to_relay_the_reference`, `::test_dn3_the_claude_agent_sdk_recipe_names_the_example_that_relays`, `::test_dn3_nothing_in_the_gateway_raises_a_busy_target_as_an_error` |
| DN4 | Each of four denials, first over HTTP and then first over MCP: the world's own target while another agent's admission holds its claim (`RESOURCE_BUSY`); a second target under a principal budget of one already spent (`HARD_LIMIT:isolation-budget`); a domain controller under that spent budget (`HARD_LIMIT:isolation-budget` and `NOT_AUTHORIZED`); a domain controller with no budget (`NOT_AUTHORIZED`). Each re-sent and read back on both surfaces; the busy one again after the holder executes and its claim is released, then a new request for the same target. Separately, a reviewer's refusal read over HTTP and re-sent over MCP, and a limit denial through the Python client's `execbound_action`; and, under a spent principal budget, one target the policy allows and one a deny rule forbids | The recorded transition carries exactly those codes. The answers are `busy`, `limit`, `limit` and `withheld` (any recorded limit is `limit`, whatever sits beside it), each with exactly the view's members, `denial` and `notice`, and the notice is the category's: "Denied because another request holds this target. Re-sending this request returns this same answer; a new request after that one settles may be decided differently. A person can find it by searching the console for EB-n.", "Denied because a shared limit is spent. Re-sending this request returns this same answer; a new request after the limit resets is decided afresh. A person can find it by searching the console for EB-n." or the withheld sentence. No answer carries `NOT_AUTHORIZED`, `HARD_LIMIT`, `RESOURCE_BUSY`, the rule id, the fact, or the holding execution's id or reference. The first answer, the HTTP re-send and status read (byte for byte) and the MCP re-send and status read are one answer, and after the holder settles they still say `busy`; the new request is not denied. The reviewer's refusal is `withheld` with `approval_state` `DENIED` and no comment; the client's summary carries `denial` `limit` and its sentence and no rule id; the allowed and the forbidden target under the spent budget record `HARD_LIMIT:isolation-budget` and `HARD_LIMIT:isolation-budget` with `NOT_AUTHORIZED`, and answer alike but for their own identifiers, so the category is no per-target policy verdict | `tests/test_denial_categories.py::test_dn4_a_denial_names_its_category_and_nothing_more`, `::test_dn4_a_reviewers_refusal_and_the_clients_summary_carry_the_category`, `::test_dn4_under_a_spent_limit_an_allowed_and_a_forbidden_target_answer_alike` |
| DN5 | Every execution state through the presenter with no keys and no database; a `DENIED` view with each category and with none; a view of any other state constructed with a category | A denial gains exactly `denial` and the category's sentence, `withheld` when the view records none, and the category is not part of the view's own serialization; every other state carries neither member and is answered exactly as the view serializes; a category on any state but `DENIED` is refused at construction | `tests/test_denial_categories.py::test_dn5_the_presenter_adds_a_category_to_a_denial_and_to_no_other_state` |

`console/src/components/board/board.test.tsx` ("a request refused before a plan was frozen", and "the ticker"'s two limit cases) |

The action listing, which applies the same rule to what an agent can discover, is the LA group below.

## Action listing (LA1 to LA8)

An agent lists the operations its own space offers, through `GET /v1/actions` and the MCP tool `list_actions`, and admission refuses every other operation exactly as it refuses an unknown one: the same 503, the same statements and no record, with the cause in the audit alone. It closes the two oracles threat row T1.16 records (threat row T1.17). A listed action is not permission, and a target is never listed. Real PostgreSQL, the live endpoint provider, HTTP through the application and MCP over a real Streamable HTTP server. The console's board words the two causes admission now audits for an operation outside the space, `INVALID_CONFIGURATION` and `INVALID_BUNDLE`, as refusals before any plan, in the `console/src/components/board/board.test.tsx` cases DN10 cites.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

## Console editions (CE1 to CE15)

The console is built as one of two editions from one source tree ([Surfaces](SURFACES.md#how-the-console-is-served)): the core edition, which the package and the self-hosted image carry and which never contains hosted code, and the hosted edition, which adds the hosted service's pages, cards and counts through the slots of one `Edition` (`console/src/edition/context.ts`). The server lists core's console pages only, and serves a plugin's where that plugin is loaded. The Python rows run on real PostgreSQL, the browser rows in real Chromium, and the console rows under Vitest, which resolves `import()` without chunking, so the hosted build's own manifest is the evidence that its cards load with their own chunks (CE11).

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CE1 | The core build and core's lint over hosted code: a module under `console/src/hosted/` among those the core build loaded, a `?raw` one included, and an asset it emitted from one; `src/hosted` or the root reached through a link; a static, re-exported or lazy import of a hosted path, and `@edition` or `@route-tree`, in a core file | The core build fails with "The core build reached hosted code:" and every hosted module it reached, and not `src/hostedness.ts`, which only looks alike; an asset emitted from a hosted file fails it too; `src/hosted` is named by its real path where it or the root is a link; the core build's configuration runs that refusal in its own bundle and in every worker's, resolves both aliases to core's files and writes into the package; core's stylesheets name no hosted file, and Tailwind's scan for core leaves the hosted sources out. ESLint refuses each such import, and either alias, in every core file under `src/` and `scripts/`; it leaves alone a core import, a directory named alike (`hostedness`, `ghosted`, `unhosted`) and a lazy core chunk, lets `src/main.tsx` name the aliases while still refusing it hosted code, and leaves the hosted edition's own modules to import each other | `console/scripts/editions.test.ts` ("the core build's refusal of hosted code", "the core-only plugin", "the core build's configuration", "core's stylesheets"); `console/scripts/bans.test.ts` ("core's edition bans", "the project's edition bans") |

Core's Workspace settings asks for no plan, product counts or sandbox, My account for no preference, Getting started starts from core's own reads, the shell asks nothing for a count and lists no Invitations entry, and the palette offers no edition link whatever features the session reports. A stand-in edition's content appears where its slot is placed, after core's own: an entry after core's in the sidebar, the tab bar, the breadcrumbs and the palette, and in the tab bar only where it asks; top bar items among the actions; panels after the workspace's requests and between the profile and the data requests; definitions after the active bundle; Getting started's part after its steps; a workstation where core names none, and none where core names its own, while core's answer is on its way or where it failed; and `event` at the shell's opening and when a decision completes an approval | `console/src/edition/core.test.tsx`; `console/src/shell/shell.test.tsx` ("shows an approver Home, Approvals, Activity, Live board and Settings", "the edition in the shell"); `console/src/components/settings/WorkspaceService.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/tenant/TenantSettings.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/settings/PersonalSettings.test.tsx` and `console/src/components/welcome/WelcomePage.test.tsx` (each "" case); `console/src/approvals/approvals.test.tsx` ("tells the edition when a decision completes the approval"); `console/src/components/directory/agents.test.tsx` ("names an edition's workstation where core names none", "where an edition's workstation stays out"); `console/src/components/ui/CommandPalette.test.tsx` ("an edition's links in the palette") |

The hosted service's own tests, which are not published, hold the rest: each hosted card (the three settings panels, `Welcome`, `FixtureHint`) and the counter is a dynamic entry of the hosted build that `index.html`'s static imports never reach, and each lazy slot, once loaded, renders its card. The test that reads the hosted build's manifest passes without checking anything where `console/dist/hosted` is absent. In the development repository, CI builds the hosted edition before the suite, and CI's `console` job holds `pnpm build:hosted` to every budget | `console/scripts/budget.test.ts` ("charges a hosted edition's route like any other route", "holds a workspace approval link, opened cold, to the approval budget"); in the development repository, CI's `console` job (the budget itself); and the hosted service's own tests of the hosted build's manifest and of its slots |
| CE12 | A plugin's session feature check that raises, directly and through the console session; one refused by the database | The feature is withheld, the others still read, and `plugins.feature_failed` is logged at `ERROR` with the feature's name and none of the exception's text; the console session still answers 200, with the feature in neither `invitations` nor `features`; a check that raises `DormantGrantsRefused` propagates, and no feature failure is logged | `tests/test_plugins.py::test_a_session_feature_whose_check_raises_is_withheld_and_logged_by_name`, `::test_a_session_feature_check_refused_by_the_database_is_not_swallowed`; `tests/test_plugin_surfaces.py::test_a_session_feature_check_that_raises_withholds_the_feature_and_the_session_answers` |
| CE13 | The hosted top bar in real Chromium, for an account's session with the workspace chip, at 320, 360, 390 and 500 px with Ask, 480 px without it, and at 900 and 1024 px; core's own at 900 and 1024 px; and both at 768 px, the narrowest width with the sidebar, with Ask and without it | Up to 500 px the bar holds core's six controls and no seventh, none over another or in a gutter, in both schemes, nothing scrolls sideways and axe finds nothing; from 360 px the Space switcher keeps a usable width; Menu's navigation sheet offers "Create a workspace" among its actions, inside the sheet's own box, and it opens `/signup`. At 900 and 1024 px the entry and Ask show their icons alone, each keeping its name, and at 1280 px the entry shows its label; core's bar at 900 and 1024 px, with Ask where a deployment has it, has nothing over anything else and nothing in the gutter. At 768 px, beside this long a workspace name, the emergency stop's whole box is on screen with room for a 15 px classic scrollbar, with Ask and without it: in core's bar with its label shown and Ask, where there is one, after it, and in the hosted bar with the entry after it | `tests/test_browser_workspaces.py::test_no_top_bar_control_overlaps_another_on_a_tablet_or_small_laptop`, `::test_the_emergency_stop_stays_on_screen_at_768_px`; for the hosted top bar, the hosted service's own tests |
| CE15 | The hosted cards' reads failing, first and in the background | The plan card asks again once on its own after any failure, a 404 included, and then shows the failure with Try again, which reads the plan again; the consent card shows a failure with Try again, a 404 included, which reads the setting again, keeping its heading and the focus while it does; the product counts show a failure with Try again, which reads them again; Getting started's hosted part shows a first read that failed as a failure with Try again, which reads it again; a card that has loaded, the plan, the counts, the fixture inventory with its disclosure open, and core's own data and closure requests, keeps what it read through a background read that fails | the hosted edition's own tests; `console/src/components/settings/WorkspaceService.test.tsx` ("keeps the requests it read through a background read that fails") |

Tests live in `tests/test_console_serving.py`, `tests/test_console_api_base.py`, `tests/test_plugin_surfaces.py`, `tests/test_plugins.py`, `tests/test_console_api_samples.py`, `tests/test_console_api_tokens.py`, `tests/test_route_authority.py`, `tests/test_browser_workspaces.py` and `tests/test_key_rotation.py`; in the console's `scripts/editions.test.ts`, `scripts/bans.test.ts` and `scripts/budget.test.ts`, `src/edition/core.test.tsx` and the core pages' tests CE2 names; in the development repository's CI, which holds the hosted build to the budgets (CE11); and, for the hosted plugin and its edition, in the hosted service's own tests.

## Console shell and navigation (NV1 to NV19)

The console's shell around every page ([Surfaces](SURFACES.md#how-the-console-is-served), **The console's shell**): page names and the document title, the bell, the keyboard, the phone's navigation sheet, the command palette's Recent, commands and all-spaces retry, focus, Help, a failed page's report, release navigation and the first paint. Like CR and CE, whose evidence is mostly the console's own Vitest suites, the group is not in the validation manifest's prefix list. The browser rows run in real Chromium against the real gateway on real PostgreSQL.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |

| NV2 | Two approvals assigned, then a third while the tab is hidden; the tab shown again | "(2)" then "(3)" in the title; no request of any kind while hidden; the count corrected on return | `console/src/shell/title.test.tsx` ("counts the approvals waiting for this person", "reads 50+", "asks for nothing while the tab is hidden"); `tests/test_browser_console_shell.py::test_nv2_the_title_counts_what_waits_and_asks_nothing_while_hidden` |

## Self-hosted export (EX1 to EX10)

What leaves the development repository for the public self-hosted one, and what the public copy does without the hosted plugin. `scripts/export_foss.py` builds the public tree from one commit, checks it and publishes it; `scripts/ci.py` is the public repository's CI, run one stage at a time. EX1 to EX4, EX6 and EX7 test the development repository's own export tooling and are kept in its private inventory. EX5's stage list, EX8, EX9 and EX10's preflight and provenance are exported tests and are registered as group `EX` in `ACCEPTANCE_PREFIXES`; EX10's workflow tests are private, like EX5's. The builder's and publisher's tests use a fixture repository, a local bare target and a fake API, never the network; EX8's pages and EX9's token calls run on real PostgreSQL.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EX5 | `scripts/ci.py --print` for every stage and for chosen ones, with the defaults and with every setting from the environment, a setting it cannot use and an unknown stage; a failed stage; the script under the system Python; the public `ci.yml` and `export-suite` | The stages are `sync`, `lint`, `licences`, `console`, `tools`, `database`, `suite`, `package`, `validation`, `restore`, `monitoring`, `image`, `provenance`, `fresh-machine` and `cleanup`, and `all` prints each in that order and runs nothing; ports, names and workers come from the environment, the worker count otherwise one per CPU the machine gives the run and at most four, and one that cannot be used is refused before anything runs; the restore commands never show the password; a failed stage skips the rest but not `cleanup`; the script needs nothing but the system Python; `provenance` describes the image `image` built, from an environment synced as the `Dockerfile` syncs the image's and installed apart from the project's own, records the run GitHub names, and `cleanup` removes that environment and nothing it published. The public workflow calls every stage once and in order, `cleanup` whatever failed before it, on GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` alone, with every action pinned to a full commit and a token that reads the repository and nothing else; the export puts it in place of the development repository's workflow; `export-suite` runs every stage against a tree built from its own commit | `tests/test_ci_stages.py::test_the_stages_are_the_workflows_in_order`, `::test_all_prints_every_stage_in_order_and_runs_nothing`, `::test_every_setting_comes_from_the_environment`, `::test_the_default_worker_count_follows_the_machine`, `::test_an_explicit_worker_count_wins_over_the_machine`, `::test_a_setting_that_cannot_be_used_is_refused_before_anything_runs`, `::test_an_unknown_stage_is_a_usage_error`, `::test_the_restore_commands_never_show_the_password`, `::test_a_failed_stage_skips_the_rest_but_not_cleanup`, `::test_the_script_runs_with_the_system_python_alone`, `::test_provenance_describes_the_image_the_image_stage_built`, `::test_the_sbom_environment_is_synced_as_the_dockerfile_syncs_the_image`, `::test_a_real_provenance_run_records_the_run_and_installs_apart`, `::test_cleanup_removes_the_environment_provenance_described`, `tests/internal/test_public_workflow.py::test_the_workflow_calls_every_stage_once_and_in_order`, `::test_cleanup_runs_whatever_failed_before_it`, `::test_the_workflow_runs_on_github_hosted_ubuntu_alone`, `::test_every_action_is_pinned_to_a_full_commit`, `::test_the_token_reads_the_repository_and_nothing_else`, `::test_the_export_puts_it_in_place_of_this_repositorys_workflow`, `::test_the_export_suite_runs_every_stage_against_a_tree_built_from_the_commit` |
| EX8 | `ServiceLinks` with each kind of bad link and address, and the longest it admits; a runtime file with a `service` block, a bad one and one changed on reload; a gateway with no service links, with only core's default, with links from its configuration, from a plugin, and from both; a `service` block that leaves out `source_url`, names another or sets it to `null`; the sign-in page, the footers, the MFA pages and the console session; a plugin registering links twice, and two plugins registering them | A link is `https://` with a host and no user information, printable, at most 200 characters and free of quotes, angle brackets, backquotes and backslashes, while an `@` after the host is admitted; an address is one `@` between letters, digits and `._+-` and a dotted domain of at most 190 characters, so nothing in it can add a header to a `mailto:` link; a bad block or an unknown field refuses the file, and a changed block is refused on reload; without links no page links to a service page; with neither a block nor a plugin's links every footer links only Source, to the public repository, a block keeps it unless it names another address or `null`, and a plugin's links replace it with theirs; with them each page links exactly the deployment's addresses and the session carries them in `service`; a plugin sets them once, and a second plugin's links, or a block beside a plugin's, is `PLUGIN_CONFLICT` and refuses start | `tests/test_service_links.py::test_no_service_links_is_every_link_absent`, `::test_a_link_is_an_https_address_of_at_most_two_hundred_characters`, `::test_a_link_carries_no_user_information`, `::test_an_at_sign_after_the_host_is_admitted`, `::test_a_support_address_is_one_at_sign_between_a_short_local_part_and_a_domain`, `::test_the_longest_address_and_link_are_admitted`, `::test_a_service_block_with_a_bad_link_or_an_unknown_field_refuses_the_file`, `::test_a_reload_that_changes_the_service_block_is_refused`, `::test_a_service_block_beside_a_plugins_links_refuses_start`, `::test_without_service_links_no_page_links_to_a_service_page`, `::test_the_sign_in_page_and_every_footer_link_the_deployments_pages`, `::test_the_mfa_pages_offer_account_help_only_at_the_deployments_address`, `::test_the_console_session_carries_the_deployments_service_links`, `::test_core_names_its_public_source_where_nothing_else_names_links`, `::test_a_service_block_keeps_the_source_link_unless_it_names_another_or_none`, `::test_every_footer_links_the_public_source_where_nothing_names_other_links`, `tests/test_plugins.py::test_a_plugin_sets_its_service_links_once`, `::test_service_links_are_exactly_a_service_links_whose_values_hold`, `::test_a_second_plugin_registering_service_links_conflicts`, `::test_the_service_links_come_from_the_configuration_or_a_plugin_never_both` |

## Step-up in place (SU1 to SU15)

The console's MFA step-up, as decided on 28 September 2026: the check stays on every route that had it, its window is thirty minutes, and the console asks for the factor in a dialog over the page instead of sending the person to Account security. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the client in Vitest; the case that prompted it in Chromium.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SU1 | A factor verified on a session, then the injected clock moved 29 and 31 minutes on, the session read every seven minutes so it stays alive; the console's step-up, a recovery-code replacement from the security page, and the definer function's own `recovery` change reached directly; a first-time enrollment 4 and 6 minutes after signing in | At 29 minutes the step-up passes and both changes go through; at 31 each is refused `RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED`, the definer function included (0067). `mfa.STEP_UP_WINDOW` is thirty minutes. Enrollment at 4 minutes starts and at 6 is refused: the sign-in's own age keeps its five minutes | `tests/test_mfa_sessions.py::test_a_verified_factor_steps_a_session_up_for_thirty_minutes`, `::test_the_database_holds_the_same_thirty_minute_window`, `::test_first_time_setup_still_needs_a_sign_in_within_five_minutes`, `::test_security_changes_require_recent_factor_and_rotation_invalidates_old_token` |
| SU2 | Migration 0067 applied from 0066 and rolled back | Only `fresh`'s interval changes in `account_security_change` and `user_security_change`, from five minutes to thirty; every other five-minute interval stays; both keep their ACLs; the downgrade restores both definitions byte for byte | `tests/test_step_up_window_migration.py::test_only_the_recent_factor_interval_moves_and_the_downgrade_restores_it` |
| SU3 | `POST /api/console/v1/session/verify` with an agent's bearer credential, with no session, with a wrong CSRF header, another Origin, `text/plain`, an unknown field, a 65-character code, and as a GET | 403 `FORBIDDEN` audited once; 401 `SESSION_REQUIRED` as JSON; 403 `INVALID_CSRF`; 403; 415; 400 `INVALID_BODY`; 400 naming `code`; 405; none of them touches the factor, whose code still works afterwards. A personal access token is refused by `token_rules.py` | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_only_a_signed_in_person_reaches_it`, `::test_the_token_the_origin_and_the_body_are_checked_first` |
| SU4 | An enrolled administrator whose factor is past the window: a wrong code, five wrong codes then a right one, an unused recovery code and the same one again | A wrong code answers 400 `INVALID_FACTOR`, "That code could not be used.", records `account_security.verification_failed`, sets no cookie, and the change still asks; after five the right code is refused too, the page's own lock; a recovery code steps the session up once, records `account_security.recovery_used`, leaves nine, and is refused a second time | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_a_wrong_code_says_so_and_changes_nothing`, `::test_five_wrong_codes_lock_verification_as_the_page_does`, `::test_a_recovery_code_steps_up_once` |
| SU5 | A token issue refused `MFA_REQUIRED`, a current code verified, the token issue sent again with the old CSRF token and then with the new one; a session still at the second step of signing in; an administrator with no factor; a deployment with no account security | The refusal creates nothing. The code answers 200 `{csrf_token}` under `no-store`, sets both cookies HttpOnly and SameSite=Strict, ends the old session token and keeps the session id, and records `account_security.verified`. The old token's resend is refused `INVALID_CSRF` and creates nothing; the new one's issues the token, and `GET /session` hands over the same new token. The signing-in session is refused `MFA_REQUIRED` and keeps `/mfa`; no factor answers 409 `CONFLICT`; no account security, 503 `UNAVAILABLE` | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_a_current_code_rotates_the_session_and_the_change_goes_through_resent_once`, `::test_a_session_still_signing_in_is_left_to_the_mfa_page`, `::test_without_a_factor_or_account_security_it_refuses` |
| SU6 | An account's password sign-in with a factor enrolled; its password changed twenty minutes on, then again thirty-five minutes on, verified in place and sent again | The change at twenty minutes goes through: the console's check and the database's agree. At thirty-five it is refused `MFA_REQUIRED`, the code (with the workspace header) answers a new token, the old token's resend is refused `INVALID_CSRF`, and the new token's goes through | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_an_account_changes_its_password_twenty_minutes_on_and_after_stepping_up` |
| SU7 | The console: a change refused `MFA_REQUIRED` then accepted; cancelled; refused again after a code; two refused together; a read refused `MFA_REQUIRED`; nothing registered to ask; a poll in flight when a code is checked. The Invite teammate dialog with a wrong code, a right code and Cancel. In Chromium, the case that prompted the change: an administrator with an authenticator, signed in with it thirty-four minutes earlier on the injected clock, opens Users, then Invite teammate, types an address and clicks Invite | The change is sent again once with the new CSRF token and the same body; Cancel returns the refusal with no navigation and the page says "This change was not made."; a second refusal is shown without a second question; two refusals share one question; a read still goes to `/mfa`, as does a change when nothing can ask; the code waits for the poll and later requests wait for the code. The dialog opens over the invitation's, which keeps the address; a wrong code says so and can be tried again; a right one sends the invitation, which appears under Pending invitations, without the page navigating; the session cookie has rotated and the page carries on with it; axe finds nothing in either scheme at 1280 and 390 px with the dialog open, nothing scrolls sideways, and the page records no policy or Trusted Types violation | `console/src/lib/client-config.test.ts` ("a change refused for a factor verified too long ago"), `console/src/components/StepUpDialog.test.tsx`; `tests/test_browser_console_step_up.py::test_an_invitation_asks_for_a_code_over_the_users_page_and_is_sent_without_leaving` |

The first registration is refused `MFA_REQUIRED` and registers nothing; after the code the same registration goes through; the switch is refused `MFA_REQUIRED` and the provider stays a draft. | SU9 | An enrolled administrator whose factor is past the window creates, changes and disables an approval rule, assigns and unassigns an approver, and assigns and unassigns a space member, then renames a space; then verifies a code and sends the same seven changes again | Each of the seven is refused `MFA_REQUIRED` before its body is read; the rename is not asked; after the code none of the seven is refused for the step-up. Added on 3 October 2026, since who approves a request and who acts in a space are as sensitive as people and access | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_who_approves_and_who_acts_in_a_space_ask_for_a_recent_factor`, `::test_every_step_up_prefix_covers_a_change_core_serves` |

Then: no passkey saved; five assertion failures then a valid assertion; the request checks; a token; an agent bearer | Each assertion failure answers 400 `INVALID_FACTOR`, records `verification_failed` and counts toward the lock; each challenge failure answers 400 with neither, and fresh options then succeed; no passkey saved answers 409 `CONFLICT`; after five failures the valid one answers 400 with no new event; a token is refused (the routes are `TOKEN_REFUSED`) and an agent bearer 403 `FORBIDDEN`; none but a success rotates the session | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_an_assertion_that_fails_verification_counts_and_is_audited`, `::test_a_spent_or_missing_challenge_is_refused_without_counting`, `::test_the_lock_refuses_a_valid_passkey_silently`, `::test_options_without_a_saved_passkey_conflict`, `::test_the_passkey_routes_check_the_request_first` |
| SU14 | Chromium with a virtual authenticator, the console served over `https://localhost` with a throwaway certificate: an administrator saves a passkey, the factor ages past the window, a key is revoked from the agent page with Use a passkey; past the window again, a second key the same way; the dialog's own states in Vitest (a click asking the browser synchronously, a spent challenge, a cancelled prompt, stale options) | The dialog reads "To revoke key N for Agent M, confirm it is you."; each key is revoked with one click and without the page navigating; axe finds nothing in either scheme at 1280 and 390 px with the dialog open; the browser is asked inside the click, options are fetched fresh after every attempt, kept after a cancelled prompt, and never used stale | `tests/test_browser_console_agents_account.py::test_su14_a_passkey_steps_up_a_key_revocation_over_the_agent_page`, `console/src/components/StepUpDialog.test.tsx` ("a passkey in the step-up") |
| SU15 | An enrolled workspace administrator whose factor is past the window switches the mode to Observe only, sets the emergency stop, asks for an admission, switches the mode again and releases the stop, without verifying a code | Both mode switches are refused `MFA_REQUIRED` and no `tenant.mode_changed` event is written, so the session was stale throughout; the stop answers 200 naming the administrator and its reason, writes one `tenant.stop_activated`, and the admission is refused `TENANT_STOPPED`; the release answers 200 with no stop and writes one `tenant.stop_released`; `guards.steps_up` matches neither `/tenant/stop` nor `/tenant/resume`. The stop is the immediate tightening control and stays outside the step-up by design (THREAT_MODEL.md T4.48); its release is outside it today too. Added on 20 October 2026 for gap 10 of the product control evidence: until then a change that put `/tenant/stop` in a step-up set failed no test | `tests/test_console_api_step_up.py::test_su15_the_emergency_stop_is_set_and_released_without_a_recent_factor` |

## First password (FP1 to FP7)

An account that signs in only with Google or GitHub adds its first password from Account security, under the bar provider linking already uses. Real PostgreSQL throughout, including an owner that does not bypass row security.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FP1 | A provider-only account signed in just now, with a second session open, sets a first password | The password is stored as `user_passwords` holds every other one (scrypt) and signs the account in by address; `account_security.password_set` is recorded with only the session id, and `user_security_events` holds `password_set`; the other session ends and the one that made the change stays | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_a_provider_account_sets_a_first_password_and_signs_in_with_it` |
| FP2 | An account that already has a password asks for a first one; then the definer function's `password_set` is reached directly | `PASSWORD_EXISTS`; the database refuses as well, the stored password is unchanged and still signs in, and nothing is recorded | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_an_account_with_a_password_cannot_set_another_this_way` |
| FP3 | The account's sign-in six minutes old; then the function reached directly | `RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` both ways; no password is written | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_a_sign_in_older_than_five_minutes_is_refused` |
| FP4 | An enrolled factor last verified 31 minutes ago; then the function reached directly; then a recovery code verified | Refused `RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` both ways; after the verification the password is set | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_an_enabled_factor_must_have_been_verified_recently` |
| FP5 | A password below the minimum | `INVALID_PASSWORD`; nothing written or recorded | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_a_password_below_the_minimum_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written` |
| FP6 | The same change under a schema owner with the hosted owner's attributes (`NOBYPASSRLS`) | The row is written and read back through that owner fenced to the account, and the password signs in | `tests/test_first_password.py::test_a_first_password_is_written_by_an_owner_that_does_not_bypass_row_security` |
| FP7 | Account security for a provider-only account: two different entries, then the same entry with characters outside ASCII; a sign-in six minutes old; an account with a password; a wrong CSRF token, a missing field and an extra one; migration 0070 applied and rolled back | "not the same", then set, the form gone afterwards and the address and password signing in, and no response carrying the password; "Sign in again first"; no form, and the route answers that a password exists; 403 for each; the upgrade adds only the branch and its rotation entry, keeps the grants, never updates a password row, and the downgrade restores the function byte for byte | `tests/test_first_password_page.py::test_a_provider_account_adds_a_password_and_then_signs_in_with_it`, `::test_a_sign_in_older_than_five_minutes_is_told_to_sign_in_again`, `::test_an_account_with_a_password_is_offered_no_form_and_the_route_refuses`, `::test_the_form_needs_its_csrf_and_its_own_fields`; `tests/test_first_password_migration.py::test_the_upgrade_adds_one_branch_and_the_downgrade_restores_the_function` |

## Security signals (SG1 to SG17)

An out-of-process EDR's verdict about the target decides an isolation through the operator's mapping, never on its own authority, and every way it can be missing fails closed ([Security signals](SIGNALS.md); threat rows T13.1 to T13.18). `tests/signal_conformance.py` holds the producer-facing rows every producer runs with its own envelope builder: the kernel's reference producer in `tests/test_signal_conformance.py`, and Defender for Endpoint's producer, `integrations/mde-producer`, in `tests/test_mde_producer_conformance.py`. Each runs through the module's one harness, `check`: first `accepted`, the control that the producer's own envelope is accepted and stored; then every row `rows()` returns, each refused with nothing stored (SG1's foreign account, header and query string, SG4, SG5, SG6's capability and subject kind outside the schema, SG7 and SG9); then `conflicting_duplicate`, which is SG8. SG2 and SG3 need a live producer token and a live agent token, which the module does not mint, so only the kernel runs them, in `tests/test_signal_conformance.py` against sources and credentials registered for real; SG5's admitted-record check, SG6's registration check and SG10 to SG17 are the kernel's own tests. Three cases are narrower than a reader might expect. A record from a source whose principal scope does not hold the agent does not apply (SG12) rather than being refused at the intake, because a resource-subject envelope names no principal and the scope is checked at resolution. A record after dispatch is not recorded as Monitor evidence (SG16), because Monitor does not surface signal records. And the run-subject rows (a signal for run A offered to run B, a run of another principal, an expired run) arrive with the first run-subject capability, as T13.11's run correlation does; signals have resource subjects only. Real PostgreSQL, the protected endpoint mock as the live provider, and HTTP through the application. Defender for Endpoint's producer posted to this intake in the [30 September 2026 live trial](trials/2026-09-30-mde-signal.md).

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SG1 | An envelope naming an account this tenant does not have; another tenant or principal named in an `x-tenant-id`, `x-principal-id` or `x-role` header or in a query string | 403 `INVALID_SUBJECT` for the account, recorded against the source; 400 with the intake's own answer, before the envelope is read, for a header or a query string; nothing stored in either tenant | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_another_tenants_account_is_an_invalid_subject`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_a_request_shaped_outside_the_bounds_is_refused_and_stores_nothing` |
| SG2 | An unknown, malformed, forged or wrong-kind token: an unknown source or tenant, a wrong secret, a revoked, expired or inactive source, another credential kind's prefix, another scheme | 401: a bearer token the writer cannot authenticate is `INVALID_SOURCE` whatever the reason, and a credential of another shape is refused before the body is read; no record stored, the source's rate and health untouched, and the reason recorded on the `signal` authentication-failure surface | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg2_an_unknown_or_wrong_kind_token_records_nothing`, `::test_sg2_a_token_shaped_right_but_wrong_underneath_records_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_an_unauthenticated_call_stores_and_records_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_every_credential_refusal_answers_the_same_and_records_its_reason`, `::test_a_credential_of_another_shape_is_refused_before_the_body_is_read` |
| SG3 | An agent credential at `/v1/signals`; a producer token at every other route that takes a credential | Refused both ways: 401 at the intake with nothing stored, and 400, 401 or 403 everywhere else | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg3_credentials_are_refused_both_ways`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_a_producer_token_opens_no_other_route`, `tests/test_route_authority.py::test_the_signal_intake_refuses_an_agent_credential`, `::test_a_producer_token_reaches_nothing_but_the_signal_intake` |
| SG4 | A body over 8 KiB, by raw bytes or by multi-byte content; a repeated key; a compressed body; a float or an exponent where the schema requires an integer | 400; nothing stored; an envelope of exactly 8,192 bytes is read | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_a_body_outside_the_envelope_is_refused`, `::test_an_envelope_of_exactly_the_bound_is_read_and_one_byte_more_is_not` |
| SG5 | An unknown schema version or field, including a field for a prompt; secret-shaped text; a right-to-left override or another invisible character; a verdict with edge whitespace; an account id in another UUID spelling; a request reference, which `resource_compromise.v1` does not take because the agent chooses its own reference; an admitted record's severity, attributes and evidence reference and digest | 400 `INVALID_ENVELOPE`; nothing stored; secret-shaped text never quoted back or logged; an admitted record's severity, attributes and evidence stay in `security_signals`, out of every plan, audit payload and agent answer and out of the approver's views, the console's and the classic approval page, and the administrator's, and its severity changes no decision | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_envelope.py::test_anything_else_is_refused`, `::test_invisible_and_control_characters_are_refused_in_free_text`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_secret_shaped_text_is_refused_and_never_quoted`, `::test_a_request_reference_is_refused_before_the_token_or_the_writer`, `tests/test_signal_views.py::test_severity_attributes_and_evidence_never_leave_the_record`, `::test_the_classic_page_names_the_label_and_the_capability_and_nothing_of_the_record` |
| SG6 | A capability or subject kind the source is not registered for | Outside the envelope's schema: 400 `INVALID_ENVELOPE`, nothing stored. Inside the schema but outside the registration: refused by `signal_admit` as `CAPABILITY_REFUSED` or `SUBJECT_REFUSED` and recorded against the source; no envelope reaches that today, because the schema and every registration admit one capability and one subject kind | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_a_field_outside_the_registration_is_refused_and_recorded` |
| SG7 | A producer version outside the registered range, or another producer name | 403 `INCOMPATIBLE_PRODUCER`, recorded against the source; versions compare as numbers | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_a_field_outside_the_registration_is_refused_and_recorded`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_the_writer_s_refusals_answer_their_codes_and_are_recorded` |
| SG8 | The same event id with different content | 409 `CONFLICTING_DUPLICATE`; the first kept and the conflict counted against the source; the same content again answers its first receipt | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg8_a_conflicting_duplicate_keeps_the_first`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_a_retry_answers_its_receipt_and_a_conflict_keeps_the_first`, `tests/test_signal_intake.py::test_accepted_then_a_retry_then_a_conflict` |
| SG9 | An observation more than 60 seconds ahead of receipt, or 3,600 seconds old or more on arrival; a time written as an epoch number, without an offset or with a seventh fractional digit | 400 `FUTURE_OBSERVATION`, `STALE_ON_ARRIVAL`; 400 `INVALID_ENVELOPE` for a malformed time | `tests/test_signal_conformance.py::test_sg1_to_sg9_a_hostile_or_broken_envelope_is_refused_and_stores_nothing`, `tests/test_signal_admit.py::test_time_bounds`, `tests/test_signal_envelope.py::test_anything_but_an_rfc_3339_instant_is_refused_in_either_field` |
| SG10 | A required signal missing, stale, expired by its producer, unmapped, from an inactive or revoked source, with no mapping entry, from one admitted source of two, or newest in a record observed or received after the decision | `INVALID_CONTEXT`: refused and audited, the agent answered 503, and nothing recorded or dispatched | `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_every_way_the_fact_is_not_there_is_invalid_context`, `::test_every_admitted_source_must_speak`, `::test_a_record_observed_after_the_decision_refuses`, `::test_a_record_received_after_the_decision_refuses`, `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_no_signal_is_refused_audited_and_nothing_runs` |
| SG11 | An approval over a signal whose source is revoked, or whose record goes stale, before the decision; an approved request whose source is revoked before it runs | `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`; nothing dispatched | `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_an_approval_cannot_rescue_a_revoked_source`, `::test_a_decision_after_the_signal_goes_stale_is_refused`, `::test_an_approved_request_cannot_run_on_a_revoked_source` |
| SG12 | A record about another resource, account or resource type; from a source scoped to another agent, or to a group the agent is not in; from another space's source that is not offered; scoped to a request reference, including one the request names | Does not apply, and `INVALID_CONTEXT` when required; the same source applies once offered; a request-scoped record never applies, and the source's newest unscoped record is read in its place | `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_every_way_the_fact_is_not_there_is_invalid_context`, `::test_the_correlation_is_exact`, `::test_a_source_scoped_to_another_agent_does_not_apply`, `::test_a_group_scoped_source_applies_only_to_its_members`, `::test_a_source_of_another_space_does_not_apply_unless_offered`, `::test_the_statement_never_reads_a_source_of_another_space`, `::test_a_request_scoped_record_never_applies_even_to_the_request_it_names`, `::test_a_request_scoped_record_alone_is_no_record` |
| SG13 | A `required` mapping admitting an `agent_forwarded` source | Refused at validation at the source's location, by the check activation also runs; resolution refuses it too | `tests/test_signal_bundle_validation.py::test_an_agent_forwarded_source_is_refused_in_a_required_mapping`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_an_agent_forwarded_source_is_refused_in_a_required_mapping` |
| SG14 | A mapping producing `clean` for a source not attesting liveness; a capability with no `no_detection` value over such a source; a `clean` re-applied to frozen records from such a source | Refused at the threshold's location, or at the source's; resolution, evaluation and replay refuse it through `signals/mapping.combine` | `tests/test_signal_bundle_validation.py::test_clean_needs_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `::test_a_capability_without_no_detection_needs_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `tests/test_signal_resolution.py::test_clean_needs_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `tests/test_signal_mapping.py::test_clean_needs_every_record_from_a_source_that_attests_liveness`, `tests/test_signal_evaluation.py::test_a_forged_clean_over_a_source_without_liveness_is_refused`, `tests/test_signal_replay.py::test_a_candidate_reading_clean_from_a_source_without_liveness_cannot_be_read` |
| SG15 | `compromised` arriving while a person decides, or a changed value found on the agent's retry; the same verdict re-reported, or a verdict the mapping reads alike | The first two make the approval `STALE_AUTHORIZATION`; the last two keep it, and the approved request runs once | `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_compromised_arriving_while_a_person_decides_cancels_the_approval`, `::test_an_agent_retrying_on_a_changed_value_goes_stale`, `::test_a_heartbeat_of_the_same_verdict_keeps_the_approval`, `::test_a_verdict_the_mapping_reads_alike_keeps_the_approval`, `::test_an_agent_retrying_on_a_heartbeat_stays_pending`, `tests/test_signal_evidence.py::test_a_re_report_of_the_same_verdict_keeps_the_binding_and_changes_the_plan` |
| SG16 | A record after dispatch; a record that changes the value while dispatch commits | The first leaves the decision, the answer to a retry, the impact and the claim unchanged; the second refuses dispatch, and nothing reaches the provider | `tests/test_signal_execution.py::test_a_record_after_dispatch_changes_nothing`, `::test_a_record_arriving_while_dispatch_is_committed_refuses` |
| SG17 | Replay with the producer offline; a candidate reading the frozen records differently; a candidate needing a record the plan never froze, or one the store could never activate over those records; a candidate budget over the signal fact, with earlier units it can and cannot read | Identical decisions; each candidate reading replayed as the live kernel would decide; `UNRESOLVED` with `SIGNAL_EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE` for a record never frozen and for a candidate that could never be activated; the candidate's budget counts earlier units by its own reading, and an earlier unit it cannot read leaves a later decision `UNRESOLVED`, counted only at a lower bound; the most records a fact may carry, at their widest, fit the decision capture replay reads | `tests/test_signal_replay.py::test_the_original_bundle_reproduces_every_decision_with_no_producer`, `::test_a_candidate_reading_is_replayed_and_an_unreadable_one_is_unresolved`, `::test_a_candidate_admitting_an_unfrozen_source_cannot_be_read`, `::test_each_candidate_reading_is_replayed_as_the_live_kernel_would_decide`, `::test_a_candidate_that_could_never_be_activated_over_the_records_is_unreadable`, `::test_a_candidate_budget_counts_earlier_units_by_the_candidates_reading`, `::test_an_earlier_unit_the_candidate_cannot_read_leaves_a_signal_budget_unknown`, `::test_four_records_at_maximum_width_fit_the_decision_capture` |

## Protocol and connector packages (PK1 to PK10)

The provider protocol and the connectors leave the server as two Apache-2.0 packages, `execbound-protocol` and `execbound-connectors`, which import nothing from it.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PK1 | Every module of both packages, and a planted file that imports the server or names a server module to `importlib` | Neither package imports `execbound` or `execbound_harness`, the protocol imports no connector module, and the planted import is found | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_neither_package_imports_the_server`, `::test_the_protocol_imports_neither_the_connectors_nor_the_server`, `::test_the_walk_refuses_a_planted_import`, `::test_the_walk_lets_the_protocol_and_signed_literals_through` |
| PK2 | Every helper, the catalog, the records, the connectors and the signal envelope at their old server paths | Each old name is the very object the package defines: one definition, so no copy can drift from history | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_the_server_names_the_protocols_helpers`, `::test_there_is_one_production_catalog`, `::test_the_server_names_the_protocols_records`, `::test_the_server_names_the_connectors`, `::test_the_signal_envelope_is_the_protocols` |
| PK3 | Each frozen journal of the endpoint and identity contracts, re-signed by the protocol from its own record and key | The frozen bytes back, byte for byte, and the server's `JournalVerifier` settles them | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_a_journal_the_protocol_signs_is_the_frozen_journal_and_the_server_verifies_it` |
| PK4 | An executor outcome signed with the protocol alone, and the same envelope with its record changed | The server attests the first and refuses the second | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_an_outcome_the_protocol_signs_is_one_the_server_attests` |
| PK5 | A dispatch through the gateway | The connector is handed exactly the `ProviderCommand` frozen from the dispatch, and nothing of the dispatch record | `tests/test_protected_path.py::test_a_connector_receives_the_frozen_provider_command` |
| PK6 | A connector that forges a journal under a key of its own and carries a verifier accepting it, on dispatch and on reconciliation | `INVALID_EVIDENCE` from the server's verifier; the dispatch is retained `INDETERMINATE` and the reconciliation is refused | `tests/test_protected_path.py::test_the_verifier_comes_from_the_server_not_the_connector`, `::test_reconciliation_settles_with_the_server_verifier_too` |
| PK7 | A connector that reports a configuration with a key of its choosing after the runtime is built, and one that ignores `rotate_keys` | The runtime's verifier refuses the forged journal, and a verifier handed out before a rotation answers with the rotated ring | `tests/test_protected_path.py::test_the_runtime_verifies_with_the_config_it_was_given_not_what_a_connector_reports`, `::test_a_rotation_reaches_the_runtimes_verifier_whatever_the_connector_does` |
| PK8 | Every call in the server to `dispatch_owned` or `reconcile_operation`, and a planted call naming no verifier | Each passes the runtime's verifier; the planted call is found | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_every_server_caller_settles_with_the_runtimes_verifier` |
| PK9 | The code of both packages, and a planted module naming `TerminalEvidence` | Neither package names the evidence the kernel settles on; the planted name is found | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_neither_package_can_build_the_evidence_the_kernel_settles_on` |
| PK10 | An installed `compose.yaml` that starts `execbound.provider.mock_app:create_mock_app` | The old path, as uvicorn resolves it, is the factory the connectors package defines | `tests/test_connector_packages.py::test_the_old_mock_app_path_still_starts` |

## Approval routing (AR1 to AR23)

A held request should never wait for a person who does not exist. An invitation can put an Approver straight into the reviewer group a rule routes work to, checked when it is created and again when it is accepted, and an invitation that expired stays visible with a way to send it again. Workspace settings states how long a held request can wait for each action the active policy can hold and where approvals are announced, warns when short approvals reach nobody, and the deadline dialog warns when a message can arrive after a short deadline. A held request records how many people could decide it when it was held; a workspace administrator is told on Home and Approvals when a held request has fewer people who can decide it than it needs, and Approval rules shows, for each Space and each action its active policy can hold, who can decide it and which resources nobody, or too few, can. Real PostgreSQL throughout, Vitest for the console clauses and Chromium for the browser ones. Rows are listed as their tests land.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AR1 | Migration `0083_invitation_reviewer_group` from the previous head and back | The column and the function exist and the acceptance is patched; `workspace_invitation_set_group` is a definer the runtime may execute and `PUBLIC` may not; the downgrade refuses while a pending invitation names a group, and otherwise restores `workspace_invitation_accept` byte for byte and drops the column and the function | `tests/test_invitation_reviewer_group_migration.py::test_ar1_the_set_group_function_is_the_runtimes_and_not_publics`, `::test_ar1_the_acceptance_gains_step_8b_exactly_once`, `::test_ar1_downgrade_refuses_a_pending_invitation_with_a_group`, `::test_ar1_downgrade_restores_the_acceptance_byte_for_byte` |

A Viewer-only and an Administrator-only invitation with a group.
