## Console foundation (CF1 to CF14)

The console prints every instant one way in the zone the viewer chose, names every agent and person one way on every page, and uses one word for each thing a person decides or sees decided in the approval flow. Numbers are the workspace's own counting numbers (migrations 0058 and 0062): display handles only, read inside the tenant fence, and never part of anything an agent receives.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CF1 | In real Chromium with `timezone_id="America/New_York"`, an approval for a held request | The detail reads the request time with its date and `EDT` or `EST`, never "UTC" beside a local time; the title of that time reads the instant in UTC; the stop notice reads its time the same way |
| CF2 | The same viewer chooses UTC under My account, then opens the approval, Activity, Home and the Live board, then reloads | Every time reads in UTC with "UTC"; the board's day reads "Today (UTC)"; the choice survives the reload; the live board keeps updating after the choice; with storage that refuses, the console shows local times and does not fail |
| CF3 | `pnpm lint` over the source tree, and `console/scripts/bans.test.ts`'s fixtures for each time and name ban and for each exempt file | The tree passes; each forbidden form is a lint error, including the Activity chip's former shape; `src/lib/time.ts` and `src/components/directory/format.ts` pass their own exemptions and nothing else does |
| CF4 | `/board`, `/board/stream` and `/approvals?view=decided` with valid, missing, out-of-range, malformed and repeated `utc_offset` | Valid moves the start of the day; missing keeps UTC midnight; the rest are `INVALID_REQUEST` before any read and before the stream's first byte |
| CF5 | A held request on the board | `expires_at` equals the approval's expiry; the ticker reads "N min left, by HH:MM ZONE" from it |
| CF6 | An unlabelled agent with one held and one executed request, read as an administrator | It reads "Agent 1" in Activity, the drawer, Home, the approval and its queue, the Live board's lane and ticker, Getting started and the palette; its agent page is "Agent 1" |
| CF7 | The same agent labelled "triage-bot", and a second agent also labelled "triage-bot" | Every surface in CF6 reads "triage-bot" with "Agent 1" beside it; the second agent's lane and ticker cell read "triage-bot" with "Agent 2" |
| CF8 | An administrator stops the workspace, changes the mode and the deadline, acknowledges a finding and binds an agent; a person files a data request; an approver reads `/session` during the stop | Each reference names the administrator by kind and number on the POST that made the change and on the next GET, beside the unchanged UUID; the request's requester carries their number; the approver's session carries `stopped_by_ref: null` and its banner reads "activated by an administrator" |
| CF9 | Two workspaces, each with an Agent 1, a User 1 and a held request, read in turn | Each read names only its own principals; `principal_facts` across workspaces returns nothing |
| CF10 | An agent and a person labelled with markup, on the record table, the palette, the approval and the name prompt | The label is the characters it is; no element it describes exists; nothing ran |
| CF11 | The agent OpenAPI document; a held request, its refusal by a person over HTTP and MCP, and a refusal before any plan over MCP, in a workspace with a labelled agent and numbered people | The document is unchanged; no answer carries a number, a kind or a principal's name |
| CF12 | Search as an administrator for `agent 1`, `Agent #1` and an id prefix; as an approver; under a space view that excludes the agent. Search for `user 2`, `User #2`, a person's id prefix and a person's email, as an administrator with `access.manage`, as one without it and as an approver, with the person deactivated and with another workspace holding people of its own | The administrator finds the unlabelled agent each way; the approver finds no agent; the excluded space's agent is not returned. Only the administrator with `access.manage` finds the person, by number and by id prefix, never by email, marked deactivated when they are; the result opens `/people/<id>`; the other workspace's people are never returned |
| CF13 | An unlabelled agent's page and an unlabelled person's page; save a name; save a refused name | The prompt is under the heading; a saved name replaces the prompt and the heading after the server answers; the person's save asks for the step-up when it is due; a refused name shows the existing refusal and nothing changes |
| CF14 | In real Chromium at 1280 and 390 px, in light and dark: the approval detail, Activity with the drawer open, the Live board, Home and My account | Nothing overflows the 16 px gutters or scrolls sideways; axe reports nothing; no policy violation; the approval words read Approve, Refuse, Refused and Lapsed |

Tests live in tests/test_console_foundation.py (CF4, CF8, CF9, CF11, CF12 at the API), tests/test_principal_names.py (CF9's fenced read), tests/test_console_api_board.py (CF5), tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py, tests/test_console_api_agents.py and tests/test_account_api.py (CF8's acknowledgement, binding and queued request), and tests/test_browser_console_foundation.py (CF1, CF2, CF6, CF7, CF12's palette, CF13 for an agent, CF14). Two rows have no pytest node: CF3 is held by `console/scripts/bans.test.ts`, and CF10 by the console's component tests (`console/src/components/PrincipalName.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/ui/CommandPalette.test.tsx`, `console/src/approvals/approvals.test.tsx` and `console/src/components/directory/agents.test.tsx`), beside CF13's person case and its step-up in `console/src/components/directory/people.test.tsx`.

## Approval delivery (AY1 to AY19)

An approval notification is delivered while the approval can still be decided, says what the approver needs, and is followed up with how the approval ended where a destination asks. Each approval is created at a fixed `T` under the default 120-second deadline, so it expires at `T + 120` and the fifteen-second lapse margin closes delivery at `T + 105`. Real PostgreSQL throughout, the clock injected. The reminder rows change the deadline to 600 s on the audited path, with facts valid for most of an hour. The hosted rows AY20 and AY21 are in the hosted inventory.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AY1 | A pending approval whose `expires_at` is 14 s after the drain's clock, with one webhook, one email and one Slack destination | Each row `FAILED` with `LAPSED_BEFORE_DELIVERY` and `attempts` 0, nothing sent through any transport, `closed` counts 3; the approval, the execution and the audit chain unchanged | `tests/test_approval_delivery.py::test_ay1_a_row_inside_the_lapse_margin_is_closed_unsent` |
| AY2 | The same approval drained with 16 s left | Each destination sent once and `DELIVERED` | `tests/test_approval_delivery.py::test_ay2_a_row_outside_the_margin_is_sent` |

## Monitoring queue (MQ1 to MQ16)

Monitoring becomes a queue an administrator works through every day. The group covers acknowledgement held to the spaces the caller sees and audited (MQ1 and MQ2), the findings queue, freshness and cross-links (MQ3 to MQ12), and the acknowledgement note, the write-once rule and bulk acknowledgement (MQ13 to MQ16). Rows are listed as their tests land.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MQ1 | An administrator narrowed to one space, and a workspace-wide one looking at one space, acknowledge a finding of another space, through the store and over HTTP | Refused as `FINDING_UNAVAILABLE`, answered 404 `NOT_FOUND` with a body byte-identical to an unknown identifier's; the row is unchanged and no event is appended; the narrowed administrator still acknowledges a finding of their own space, and the workspace-wide one on All spaces acknowledges the other | `tests/test_monitoring_acknowledgement.py::test_mq1_an_administrator_cannot_acknowledge_a_finding_outside_the_spaces_they_see`, `tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py::test_mq1_acknowledging_outside_the_space_answers_as_unknown` |
| MQ2 | Acknowledge in the browser, through a personal access token, and a second time; then decide and replay | Exactly one `monitor.finding_acknowledged` per finding acknowledged, whose payload is exactly `finding_id`, `rule_version` and `actor_credential_id` (the token's backing credential for the token, the session's own for the browser), with the finding's space on the row and no observation content; the repeat and a refusal append nothing; every later decision replays complete | `tests/test_monitoring_acknowledgement.py::test_mq2_an_acknowledgement_appends_one_event_and_a_repeat_none`, `tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py::test_mq2_a_token_acknowledgement_names_its_credential`, `tests/test_replay_informational_kinds.py::test_an_acknowledged_finding_leaves_every_later_decision_replayable` |
| MQ3 | The sidebar with 3 open findings and 2 quiet items; with none open and 2 quiet; with neither; an approver. The Findings and Connections tabs with and without counts. Home with 1 stale source and 1 overdue collection, with only the overdue one, and with neither | The Monitoring entry links to `/monitor?view=findings`, `?focus=quiet` and `/monitor` in turn; its pill shows 3, 2 and nothing, and its accessible name says what it counts; the entry is current on every `/monitor` view; an approver sees no entry and no tenant read is made. The tabs read "Findings, 3 open" and "Connections, 2 quiet", and are bare at zero. Home's row reads "2 monitoring sources or collections quiet" and links to `/monitor?focus=quiet`, still shows for an overdue collection alone, and is absent with neither | Vitest: `console/src/shell/shell.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/monitor/badge.test.ts`, `console/src/components/monitor/quiet.test.ts`, `console/src/components/monitor/MonitorPage.test.tsx`, `console/src/routes/-home.test.tsx` |
| MQ4 | 250 findings, 230 acknowledged, then ten open ones moved to another space; `GET /monitor/findings` with each state, no state, a bad state and an extra query key; an approver and an agent credential | Each state answers its findings newest first, at most 200; the open and acknowledged counts are right beyond 200 and per space; the snapshot's `open_findings` equals the open count; no state is `open`; a bad state is 400 `INVALID_REQUEST`; an extra key is refused; an approver is refused 403 and an agent credential 403 and audited. In the console the view reads only this route, sets no timer, asks again when the window regains focus, and filters with chips that keep the focus | `tests/test_monitoring_acknowledgement.py::test_mq4_findings_read_by_state_counts_beyond_the_page`, `tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py::test_mq4_findings_answer_by_state_and_refuse_anything_else`, `tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py::test_the_monitor_routes_are_an_administrators`, `tests/test_console_api_monitoring.py::test_every_monitor_route_refuses_a_bearer_credential_and_a_missing_session`; Vitest: `console/src/components/monitor/Findings.test.tsx` |

## Policy lifecycle (PL1 to PL10)

The policy pages put the preview before activation, let a Policy manager preview over the Spaces they manage, record why each console activation was made, compare a draft with the policy in use, calm the list, and carry rules between workspaces as a rules file. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the pages in Vitest. This is console evidence, not kernel evidence, so the group is not in the release manifest's acceptance prefixes.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PL1 | A validated policy opened; no preview run; a preview run over 7 days with changes; the activation dialog opened before and after; a policy active only in another Space opened | The stepper sits under "What this policy does" in the order Validate, Preview, Activate; nothing replays on arrival; the button reads "Preview the last 7 days" and follows the window chosen; step 3 reads "Not previewed", then the summary with transition counts; the dialog opens with the not-previewed line when none ran and with the summary when one did; the policy active elsewhere offers the preview as its current step, which the earlier page did not | `console/src/components/policy/lifecycle.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/policy/policy.test.tsx` ("the policy words") |
| PL2 | A Policy manager of Space A (a viewer of B), with executions in A and B that the candidate decides differently, previews; an `ADMIN`-kind principal seated as `ADMIN` in A alone previews; a workspace-wide administrator previews the same; a manager's preview that cannot be computed; a manager of an empty Space previews | The manager's and the narrowed administrator's answers count and list only A's executions, with `scope: "spaces"`; the workspace-wide administrator's lists both with `scope: "workspace"` and its counts are what they were; the unavailable preview answers `scope: "spaces"` for the manager and `"workspace"` for the administrator; the empty Space's manager counts nothing | `tests/test_policy_preview_spaces.py::test_a_policy_managers_preview_holds_only_their_spaces_decisions`, `::test_a_narrowed_administrators_preview_holds_only_their_spaces_decisions`, `::test_an_administrators_preview_is_unchanged`, `::test_a_policy_managers_unavailable_preview_says_spaces`, `::test_a_policy_manager_of_no_space_with_executions_sees_nothing_of_theirs`, `tests/test_admin_cli.py::test_bundle_preview_reports_changed_decisions_without_writes` |
| PL3 | A Policy manager calls `export_history` directly; an approver and a viewer call the preview route; a manager of A previews a policy owned by B and not offered | `export_history` refuses the manager as it did before (`ExportError`), and admits them only with `permission="policy.manage"`, which only the preview passes; the approver and the viewer are refused 403 `FORBIDDEN` and nothing is replayed; the other Space's policy answers 404 and nothing is replayed | `tests/test_policy_preview_spaces.py::test_the_replay_export_still_refuses_a_policy_manager`, `::test_a_policy_manager_previews_through_the_console_and_others_are_refused`, `::test_another_spaces_bundle_is_not_found_for_a_manager` |
| PL4 | Activation through the API with no reason, an empty one, a blank one, 501 characters and a secret-shaped one, then a plain one; the store with a blank, long or secret-shaped reason; the CLI with and without `--reason`, and with a blank one | 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming `reason` for each refused body, nothing activated and no event, and the value never echoed; the plain reason is in `policy.bundle_activated`'s payload; the store refuses `INVALID_REASON` at `reason` and moves nothing; without a reason the payload has exactly the keys it had before, byte for byte; the CLI prints `INVALID_REASON` for the blank one | `tests/test_console_api_policy.py::test_activation_requires_a_reason_and_records_it`, `tests/test_policy_store.py::test_an_activation_records_its_reason_in_the_event`, `::test_an_activation_without_a_reason_writes_todays_payload`, `::test_an_activation_reason_shaped_like_a_secret_is_refused_unquoted`, `::test_a_blank_or_long_activation_reason_is_refused`, `tests/test_admin_cli.py::test_bundle_lifecycle_through_the_cli` |
| PL5 | Activations of one policy with two reasons and of another between them; a policy offered tenant-wide and activated in Space B with a reason, read by an administrator and by a Policy manager of Space A; the partial index | The list's row and the detail carry the latest activation's note, its time, actor and number; a never-activated draft carries none; the manager never reads B's note, while the administrator does; `audit_events_policy_activation` exists, partial on `policy.bundle_activated` | `tests/test_console_api_policy.py::test_the_list_and_the_detail_carry_the_latest_activation_note`, `::test_a_note_from_a_space_the_reader_cannot_see_is_not_shown`, `::test_the_activation_notes_index_exists` |
| PL6 | A candidate that adds an allow, removes a deny, raises a ceiling, changes a condition and changes only a note, compared with the active policy; candidates that only map a second connector account for an existing action, only declare `external_executor`, only turn `observe_only` off, only change a mapping version, only raise a context setting's `max_age_seconds`, or only add a signal mapping; a Cedar candidate; no active policy; the active policy itself; a Space not chosen | Each change carries its direction as `policy/compare.py` classifies it (an added allow, a removed deny, a raised ceiling: allows more; a changed condition: check; a note alone: none), and none of the mapping-only candidates reads as no change; Cedar is `why_not: "cedar"`, no active is `"no-active"`, the active policy is `"is-active"`, all Spaces is `"no-space"`; another Space's policy that is not offered answers 404; nothing is replayed; the card leads each change with its word and renders rule text as text | `tests/test_policy_compare.py`, `tests/test_console_api_policy.py::test_the_comparison_names_each_change_against_this_spaces_active_policy`, `::test_a_draft_that_only_adds_an_account_mapping_reads_as_allowing_more`, `::test_the_comparison_says_why_it_cannot_compare`, `::test_a_recognized_seed_compares_through_its_reviewed_rules`, `::test_another_spaces_bundle_is_not_compared`, `console/src/components/policy/lifecycle.test.tsx` ("changes from the active policy") |
| PL7 | A Space with one active, two drafts, one validated, one active in another Space and twelve retired versions | Active first, then drafts and validated newest first, then the one active elsewhere; "Show 12 retired versions" reveals a second table and sets `?retired=1`; Back hides it; a version's note shows under its time as text | `console/src/components/policy/policy.test.tsx` ("a calmer list") |
| PL8 | Download from a rules bundle with a tenant budget, a group budget, a selector budget, a note and context; from a Cedar bundle; from a recognized seed; a Policy manager of A downloading B's policy that is not offered | The file parses with `load_ruleset_file` and equals the stored ruleset minus the group budget and the context; it holds no UUID; the selector budget and the note are kept as written; `omitted_budgets` names the group budget and the detail's `rules_file` says so before the click; Cedar and the seed answer 409 `CONFLICT`; the other Space's policy answers 404; the console builds the file from the answer as text and offers no download for Cedar or a seed | `tests/test_console_api_policy_rules_file.py::test_a_download_is_the_ruleset_file_the_cli_reads`, `::test_a_download_carries_no_identifier`, `::test_a_download_keeps_selector_budgets_and_notes_as_written`, `::test_a_bundle_not_written_as_rules_cannot_be_downloaded`, `::test_another_spaces_bundle_is_not_downloaded`, `console/src/components/policy/rulesfile.test.tsx` ("download rules") |
| PL9 | Create from a rules file: the downloaded file in a second Space offered the same account; a file naming an unmapped action; an action served only by a signal mapping; mappings that cannot share a bundle; a qualified budget; context; a secret-shaped note; duplicate keys; not JSON; an array; `version: 2`; 513 KB; 270,000 two-byte characters; a 511 KB file of newlines; a body over the route's cap; the file beside a bundle or a template; a Policy manager in a Space they only view | The first lands as a `DRAFT` owned by the second Space, over the mappings it may use, and is not active, with `policy.bundle_created` and no `policy.bundle_activated`; the others answer 400 at their locations (`INVALID_BODY` at `ruleset.rules.0.action`, `SIGNAL_MAPPING_ONLY` and `MAPPINGS_CONFLICT` at the action, `INVALID_BODY` at `ruleset.budgets.0.group_id`, `ruleset.context`, `ruleset.rules.0.note` and `ruleset.version`, and naming `ruleset` for the unreadable and oversized ones), the newline file passes the size checks, the body over the cap answers 413 `TOO_LARGE`, and the Space the person only views answers 403; each refusal writes nothing; the console refuses a file over 512 KB before reading it, words an action refusal from the person's own file, and keeps nothing in storage | `tests/test_console_api_policy_rules_file.py::test_a_rules_file_becomes_a_draft_over_this_spaces_mappings`, `::test_a_rules_file_is_refused_at_its_location_and_writes_nothing`, `::test_an_action_served_only_by_a_signal_mapping_is_named_as_such`, `::test_two_mappings_that_cannot_share_a_bundle_are_named_at_the_action`, `::test_an_oversized_rules_file_is_refused`, `::test_a_rules_file_is_refused_beside_a_bundle_or_template`, `::test_a_policy_manager_imports_only_into_a_space_they_manage`, `console/src/components/policy/rulesfile.test.tsx` ("a draft from a rules file") |

## Home, value reporting and the board (HV1 to HV17)

Home says what ExecBound did. An approval past its deadline is classified at read time on the server's clock, as lapsed before a decision or approved but not used, and nothing is written until the recovery run's sweep. "Effect unknown" is its own open figure. One classification (`surfaces/outcomes.py`) serves both Home's value card and report and the Activity list each count opens. A denial links to the rule that decided it where the reader may open that policy. A person may turn on a plain-text weekly summary of counts, and stop it with one click from the email.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HV1 | A pending approval past its deadline inside the window, an approved one past its deadline, and an unswept pending one that lapsed eight days ago, read through the overview before and after the recovery run, on one clock | Before: `pending_approvals` 0, `lapsed_approvals` 2 (the two undecided lapses), equal to the list `outcome=lapsed&state=PENDING_APPROVAL` opens; the two in-window rows under `stale` and bucketed by `expires_at`; the old lapse in no window figure; none in `awaiting`. No read appends an event or changes an approval. Sweeping the two in-window lapses leaves the window's figures as they were; the run then sweeps the old one |
| HV2 | `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN`, `STALE_AUTHORIZATION` and `FAILED_NOT_EXECUTED` rows, and one `FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` row changed ten days before | `unknown` counts both effect-unknown rows, `stale` the other two, `decisions` is their sum, the series holds the windowed rows only, and `?state=FAILED_EFFECT_UNKNOWN` lists both |
| HV3 | The board with one held request inside its deadline, one lapsed and one approved but not used | The first is held for a person; the lapsed one is phase `hollow`, word `lapsed`, `lapsed: true`; the unused one `unused: true`; `held` 1 and `lapsed` 1 for the window and for today |
| HV4 | `GET /executions/{id}` and the list for an undecided lapse and an approved-but-unused approval, before the deadline, after it and after the sweep, and a request inside its deadline | `approval.lapsed` and `approval.unused` flip with the server's clock alone, without a write, and say the same on both sides of the sweep; both false inside the deadline |
| HV5 | One request of every outcome, read by `value.read` | Each count is one, except `approved` 2 (it holds the unused one), `settled` 2 (the reconciled row and the corrected one), `would_refuse` 2 (it holds the by-limit one) and `held` 6; the uncorrected unknown effect is not settled; the top action is the catalog's label; the median is the fixture's; `denial_kind` agrees with `store.denial_category` and with the SQL kind on every payload; `settled` needs a settled state; no request value reaches the assembled text |
| HV6 | For every outcome key, the report's count and `GET /executions?outcome=<key>&from=<since>` | The list holds exactly the counted rows |
| HV7 | The report for an administrator, a legacy approver in scope, an approver with no scope, an administrator seated in a second space, and another workspace's administrator | Each counts only what that reader may read; `refused_before_decision` is a number only for the workspace administrator |
| HV8 | `/report` and `/report.csv`: a bearer, no session, an unknown key, a bad range or offset, a workspace named `=HYPERLINK("x")`, and an account session's CSV with no, another's or its own `generation` | 403 audited, 401, 400, 400; the CSV is an attachment under `no-store`, every cell quoted, the name prefixed with `'` and named once, with a `space` row; 409 `WORKSPACE_CHANGED`, 409, 200 |
| HV9 | `/executions` with an unknown `outcome`, a `from` in the future, 32 days back, without a zone, beside `since`, malformed or empty | 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` each; `outcome=lapsed` and `outcome=approved_unused` list exactly their rows |
| HV10 | A denial under a ruleset bundle, a held request's review obligation and its limits, a limit code, and a denial under a Cedar-only bundle; the denial read by an administrator and by an approver who may not open the policy | `bundle_id` is the row's; `rules` names the deny rule, the review rule and the limits; the Cedar-only one has `rules: []` and its recorded sentence; `rule_id` is unchanged; `policy_readable` is true exactly where `GET /policy/{bundle}/rules` answers |
| HV11 | The migrations, the grants and every definer function under the local owner and the `restricted` owner without bypass | Forced row security and the tenant policy on both tables; the runtime role's exact grants; enqueue fenced to its own tenant and refused without one; `tenants`, `due` and `sent` return identifiers or a count; unsubscribe ends only a matching active subscription started no later than its message; the downgrade refuses with a subscription and otherwise drops cleanly; the rejection index exists |
| HV12 | `POST /settings/weekly-summary` on, off and on again; a no-op; no address; a deployment without the summary; the harness refusals; an account in two workspaces whose second principal has no address | Own row only, audited with `via: settings`; 409 `CONFLICT`; 409 with nothing created; 503 `UNAVAILABLE`; the account's address in both workspaces |
| HV13 | A week with activity, a week with none, an approver, subscribers deactivated before and after the enqueue, an addressless one, hostile labels, both caps, a refusing relay, a week past, an account in two workspaces, and the drain under the `restricted` owner; the template alone for a workspace named as written, one named in a non-Latin script, and a tenant id that is not one; the template alone for the longest link it takes with a long workspace name, with and without spaces, and with a line break written into its one-click header; a summary drained for a workspace named `Acme & Co` | One message per subscriber per week; the approver's counts are their scope; the empty week skipped with no SMTP traffic; no message for the first deactivated person and `PRINCIPAL_INACTIVE` for the second; `NO_ADDRESS`; no label, target, person or identifier in subject or body; `List-Unsubscribe` and `List-Unsubscribe-Post`, the first on one unfolded line as the bracketed link with no encoded word, in the template and in what the relay received, while the subject still folds, every other line within 78 columns and every encoded word within 75 characters; the line break refused on the way out; capped messages stay `PENDING`; failures back off and end `FAILED`; `WEEK_PASSED`; one summary per workspace at the account address; both workspaces delivered without bypass; the workspace chosen by name only where it is shown as written, else by the label as a hint beside the tenant id, or by the tenant id alone where nothing of the name survives, and "your workspace", never "unknown", where nothing of the name survives; the bad tenant id refused; the drained summary for `Acme & Co` says to choose the one named like `Acme Co` with that workspace's own tenant id |
| HV14 | The unsubscribe link: a GET; a valid POST; the same again; a forged, unknown, other workspace's, nonexistent workspace's and malformed link; an old link after renewing; the request shape; both limits; every 400 and each limit's 429, asked for as a page and not; the log around a GET, a forged link, a nonexistent workspace's link, a malformed link and a stop; a database fault while stopping, from the audit append and from the definer | The GET changes nothing and sets no cookie; the valid POST ends it with `via: link`; every POST answers the same status and body; the old link leaves the renewal active; `no-referrer` and `no-store`; the request log names no path; 429 past each limit; `no-referrer` on every 400 and 429 too; no ERROR line for the forged, nonexistent or malformed link, no secret, digest or link in any line, and one `weekly_summary.unsubscribed` line with the tenant and message ids alone; the fault answered with the same page, nothing ended, and one `weekly_summary.refused` line at ERROR with its code and no ids, nothing of the link in any line |
| HV15 | `schedule.jobs_for` and the runtime configuration with and without a relay and the flag | `weekly-summary-run` is scheduled, on lock 9006, only with both |
| HV16 | In Chromium: Home with the card, a card number, the findings row, a lapsed record, a denial's rule link, `/report` and its CSV, the board with a lapsed shot, My account's summary card, an agent labelled with markup | In light and dark at 1280 and 390 px: no axe WCAG 2.2 AA violation, no policy or Trusted Types violation, no sideways scroll; the number opens Activity with its outcome pinned and its rows; the findings row opens `/monitor?view=findings`; the rule is marked and in view; the CSV is an attachment; the switch shows "On" only from the answer; the label renders as text |
| HV17 | The card's, the page's, the CSV's and the summary's words | None contains "prevent", "attack", "threat" or "blocked", except the one disclaimer that a refusal is not proof that harm was prevented |

Tests live in tests/test_home_value.py (HV1 to HV7, HV10), tests/test_console_api_report.py (HV8, HV9), tests/test_weekly_summary_schema.py (HV11), tests/test_weekly_summary.py (HV13), tests/test_jobs_schedule.py (HV15), tests/test_console_api_weekly_summary.py (HV12), tests/test_summary_pages.py (HV14) and tests/test_browser_console_home_value.py (HV16); HV17 is held by `console/src/components/home/home.test.tsx`, the CSV and summary tests above.

## Approval pages (AQ1 to AQ13)

The console's approval pages: an administrator sees the approvals waiting on other people and can assign themselves to one in place, the view lives in the address, the decision bar restates what deciding does and where, a typed comment survives signing in again, the page says when the message reached its approvers against the deadline, and a lapsed approval tells an approver what happened. The decision path itself does not change. Real PostgreSQL for the reads, Vitest for the page, Chromium for the flows. AQ10 is in the hosted inventory.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AQ1 | An unassigned administrator, one approval assigned to an approver | Waiting on others lists it with reviewers 1, `reviewers_left` 1 and time left; the detail answers `reviewing` false, no continuation and the same counts; a decision with a borrowed continuation is `INVALID_CONTINUATION`, audited, and changes nothing. `records()` refuses `others` to anyone but an administrator reading outside their own review scope, and it is the complement of their own scope | `tests/test_console_api_approvals.py::test_an_administrator_sees_approvals_waiting_on_others_and_cannot_decide_them`, `::test_records_refuses_others_to_anyone_but_an_administrator_reading_outside_review`, `::test_others_is_the_complement_of_the_administrators_own_scope`, `::test_an_approval_the_administrator_reviews_is_not_waiting_on_others`, `::test_the_reviewer_count_is_people_who_can_open_the_record`, `::test_an_administrators_lapsed_view_covers_every_reviewer`, `tests/test_browser_console_approvals.py::test_an_administrator_chases_an_approval_waiting_on_someone_else` |
| AQ2 | An approver asks for `view=others` | 403 `FORBIDDEN`; their rows and detail carry `null` reviewers, `reviewers_left`, `assigned_to_you` and `your_review_elsewhere` | `tests/test_console_api_approvals.py::test_waiting_on_others_is_an_administrators_view` |
| AQ3 | A space member administrator; approvals in two spaces | Only their space's approval is listed; the other's detail is `NOT_FOUND` | `tests/test_console_api_approvals.py::test_waiting_on_others_stays_inside_the_administrators_spaces` |
| AQ4 | The view chip, Back and a shared `?view=lapsed` link; a non-administrator at `?view=others` | The chip and the list follow the address; the default is stripped; focus stays on the chip; the non-administrator sees Pending and the address loses `view=others` without a new history entry; a row's link carries the view, and Back and a reload return to it | `console/src/approvals/pages.test.tsx` ("the view lives in the address"), `console/src/approvals/search.test.ts`, `tests/test_browser_console_approvals.py::test_an_administrator_chases_an_approval_waiting_on_someone_else` |

## Agents, keys and account security (AK1 to AK12)

An agent key's lifetime is a deliberate choice, a key records when it was last used, a rotation is recorded rather than inferred, and a used key that expires within seven days is announced on the agent's page and on Home. Real PostgreSQL for the functions, authentication and the routes, Vitest for the pages, Chromium for the flow.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AK1 | Every agent key form: the agent page's govern form and API keys card, Connect this agent, the API keys page dialog and the rotate dialog; a personal access token | Each starts at "Choose a lifetime" with the hint, cannot be submitted until a lifetime is chosen, and sends the chosen value; a personal access token still starts at 30 days | `console/src/components/directory/agent-keys.test.tsx` ("choosing a key's lifetime"), `console/src/components/directory/access.test.tsx`, `tests/test_browser_console_agents_account.py::test_ak6_issue_and_rotate_a_key_in_both_schemes` |
| AK2 | A key authenticates three times within a minute, then again after `last_used_at` is backdated 61 seconds; a wrong secret, a revoked, an expired and an inactive principal's key, and an inactive tenant; a key issued 60 s ahead of the database's clock; a stamp that raises; a request through `/v1/execute`; `credential_used` called directly | One stamp, then another; every refusal stamps nothing; the key issued ahead authenticates and stamps nothing; with the stamp raising, authentication still returns the identity, nothing is stamped and the transaction commits; the gateway stamps; the function stamps a live key of the fence's tenant once a minute and raises `INVALID_TENANT` or `INVALID_CLOCK` only for a missing fence or a clock 301 s off | `tests/test_key_last_used.py::test_ak2_authenticate_stamps_after_every_check_and_never_on_a_refusal`, `::test_ak2_a_key_issued_ahead_of_the_database_clock_authenticates_and_stamps_nothing`, `::test_ak2_a_stamp_that_raises_never_changes_authentication`, `::test_ak2_the_gateway_path_is_unchanged`, `::test_ak2_credential_used_stamps_once_a_minute_and_only_a_live_key` |
| AK3 | An open transaction holding the key share lock an execution insert takes; an open transaction holding the key's row `FOR UPDATE`, as a revocation does; two authentications of one key at once | The stamp lands within a second while the share lock is held; with the row locked, authentication returns within a second and stamps nothing; one stamp lands for the two | `tests/test_key_last_used.py::test_ak3_a_share_lock_from_an_open_execution_does_not_delay_the_stamp`, `::test_ak3_a_locked_key_authenticates_at_once_without_a_stamp`, `::test_ak3_two_authentications_at_once_stamp_once`, `::test_ak3_credential_used_skips_a_locked_row` |
| AK4 | The migration downgraded, upgraded and downgraded again; the runtime's catalog check | Upgrade adds both columns, the index and three definer functions owned by the owner whose ACL is exactly the owner's and the runtime's EXECUTE; the runtime keeps no `UPDATE` on `credentials` and no check constraint names `last_used_at`; `check_runtime` accepts the schema; the downgrade removes all of it | `tests/test_key_last_used.py::test_ak4_the_migration_adds_both_columns_and_three_functions_and_the_downgrade_removes_them`, `::test_ak4_check_runtime_accepts_the_migrated_schema` |
| AK5 | A used key expiring in 3 days; the same agent's second used key and a third issued without Rotate; then a key issued with `replaces` naming the first; keys never used, expiring in 8 days, never expiring, of an asleep or deactivated agent, revoked; seven qualifying agents; a narrowed administrator; an approver; a token; `admin_credential_replaces` and the issue route with another agent's key, a revoked, deleted or expired key, the new key itself and a key already named | `GET /credentials/expiring` lists the first key alone while the others exist and no row reads replaced; after Rotate it is replaced and gone; the others never notify; five agents and `more` 2, each by its soonest key, the same key the agent detail's `expiring_key` names; another space's key is absent for the narrowed administrator; an approver is refused 403 and a token may read; every invalid replacement raises `INVALID_REPLACEMENT`, answers 409 and issues nothing; Home lists at most five and links the rest | `tests/test_console_api_expiring_keys.py`, `tests/test_key_last_used.py::test_ak5_admin_credential_replaces_records_a_rotation_and_refuses_the_rest`, `tests/test_console_api_agents.py::test_ak5_ak6_an_issue_with_replaces_records_the_rotation_and_refuses_the_rest`, `console/src/components/home/AccountNotices.test.tsx` |
| AK6 | Rotate on a live key, from the agent page | A new key for the same agent naming the old key, behind the same step-up, shown once with the sentence saying where it goes; `credential.issued` carries `replaces` only then; the old key stays live and reads "live, replaced by a newer key"; the secret is in no query or mutation record, URL or storage once the dialog closes; a refusal shows no secret | `tests/test_browser_console_agents_account.py::test_ak6_issue_and_rotate_a_key_in_both_schemes`, `console/src/components/directory/agent-keys.test.tsx` ("rotating a key"), `tests/test_console_api_agents.py::test_ak5_ak6_an_issue_with_replaces_records_the_rotation_and_refuses_the_rest` |
| AK7 | The agent page with `?section=` set to each section, to an unknown value, and to `connection` on a discovered agent; a just-created agent with no section; a governed agent with requests and no section; an agent that makes its first request while Connection is open; a link with `?section=permissions` through a bound agent's monitoring id; a reader who sees an agent without administering it | Each known section renders alone and the strip marks it `aria-current="page"`; unknown and inapplicable values render the default; the just-created agent opens on Connection with the steps and the key form; the agent with requests opens on Overview with its summary and its Connection folds the steps under one line; a first request while watching keeps Connection open; Back restores the previous section; the canonical redirect keeps the section; the non-administering reader is offered no change; every section is axe-clean in both schemes at 1280 and 390 px | `tests/test_browser_console_agents_account.py::test_ak7_the_agent_page_sections_open_and_read_in_both_schemes`, `console/src/components/directory/agent-sections.test.tsx` ("the agent page's sections"), `tests/test_console_api_agents.py::test_an_agent_the_reader_sees_but_does_not_administer_says_so`, `tests/test_browser_console_setup.py::test_the_listening_panel_links_the_first_request` |
| AK8 | A recognised seed bundle, a rules bundle and a custom Cedar bundle on the agent page; recent activity with and without a target; a target label holding markup | Seed and rules bundles show effect badges and sentences with ids linking to the bundle page or the rules form, with `rules_origin` saying which; the seed keeps its exact text under a closed "Exact policy documents"; custom Cedar keeps its summary and `rules_origin` null; each activity row has one link, to its Activity record, and names its target by provider identifier, rendered as text | `tests/test_console_api_agents.py::test_ak8_a_recognised_seed_reads_as_rules_and_keeps_its_exact_text`, `::test_ak8_recent_rows_name_their_target`, `console/src/components/directory/agent-sections.test.tsx` ("readable rules and linked activity") |
| AK9 | `admin_member_factors` called by an administrator, an approver, a deactivated administrator, another tenant's administrator, and with 501 ids; people with a legacy factor and no membership, an account factor through a membership, a stale enabled `account_factors` row beside a disabled `user_factors` row, and none; the function in a database owned by a role without superuser or `BYPASSRLS`; Users and the person page | Only the active administrator of the tenant gets rows, one boolean each; the others raise `INVALID_ACTOR`; 501 raise `INVALID_REQUEST`; an agent or another tenant's id yields no row; the caller's account setting survives; the stale legacy row reads not enrolled; the account factor reads enrolled without bypass; `GET /people` and the detail carry `second_factor`, and Users and the person page say Enrolled or None | `tests/test_key_last_used.py::test_ak9_member_factors_answers_an_administrator_one_boolean_per_person`, `tests/test_member_factors_without_bypass.py::test_ak9_member_factors_reads_account_factors_without_bypass`, `tests/test_console_api_people.py::test_ak9_people_carry_their_second_factor_as_enforcement_reads_it`, `console/src/components/directory/people.test.tsx` ("second-factor status and the history") |
| AK10 | My account for a person with an authenticator, two passkeys and seven recovery codes left; with none; on a deployment without account security; through a personal access token | The view's `security` states each figure and the card offers Manage; with none, Add a second factor; without account security, the not-configured sentence; a token reads the same `security` as its owner's session; no seed, digest or passkey id is in the answer | `tests/test_console_api_settings.py::test_ak10_my_account_states_the_factor_its_passkeys_and_recovery_codes`, `console/src/components/settings/PersonalSettings.test.tsx` ("the second-factor card") |
| AK11 | Home for an administrator without a factor, dismissed, reloaded; an approver; an administrator with a factor; a deployment without account security; storage that throws | The card shows, then never again in that browser; absent for the others; with storage throwing it shows and dismisses for the page's lifetime without an error; Users, the person page, My account and Home are axe-clean in both schemes at 1280 and 390 px | `tests/test_browser_console_agents_account.py::test_ak11_people_settings_and_home_show_second_factor_status`, `console/src/components/home/AccountNotices.test.tsx` ("Home's second-factor card") |
| AK12 | The person page's History for an administrator with decisions and administrative events on a bundle, an agent, a person, a space and a credential; a narrowed administrator with a decision on an execution outside their spaces; a payload value that is not an identifier | Three visible subheadings; each visible decision links to its Activity record by `execution_id`; the hidden one has a null `execution_id` and shows its prefix as text; bundle, agent and person subjects link to their pages by router-built links with the subject's kind and number; space and credential subjects stay text; a value that is not an identifier yields no subject | `tests/test_console_api_people.py::test_ak12_a_decision_carries_its_approval_and_execution`, `::test_ak12_a_decision_outside_a_narrowed_administrators_spaces_has_no_execution`, `::test_ak12_administrative_events_name_their_subjects_by_kind`, `console/src/components/directory/people.test.tsx` ("second-factor status and the history") |

## Working across workspaces (XW1 to XW12)

An account that belongs to several workspaces sees, in its own list of them, what each makes of it and what entering it asks, and a count of its own waiting approvals in the others, never a record; a switch lands on the same page of the other workspace, through the second factor too, and never on a page its new session cannot open; a tab left behind says which workspace it showed and where the browser went, and keeps its section; the workspace menu is a keyboard listbox and the palette offers the same switch; and a request reference names its workspace for a person with several. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the client in Vitest; the pages in Chromium. The hosted review step's rows are in the hosted inventory.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| XW1 | An account with three memberships (ADMIN in A, a viewer in B with an approver grant in one of B's spaces, an approver in C) lists its workspaces from A | Exactly the item fields. Kinds and roles are each workspace's own, and B's roles are `["viewer"]`: the space-scoped grant is not a workspace role. A's item is `current` with no count. A workspace name holding markup is returned as the characters it is | `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw1_each_workspace_answers_its_own_kind_and_workspace_roles` |
| XW2 | In C, approvals assigned to the account's principal directly and through a rule group, plus unassigned (assigned to someone else), expired, decided, other-space and permission-removed ones | `waiting` equals the pending queue's length read by a fresh session switched into C. `next_lapse` is the earliest assigned deadline floored to the minute, and null with nothing waiting | `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw2_the_count_is_the_pending_queue_a_fresh_session_there_reads`, `::test_xw2_a_principal_without_the_decide_permission_has_nothing_waiting`, `::test_xw2_next_lapse_is_floored_and_never_overstates` |
| XW3 | B requires directory sign-in; then C's policy requires a factor for all with the account holding no factor, then for admins with the account a member and then an ADMIN there, then the account enrols a factor, verified on this session longer ago than the step-up window and then within it | `entry` is `directory`, then `enrol` while the account has no factor and `factor` once it has one, and `open` where none applies; with the factor verified within the window, `open` and counted wherever a switch would carry it (SN14), still `directory` where a directory is required. `waiting_state` is `withheld` with `waiting` and `next_lapse` null wherever entry is not `open`, and no count statement runs for that tenant (statement capture). The tenant setting is set back after each workspace's block, and an error inside one is the error raised | `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw3_entry_says_what_entering_asks_and_withholds_the_count`, `::test_xw3_a_factor_turns_every_other_open_workspace_into_factor`, `::test_xw3_a_recent_verification_opens_what_a_switch_would_carry_into`, `::test_xw3_inside_sets_the_tenant_and_then_the_callers_back`, `::test_xw3_an_error_inside_a_workspace_is_the_error_raised` |
| XW4 | The list answered over HTTP with assignments waiting in another workspace | The body holds none of that workspace's approval ids, execution ids, request numbers, target ids or names, agent ids or labels, or space names. A removed membership and an inactive tenant are absent. The workspace cookie alone is 401. A personal access token is refused. Every statement naming a membership's tenant ran under that tenant's setting, and every read of the setting before a workspace is entered saw the caller's (a statement listener records `current_setting('execbound.tenant_id')` beside each statement) | `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw4_nothing_of_the_other_workspace_crosses`, `::test_xw4_a_removed_membership_and_an_inactive_tenant_are_absent`, `::test_xw4_a_personal_access_token_is_refused_the_list` |
| XW5 | 30 memberships in 30 distinct tenants, and 120 assigned approvals in one | Memberships after the 25th answer `not_counted`. `waiting` is 100 for the one with 120 | `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw5_counting_stops_at_the_caps` |
| XW6 | `selectWorkspace` with each section into an ADMIN destination, a viewer destination and an approver destination holding `approvals.decide`, with none, and with `/ask`, `/policy/{id}`, `/activity?open=x`, `https://example.test/`, `//x`, an empty string and an extra field | Each section into ADMIN is answered as itself. For the viewer, sections it cannot open answer `/`, never `/approvals`. For the approver, sections it cannot open answer `/approvals`. With none, the session's start: `/` for the viewer and the administrator, `/approvals` for the approver. Every malformed value is 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming `section`, the extra field 400 `INVALID_BODY` naming that field, and nothing switches. Over every section, kind and permission set the answer is in the closed set and its rule holds; the server's roots and rules equal the console navigation's | `tests/test_workspace_sections.py::test_xw6_place_answers_the_section_exactly_when_its_rule_holds`, `::test_xw6_a_viewer_starts_at_home_and_an_approver_on_approvals`, `::test_xw6_place_refuses_to_answer_a_section_it_cannot_open`, `::test_xw6_sections_and_rules_mirror_the_console_navigation`, `::test_xw6_a_switch_answers_the_section_its_session_may_open`, `::test_xw6_a_malformed_section_is_refused_and_nothing_switches`, `::test_xw6_an_extra_field_is_refused_and_nothing_switches` |
| XW7 | A switch into a workspace that asks for a factor, with `section="/activity"`; then the code; then a tampered, an expired and a duplicated return cookie; then `GET /login?review=/agents` | The answer is `/mfa` with one signed cookie naming `/activity`, and after the code the person lands on `/activity`. A tampered or expired cookie lands where the session starts; a duplicated one is refused at the request boundary. `remember_section` still admits a section root alone. Since a sign-in link may name any core console page (SN1 to SN5), `GET /login?review=/agents` answers 200 with a hint naming `/agents`, and `login_location("/activity")` is `/login?review=%2Factivity` | `tests/test_workspace_sections.py::test_xw7_the_section_survives_the_second_factor`, `::test_xw7_a_bad_return_cookie_lands_where_the_session_starts`, `::test_xw7_a_sign_in_link_may_name_a_section_like_any_console_page`, `::test_xw7_only_the_section_writer_is_held_to_sections` |
| XW8 | Two tabs in Chromium on A at `/policy/{id}` and `/board`; the `/board` tab switches to B; then a sign-in again into A from a third tab | The left tab reads "This tab was showing A. This browser is now in B." and its link is `/policy`, never the id. After the sign-in again into A, which the server confirms, it reads "You signed in again" and its link reloads its own path; the tab that switched now names B and A. A stale request is still 409 `WORKSPACE_CHANGED`. Each page passes the audit at 1280 and 390 px in both schemes | `tests/test_browser_workspaces.py::test_xw8_a_tab_left_behind_names_both_workspaces_and_keeps_its_section`, `console/src/lib/workspace-context.test.ts`, `console/src/lib/SessionProvider.workspace.test.tsx`, `console/src/lib/sections.test.ts`, `console/src/lib/client-config.test.ts` |

Arrows, Enter, Escape (focus back on the trigger) and an outside press behave as designed. The order is current, then the workspaces the account entered most recently (SN10), then this browser's recent, then the rest, or without the local store when storage throws. Signing out and "Clear recent" clear the local store. Options are disabled while a switch is pending. A refusal shows the client's own sentence | `tests/test_browser_workspaces.py::test_xw9_the_menu_by_keyboard_and_pointer`, `console/src/shell/WorkspaceSwitcher.test.tsx`, `console/src/lib/workspaces.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/ui/palette-commands.test.tsx` |

## Sign-in, sessions and returning (SN1 to SN19, SN22)

A person whose session ended, or who opens a link while signed out, signs in and is back on the page they were on, with its filters, through the second factor too; the signed hint that carries the place names only core's console pages in a closed grammar, and a hint it cannot use is ignored and clears an earlier one. The session view names the hard end no renewal passes and why, and the console warns two minutes before it, saying what is typed is kept only where it is. A new policy document is kept in the tab across a sign-in, for the same person, and only four files may import the draft store. A record and an agent have Copy link, and a record Copy reference. A switch within thirty minutes of a code carries that verification into the workspace it opens, for the same factor, and records where it came from for the account alone. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the client in Vitest; the pages in Chromium. Rows are listed as their tests land.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SN1 | Every entry of `core_console_routes` with fresh UUIDs, with and without approval links loaded, and both classic suffixes; then `https://evil.example/`, `//evil.example`, `/approvals/../admin`, `/%2Fevil`, `/a\b`, a fragment, a space, a control character, an uppercase UUID, a non-UUID parameter, a trailing slash, a 513-character input, a path not in the tree, a plugin console path, a value that is not a string, and the scoped approval with no approval links loaded; then the character checks alone, with every shape admitted | Each core shape is its own canonical form; the scoped shape only where approval links are loaded; every other input is `None`. With the shapes replaced by one that admits anything, a second origin, a backslash, an encoded slash or backslash, a fragment, whitespace and a control character are still refused | `tests/test_login_return.py::test_sn1_the_grammar_is_core_console_routes`, `::test_sn1_the_character_checks_stand_without_the_shapes` |
| SN2 | `/activity` with `open`, `state`, `since`, `agent`, `target`, `action`, `outcome`, `cursor`, `q` and `from`; unknown values; a duplicated key; seventeen fields; `/approvals?view=others`; `/monitor?view=findings&state=acknowledged&open=<id>&focus=quiet&story=a:b` and its `setup` and `connect`; `/welcome?step=connect`; `/?range=7d&open=<id>`; a free-text value on each page; a key on a page that keeps none; and the console's own enumerations | Allowed keys with valid values are kept in sorted order; `cursor`, `q`, `from`, `story`, `setup`, `connect`, invalid values and unknown keys are dropped; a duplicate or a seventeenth field refuses the target; the longest admitted target is at most 256 characters and holds no `:`. `HOME_RANGES`, `APPROVAL_VIEWS` (the array and the union), `MONITOR_SEARCH_VIEWS`, `FINDING_STATES`, the `focus` value and `WELCOME_STEPS` equal the console's constants, and Activity's states and windows equal the server's | `tests/test_login_return.py::test_sn2_only_known_keys_with_known_values_survive`, `::test_sn2_the_enumerations_match_the_console` |
| SN3 | A hint for `/activity?open=<id>&state=DENIED` written with extra keys out of order; then tampered, expired, duplicated, under an unknown key id and over 400 characters; a cookie signed as before this change for an approval path; a correctly signed value that is not its own canonical form; and the sign-in address for Home, an unusable address, a record and a Home range | It is stored canonical and read back; each damaged one falls back to the landing; the old cookie still returns its approval; the non-canonical value never returns. Home's bare address and an unusable one sign in plainly, the others carry the hint | `tests/test_login_return.py::test_sn3_a_hint_is_signed_short_lived_and_single`, `::test_sn3_sign_in_carries_a_page_but_never_home_bare`, `tests/test_sign_in_surface.py::test_an_invalid_navigation_cookie_never_controls_the_login_destination` |
| SN4 | Signed out, open `/activity?open=<id>&state=DENIED&cursor=x`; sign in by password; repeat through the Google stand-in | 303 to `/login?review=` the canonical target without `cursor`; both sign-ins land on `/activity?open=<id>&state=DENIED` and delete the hint | `tests/test_sign_in_surface.py::test_sn4_password_and_provider_sign_in_return_to_any_console_page` |
| SN5 | `GET /login?review=` with each refused input from SN1; then the same with a valid `review_return` cookie already set | 200, the ordinary sign-in page, no new `review_return` cookie; the earlier cookie is deleted and the next sign-in lands on the landing page | `tests/test_sign_in_surface.py::test_sn5_an_unusable_return_is_ignored_not_refused`, `::test_sn5_an_unusable_return_deletes_an_earlier_one` |
| SN6 | A session pending its factor opens `/policy/<id>/rules`; verifies a recovery code | 303 to `/mfa` with a hint naming the page; after the code, the page continues to `/policy/<id>/rules` | `tests/test_mfa_surface.py::test_sn6_the_second_step_returns_to_any_console_page` |
| SN7 | A setup-credential session read every 60 s for 20 minutes on the injected clock, then a page load past the cadence; then left twelve hours and a minute | Signed in throughout, with new session and CSRF cookies of twelve hours at the reads seven and a half minutes after each extension (the eighth and sixteenth minutes) and on the page load; ended (401 `SESSION_REQUIRED`) after the idle gap | `tests/test_session_end.py::test_sn7_a_setup_credential_session_renews_while_it_is_read` |
| SN8 | The session for a password account sign-in, the session a switch opens from it, a password sign-in through a reset code, a directory session, and a setup-credential session whose credential ends an hour after the idle limit read through the session view; then a renewal | `ends_at` is the sign-in credential's expiry, a moment short of two days after sign-in, with `sign_in`, for the account, password and directory sessions; the switched-to session ends at the account sign-in's two days with `sign_in`; the setup credential's expiry with `setup_credential`; a renewal moves `expires_at` and never `ends_at` or `end_reason` | `tests/test_session_end.py::test_sn8_the_view_names_the_hard_end_and_why`, `::test_sn8_a_directory_session_ends_two_days_after_sign_in` |

The sign-in stamps the membership it opens and leaves the other unstamped, and the switch stamps the destination's, each read back through that owner | `tests/test_workspace_entry_migration.py::test_sn9_only_the_fragments_change_and_the_downgrade_restores_them`, `::test_sn9_an_owner_without_bypass_stamps_the_entry` |

Each listed workspace carries its own membership's `last_entered_at` (A after B, a workspace never entered null), and the menu orders the current one first, then by when the account entered them, then this browser's recent ones, then the server's order | `tests/test_workspace_switching.py::test_sn10_sign_in_opens_the_last_workspace_used`, `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_sn10_the_workspace_list_carries_the_last_entry`, `console/src/lib/workspaces.test.tsx` |
| SN11 | With approval links loaded, an account in A (entered last) and B: sign in by password and through the Google stand-in from `/login?review=` naming an approval in B; then a hint naming a workspace it does not belong to, its membership in B made inactive, and B requiring its own directory; then a session in A that opens B's address | The hinted sign-ins open B, and an unhinted one after them opens B as the workspace entered last; the other three open A; the session in A gets the page and keeps A, with no new session cookie | `tests/test_sign_in_surface.py::test_sn11_a_sign_in_opens_the_workspace_its_link_names` |

Wait for the next code from your authenticator app, then enter it." | `tests/test_mfa_sessions.py::test_sn12_a_code_just_used_is_not_a_failure`, `::test_totp_is_single_use_across_concurrent_sessions`, `tests/test_mfa_surface.py::test_sn12_the_page_says_a_code_was_just_used` |

| SN14 | An account with an authenticator verified in A, the verification moved back through the admin engine (the clock stays inside the switch's five-minute fence) to 10 minutes, then switched to B; B's carried verification moved to 29 and 31 minutes; a verification at 31 minutes before a switch; one of a factor since replaced, called on `account_switch` directly because the session check refuses that session first; a factor removed at the same version; B asking every member for a factor of an account with none; the function's carry window; the workspace list at 10 and 31 minutes and with the factor removed; `workspaces.carries` asked directly before and after the factor is replaced or removed | At 10 minutes the switch asks nothing and B's session holds the source's own version, method and verification time; B's chain holds `account_security.assurance_carried` with exactly `session_id`, `account_session_id`, `method` and `verified_at`, and no identifier of A; the account's `assurance_carried` record names A, its session, B and the new session, and the security bell never lists it. B's step-up passes at 29 minutes and asks at 31. The older, replaced and removed factors, and the account with none, carry nothing and are asked (the last to set one up). The carry's interval equals `mfa.STEP_UP_WINDOW`. The list answers `open` with a count at 10 minutes, and the switch asks nothing; at 31 it answers `factor`, withheld, and the switch asks. With the factor removed the list answers `enrol`, withheld, and no count statement runs for B. `carries` answers true before either change and false after it, and the entry rule never answers `open` on a carry for an account with no enabled factor. The list's `factor` cases hold for a verification older than the window, and a recent one opens what the switch would carry into, never a workspace that requires its own directory | `tests/test_workspace_switching.py::test_sn14_assurance_carries_for_the_same_factor_within_the_window`, `::test_sn14_the_window_never_restarts`, `::test_sn14_an_older_or_changed_factor_still_asks`, `::test_sn14_the_carry_window_is_the_step_up_window`, `::test_sn14_entry_proof_matches_the_carry`, `::test_sn14_carries_answers_for_the_factor_the_account_holds_now`, `tests/test_workspace_waiting.py::test_xw3_entry_says_what_entering_asks_and_withholds_the_count`, `::test_xw3_a_factor_turns_every_other_open_workspace_into_factor`, `::test_xw3_a_recent_verification_opens_what_a_switch_would_carry_into` |
| SN15 | On real connections, a switch from a verified session in A to B, which asks every member for a factor, races the same session removing its factor | Both take the account lock first. The switch always goes through; the removal only when it committed first, since otherwise its session has been switched away. B's session carries a verification exactly when the factor was not removed, and only of the version current after both; `requires_mfa` and the switch's own answer agree that B asks exactly when nothing was carried | `tests/test_workspace_switching.py::test_sn15_a_switch_and_a_factor_change_serialize` |

| SN18 | The notice at three minutes and at two minutes before `ends_at`, with each `end_reason`, with and without a keeping field, with storage refusing, on a device clock five minutes fast, and after a later `ends_at` arrives; Copy link and Copy reference with the clipboard granted and refused, in the drawer and on the agent page | Nothing, then the copy and control for each case, at the server's two minutes ("kept" only with a keeping field and working storage; a setup credential's notice offers Help and no sign-in); gone once the end moves; the router's address on this origin exactly, never the address bar's, then the address as text to copy | `console/src/components/SessionEnding.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/CopyLink.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/directory/agent-keys.test.tsx` |

## Monitoring connect (MC1 to MC23)

An administrator connects Tines and Microsoft Entra from the console, collection and evaluation run on a schedule in every workspace, and a reporting source is added, rotated and revoked from the console, with honest copy wherever a deployment has not configured console connect.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MC1 | The schedule, request and lease columns' migration and the sealed keys' migration, each with its round trip and `check_runtime` | Every new column and check exists and each check refuses its violation (a mode other than `key_file`, a half-set request or lease, an interval outside its kind's range); `INTERRUPTED` is an outcome; the runtime inserts only the columns that existed before (`INSERT_COLUMNS`) and updates none of the new ones, and `check_runtime` refuses a runtime with one insertable column too many, too few, or a table-level grant; the four functions are definers with a fixed search path that the runtime alone may call, and a claim holds a connection one to fifteen minutes; the request and schedule functions admit only an active administrator of an active workspace who administers the connection's space, and refuse an agent, the scheduler principal, a person who is not an administrator, a deactivated administrator, one narrowed to another space or seated only as a reviewer in the connection's space, and another workspace's administrator; the downgrade refuses while a connection records `INTERRUPTED` and otherwise restores every definition. A `sealed` connection holds both its ciphertext (48 to 8,192 bytes) and its collector key's id (`ck1-` and sixteen hex digits), any other neither, and only a Tines connection is sealed; the runtime inserts and updates neither; `admin_create_sealed_connection` applies the store's space, capacity and one-per-platform-per-space rules under its registry lock, `admin_replace_sealed_key` touches only an active sealed connection and `collector_reseal` only one sealed under the key it names and still holding the ciphertext it names; that downgrade refuses while a connection is sealed and otherwise round-trips. The consent migration admits `platform_app` only for Microsoft Entra, with no key fingerprint (every other connection keeps one) and always a consent, a moment and a person together, which no other connection records; `monitor_connect_attempts` forces the tenant fence in its initplan form, the runtime only reads it, each of its checks refuses its violation (the states, a directory exactly from sign-in on, a refusal's code and step, ten minutes, a label or a connection) and its `state` digest is unique across the deployment; `monitor_directory_claims` has no tenant column and the runtime no privilege on it, of any kind or column, which `check_runtime` refuses (`PRIVILEGELESS_TABLES`), and its one writer is a definer trigger `check_runtime` holds by name (`TRIGGER_DEFINERS`), which keeps it equal to the active Entra connections whoever writes them; one active Entra connection per directory across every tenant, the directory's case folded, and an operator registering one another workspace holds is refused `DIRECTORY_UNAVAILABLE`; the upgrade refuses, naming only a count, while two share one, and backfills the claims, run as an owner without `BYPASSRLS`; the attempt's principal and the consenter are guarded against the scheduler principal; `admin_start_connect` refuses a full workspace, an occupied space, a sixth live attempt and a twenty-first in a day, and an update for a connection that is not an active consented one; `admin_record_signin` and `admin_complete_consent` check the attempt's state digest, binding, nonce, expiry and order, the principal still administering its space and the directory claimed nowhere (or the update's own), and `admin_refuse_connect` ends only a live attempt for the holder of its state; that downgrade refuses while a connection is consented or any attempt exists and otherwise restores the fingerprint's `NOT NULL`. The creator migration gives every reporting source the administrator who minted it, null on a source made before it, held to a principal of the source's own tenant, written with the row and never updated by the runtime; its downgrade drops it and keeps every source | `tests/test_monitor_connect_schema.py` |
