| MC21 | `process --all-tenants` over two workspaces, each with a quiet heartbeat source, a reported consequential action and a collected agent's tool run pending, and a third deactivated; the same pass misfenced; another mode beside it | One pass evaluates every pending observation in both into findings, raises each quiet source and derives each observed reach; the deactivated workspace is counted and not visited, and one deactivated after the walk checked it is its own refused line; the exit is 0, no delivery is attempted without `--deliver` (FD13 is the pass with it), no audit event is appended and no scheduler principal is made; under another workspace's fence every step refuses; `--config`, `--deliver-local` or a limit outside 1 to 50 beside `--all-tenants`, and `--deliver` beside `--deliver-local`, is refused before anything runs | `tests/test_monitor_process_all.py` |
| MC22 | Reporting sources where the deployment has not configured console connect | The options say `sources: unconfigured`; minting and rotation answer `CONNECT_UNAVAILABLE` and change nothing; reading a source and revoking one made earlier still work; the console offers no Add a reporting source, no Rotate token and no Report this story, and still offers Revoke, in Chromium in the core build | `tests/test_console_api_monitoring_sources.py` (the `test_mc22_` cases), `console/src/components/monitor/SourceActions.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/monitor/Inventory.test.tsx`, `console/src/components/monitor/Connections.test.tsx` ("reporting sources from the console"), `tests/test_browser_monitoring_connect.py::test_mc22_without_console_connect_nothing_is_minted_and_a_source_is_still_revoked` |
| MC23 | Getting started and Home in an edition whose readers run no operator commands | The start-by-watching line appears only once the deployment says a provider can be connected from the console, linking Tines where it can and Microsoft Entra where only it can; the Discover card's link follows the same rule and keeps its sentence naming the service operator until then; nothing is read to decide it, and nothing changes, where the edition offers operator commands | `console/src/components/welcome/WatchFirst.test.tsx`, and the hosted edition's own, in its build in Chromium |

Tests live in tests/test_monitor_connect_schema.py (MC1), tests/test_monitor_lease.py (MC5, MC6), tests/test_monitor_collect_due.py (MC7), tests/test_monitor_consent.py (MC8 to MC11), tests/test_console_api_monitoring_sources.py (MC13 to MC15, MC22), tests/test_monitor_process_all.py (MC21), tests/test_browser_monitoring_connect.py (MC16 to MC19 and MC22 in the core build) and in the other tests named in the rows; the hosted build's halves of MC18 and MC23 are the hosted service's own tests.

## Microsoft sign-in (MI1 to MI6)

A deployment may offer Microsoft work accounts beside Google and GitHub, through the multi-tenant `organizations` authority. The ID token is verified here against Microsoft's pinned key set, the issuer must be the token's own tenant's, personal accounts are refused, the subject is the tenant and object identifiers together, and the address is believed only when Microsoft says the account's tenant verified it. The hosted rows (MI7 to MI9) are documented with the hosted service. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the page in Chromium.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MI1 | A valid Microsoft ID token from the key-set stand-in; then each defect: `alg` `none`, `HS256`, `RS512` and `ES256`, an unknown `kid` after one fetch, a bad signature, `ver` `1.0` or absent, `tid` missing, uppercase, not a UUID or the personal-account tenant, `iss` of another tenant, a placeholder or the v1 form, `aud` another client or a list, `azp`, `exp` past or not a number, `iat` skew, `nbf` an hour ahead or not a number, `nonce` wrong, absent or a lone surrogate, `oid` missing, uppercase or not a UUID, `xms_edov` absent, `false`, `"true"` or `1`, `email` absent, empty, 255 characters or a list | The valid token yields subject `<tid>:<oid>` and the address, never `sub` or `preferred_username`; each defect its own code, before any address is read; a bad signature is refused before any claim, and another algorithm before any key is fetched | `tests/test_microsoft_sign_in.py::test_mi1_the_token_is_verified_claim_by_claim`, `::test_mi1_each_wrong_claim_is_its_own_refusal`, `::test_mi1_an_address_of_the_maximum_length_is_read`, `::test_mi1_a_not_before_within_the_skew_is_accepted`, `::test_mi1_another_algorithm_is_refused_before_any_key_is_fetched`, `::test_mi1_a_bad_signature_is_refused_before_any_claim_is_read`, `::test_mi1_an_unknown_key_refetches_once_and_then_refuses`, `::test_mi1_a_token_that_is_not_one_is_refused` |
| MI2 | Configuration with and without `sign_in.microsoft`, alone and beside Google and GitHub; a redirect URI on Google's callback, on another origin or without `/callback`; the authorization address `begin` builds and the exchange `complete` makes | The provider exists only with its block and comes after Google and GitHub; each wrong URI refuses start; the address carries the `organizations` authority, `openid email`, S256, state, nonce and `select_account` and nothing else; the exchange posts the verifier, the secret and the scope to the pinned token endpoint and keeps only the ID token, held to the flow's nonce; without the block `/login/microsoft` answers 404 and `form-action` does not name `login.microsoftonline.com`, with it it does | `tests/test_microsoft_sign_in.py::test_mi2_the_provider_is_pinned_and_present_only_when_configured`, `::test_mi2_a_token_from_the_exchange_is_held_to_the_flows_nonce`, `::test_mi2_an_exchange_that_answers_anything_else_is_refused`, `::test_mi2_each_provider_fetches_only_its_own_key_set`, `::test_mi2_the_block_builds_the_provider_after_google_and_github`, `::test_mi2_a_redirect_uri_that_is_not_this_deployments_callback_refuses_start`, `::test_mi2_the_route_and_the_form_action_exist_only_with_the_block` |
| MI3 | Through the provider stand-in: an unbound subject whose address matches an account; a link from Account security; the bound subject from an empty browser; the same object identifier under another tenant; a link from a sign-in older than five minutes, to an address that is not the account's, and from a session that has not verified its enabled factor; an account whose only workspace requires its directory | Refused with nothing created; linked; signs in and records `human_sign_in.microsoft`; refused as a different subject; each link refused until the factor is verified; refused for that workspace | `tests/test_sign_in_surface.py::test_mi3_microsoft_signs_in_and_links_by_subject_only`, `::test_mi3_a_microsoft_link_needs_a_recent_sign_in_the_factor_and_the_same_address`, `::test_mi3_a_workspace_that_requires_its_directory_is_never_opened_by_microsoft` |
| MI4 | `0091_microsoft_sign_in` from its parent and back, with the downgrade run from a fresh load of the module; a downgrade with one Microsoft binding present; both again under an owner with the hosted owner's attributes (`NOBYPASSRLS`) | The five checks and two functions change only by `'microsoft'`, keep their names, owner, `SECURITY DEFINER` and grants, and forced row security is untouched; a `microsoft` binding inserts and an unknown provider does not; the downgrade restores every definition byte for byte; with the row present it refuses `MICROSOFT_ROWS_PRESENT` and leaves the revision and forced row security as they were, under either owner | `tests/test_microsoft_migration.py::test_mi4_only_the_lists_widen_and_the_downgrade_restores_them`, `::test_mi4_a_downgrade_with_microsoft_rows_refuses`, `::test_mi4_an_owner_without_bypass_widens_restores_and_refuses` |
| MI5 | An owner links Microsoft and signs in with it, then the tenant's history is exported and verified | `human_sign_in.microsoft` is written with the subject withheld as every provider's is, and is informational; the export verifies with no unknown semantics | `tests/test_microsoft_sign_in.py::test_mi5_a_microsoft_sign_in_replays` |
| MI6 | `/login` with Google, GitHub and Microsoft configured in Chromium, light and dark, 1280 and 390 px | Three buttons in that order, each with its own decorative mark and at least 44 px tall inside the viewport; axe-core and the page's own checks find nothing; every text meets contrast; no horizontal scroll | `tests/test_browser_accessibility.py::test_mi6_the_login_page_with_microsoft_passes_the_audit` |

## Replay regression fixtures (RF1 to RF12)

`replay-fixture` turns an export, its head and up to eight candidate bundles into one versioned, sanitized file, and `replay-fixture-check` replays it offline and fails on any change of a classified decision ([replay](REPLAY.md#regression-fixtures)). RF1 to RF8, and RF10's committed fixtures, run on pure histories and need no database; RF9 builds from the signal-replay world, and RF10's demonstration runs the two-provider validation demonstration, on real PostgreSQL.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RF1 | A pure approved isolation with a stop, a labelled principal and one candidate; the same history without its human review | Each builds and checks clean. The expectations hold every decision's recorded effect, effect, reasons, obligations, outcome, provenance, counts and dependencies, and each candidate's with its differences; event order and times are kept. The incomplete history is recorded with `UNRESOLVED` and its reasons, not refused | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf1_a_fixture_replays_to_its_recorded_outcomes`, `::test_rf1_a_history_with_missing_facts_is_recorded_with_its_reasons` |
| RF2 | One expected effect, reasons, outcome or dependency edited; a decision removed; completeness changed; a baseline and a candidate decision each recorded twice; a candidate's Cedar given a comment without re-recording | The check names exactly the run, sequence and field that differ; a sequence with a decision too many is named as a decision difference, however alike the rows; the edited candidate fails as `candidate 1 candidate_hash` | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf2_one_edited_expectation_fails_and_names_its_sequence`, `::test_rf2_a_lost_decision_and_a_changed_completeness_are_named`, `::test_rf2_a_decision_recorded_twice_is_named`, `::test_rf2_a_candidate_edited_without_re_recording_fails_on_its_content_hash` |
| RF3 | An edited event payload, head, bundle or candidate; an unknown format or version, a duplicate key, a float, an extra key or nine candidates; a fixture edited back to a source identifier, case id, reason, version, note, incident source version or owner-named writer; an export wrapped by hand in the fixture format; a fixture edited to hold a secret-shaped string in a candidate's policy text or in an expectation's reasons | Refused with the verification code (`INVALID_CHAIN`, `HEAD_MISMATCH`, `INVALID_FROZEN_PLAN`, `INVALID_CANDIDATE`), or as unavailable; the edited and the wrapped fixtures refused `FIXTURE_NOT_SANITIZED`, and the secret-holding ones `PRIVATE_DATA` without naming the value, by reading, checking and re-recording alike | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf3_an_edited_history_is_refused`, `::test_rf3_a_fixture_outside_the_format_is_unavailable`, `::test_rf3_a_fixture_still_holding_source_text_is_refused_as_unsanitized`, `::test_rf3_an_export_wrapped_by_hand_as_a_fixture_is_refused`, `::test_rf3_a_fixture_edited_to_hold_a_secret_is_refused_by_reading` |
| RF4 | One export built twice; a fixture re-recorded once and twice | Byte-identical every time | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf4_a_build_is_deterministic_and_rerecording_is_byte_identical` |
| RF5 | RF1's fixture; the same history with the agent's case on its first transition; a history carrying every kind of label (an owner's operation ref, resource and mapping versions and rule notes, a reference and a hostname target, an incident's version and a context writer the owner named beside the catalog's, the case, an operator's change number as the settlement reason, a comment, stop reasons and a principal label); the same with the ref `isolate` and the versions `1` and `2` beside a Cedar comment holding only numbers; the owner's mapping version and ref named in a Cedar comment; settlement reasons that are and are not kernel codes; labels that are also kept words: the ref `contain` (the vendor's operation and the rule id), the comments `approved`, `production` and `APPROVED` and the stop reason `incident`; a comment or a stop reason that reads a word the fixture writes itself: its own field names `complete`, `expected`, `baseline`, `format`, `labels` and `provenance`, its expectations' `UNRESOLVED` and `EFFECT`, the journal key's id `replay-fixture`, and a case link pseudonym's `https`, `case` and `invalid`; a comment that reads a Cedar word (`permit`, `forbid`, `when`, `principal`, `resource`, `Action`) or the kernel reason code `PROVIDER_RESPONSE` the history's settlement writes; a comment that parses as JSON naming the resource beside a Cedar comment that names it; a signal fact's and other facts' source versions; a comment that reads `host-000001` or `resource-000001`, the names the history's one host and one resource are given, with the survival search set aside and with it, and a comment that reads `host-000002`, a name no label is given | No source UUID, dashed or undashed, in any case, and no resource id, idempotency key, stop reason, approval comment or principal label survives, every idempotency key being a pseudonym; the labelled principal's payload is empty; every UUID is a ranked pseudonym; the journal is re-signed under key id `replay-fixture`; an opaque digest's pseudonym never spells a small-integer UUID. The case becomes `source-000001`, `case-000001` and `https://case.invalid/url-000001`, and a catalog ref is kept. Every kind becomes its typed pseudonym, the journal's expected version included, and the fixture checks and re-records clean; the short ref and versions are replaced only where they are whole, so `endpoint.isolate`, the journal's numbers and the Cedar comment's numbers are untouched, while the version and ref named in a Cedar comment become the pseudonyms their positions got, in the bundle and the candidate alike. `PROVIDER_RESPONSE` and `INCONCLUSIVE_JOURNAL` are kept and any other reason replaced. Every fact's source version but a signal fact's, and the journal's resulting version, become version pseudonyms; the owner's writer becomes `writer-000001` and the catalog's is kept, each fact's writers still sorted. A label that is a kept word becomes its pseudonym where it was recorded while the plan's and journal's operation, the rule id, the payload field, the fact's value and Cedar's `production`, the state and the source kind stay as they are, and each such fixture checks and re-records clean; each word the fixture writes itself becomes the comment's or the reason's pseudonym where it was recorded and stays where the fixture wrote it, and the fixture checks and re-records clean; a comment that is a Cedar word or a kernel reason code becomes `comment-000001` while every bundle's and candidate's Cedar text and the settlement's `PROVIDER_RESPONSE` stay as they were, and the fixture checks and re-records clean; the JSON comment's key lends no kept word, so the Cedar comment's resource id is replaced; no field a label is recorded at is a vocabulary field; each label is replaced once, from its own text, so the host, the resource (the journal's included) and the comment keep their own pseudonyms with the survival search set aside, a comment whose text is the host's or the resource's pseudonym is refused `FIXTURE_NOT_SANITIZED` with it, and one reading `host-000002` checks clean | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf5_no_source_identifier_or_label_survives`, `::test_rf5_each_label_is_replaced_once_from_its_own_text`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_reads_as_another_labels_pseudonym_is_refused`, `::test_rf5_the_agents_case_on_the_first_transition_never_reaches_a_fixture`, `::test_rf5_every_kind_of_label_is_replaced_by_its_typed_pseudonym`, `::test_rf5_a_short_version_or_a_ref_inside_a_catalog_name_is_replaced_only_where_it_is_one`, `::test_rf5_an_owners_version_or_ref_inside_policy_text_is_replaced_there_too`, `::test_rf5_a_kernel_reason_code_is_kept_and_any_other_reason_is_replaced`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_also_a_kept_word_is_replaced_only_where_it_was_recorded`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_a_word_the_fixture_writes_itself_still_becomes_a_fixture`, `::test_rf5_a_label_that_is_a_cedar_word_or_a_kernel_reason_code_stays_where_the_kernel_wrote_it`, `::test_rf5_text_a_person_typed_lends_no_kept_word`, `::test_rf5_a_signal_facts_source_version_is_kept_and_any_other_facts_is_a_label`, `::test_rf5_no_label_position_is_a_field_the_fixture_keeps`, `::test_rf5_the_principal_label_and_other_informational_text_never_reach_a_fixture`, `::test_rf5_the_journal_is_re_signed_under_a_published_fixture_key`, `::test_rf5_an_opaque_digest_pseudonym_never_spells_a_small_identifier` |
| RF6 | A candidate with a secret-shaped rule note; a candidate for another tenant, or nine candidates; a source checked against the wrong head | `PRIVATE_DATA` with nothing written and nothing quoted; `INVALID_CANDIDATE`; `HEAD_MISMATCH` | `tests/test_replay_fixture.py::test_rf6_a_secret_shaped_candidate_note_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written`, `::test_rf6_a_candidate_for_another_tenant_or_too_many_candidates_are_refused`, `::test_rf6_a_source_that_does_not_verify_is_refused_with_its_code` |

## Scheduled audit-chain verification (AV1 to AV7)

`audit-verify` reads every workspace's chain on the live database again, as the runtime role with no credential, and places each retained checkpoint on it; the jobs service runs it hourly ([operations](OPERATIONS.md#verify-the-audit-chain-on-a-schedule)). The tests damage a real chain in PostgreSQL with the owner role.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AV1 | Every seeded workspace's intact chain, one with a held request; the same chain read two events at a time; an event altered near its end, read that way; an event appended from another connection after the walk's first event | Every chain verifies, the report names no workspace, nothing is logged as `audit.chain_failed` and no table changes; the paged read reaches the same result, and the late alteration is found at its own sequence; the appended event is not seen and the chain still ends at the head read first | `tests/test_audit_verify.py::test_av1_every_intact_chain_verifies_and_the_pass_writes_nothing`, `::test_av1_a_chain_is_read_a_page_at_a_time_and_a_late_break_is_found_where_it_is`, `::test_av1_events_appended_while_a_chain_is_read_do_not_break_it` |
| AV2 | One workspace's chain with an event altered, two events reordered, an interior event deleted, the tail truncated, or the stored head substituted; a rewrite that breaks one link, or shifts every later sequence, and re-hashes everything after it; the head moved back one with its event left in place | That workspace alone fails `CHAIN_BROKEN` at the sequence of the damage, the other verifies, and one `audit.chain_failed` line at `ERROR` carries only its tenant, code and sequence; each rewrite is found at the changed event, by the link check and the sequence check respectively; the row past the head breaks the chain at its own sequence | `tests/test_audit_verify.py::test_av2_a_damaged_chain_fails_alone_and_is_logged_with_its_code_and_sequence`, `::test_av2_a_rewrite_that_re_hashes_everything_after_still_fails_at_the_change`, `::test_av2_rows_past_the_stored_head_break_the_chain_at_the_first_of_them` |
| AV3 | Retained heads at genesis, at the first event and at the current head; a head from the future; a forged hash at a retained sequence and at genesis; a broken chain with a valid retained head; the tail removed with the head moved back to match, against the head retained before; a retained head for a workspace the directory does not hold, with every workspace reached and with named workspaces only | Every retained head is placed and the chain passes; `BEHIND_CHECKPOINT` and `DIVERGES_FROM_CHECKPOINT` at their sequences, genesis included; `CHAIN_BROKEN` outranks the checkpoint; the consistent truncation verifies alone and fails `BEHIND_CHECKPOINT` against the retained head; the missing workspace fails `BEHIND_CHECKPOINT` once every workspace was reached, and with named workspaces its head is counted as not placed | `tests/test_audit_verify.py::test_av3_retained_checkpoints_are_placed_on_the_chain`, `::test_av3_a_consistent_truncation_verifies_alone_and_fails_against_a_retained_head`, `::test_av3_genesis_divergence_and_a_broken_chain_outrank_the_checkpoints`, `::test_av3_a_retained_head_for_a_workspace_the_directory_no_longer_holds_fails` |

| AV6 | `execbound audit-verify --all-tenants` with a checkpoints directory, `--tenant`, an altered chain; no mode, both modes, a budget of 0; a malformed `--checkpoint`; a schema mismatch, a database error and the directory walk's own refusal | Exit 0 with the report; exit 1 with the report and a sentence naming the counts; exit 2 for each misuse; exit 2 with `INVALID_CHECKPOINT` before the database is read; exit 2 with the runtime check's sentence, `database error` or `UNSAFE_JOB_POLICY`, never a traceback | `tests/test_audit_verify.py::test_av6_the_command_exits_0_when_every_chain_verifies_and_1_when_one_does_not`, `::test_av6_the_modes_are_exclusive_and_the_budget_bounded`, `::test_av6_a_checkpoint_it_cannot_use_stops_the_run_before_the_database_is_read`, `::test_av6_a_schema_mismatch_or_a_database_error_exits_2_without_a_traceback` |

## Findings delivery (FD1 to FD15)

Each monitoring finding is sent once to every configured destination whose `classes` list `findings` and whose `rules` and `spaces` admit it, from `monitor-process --deliver` ([operations](OPERATIONS.md#send-monitoring-findings-to-destinations), [Tines monitoring](TINES_MONITORING.md#findings-delivery)). The router, drain, command, migration and schema tests run on real PostgreSQL; the configuration, message and egress tests need no database. They use the loopback receiver, the SMTP double with a CA the tests mint, the Slack and Teams fakes, and a stand-in resolver and network for the egress guard; nothing reaches a real host. FD12's deadlines in seconds are measured by `tests/test_egress_deadline.py`, which takes the machine's timing lock and is marked `timed`, so a run that selects `not timed` leaves it out while FD12's deadline tests, which read no clock, still run.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FD1 | One finding and destinations that admit it by class, rule and Space, beside ones that list another rule, another Space, approvals alone or another workspace; a delivery another router already wrote; two routers at once, then a third; a router holding the intent mid-transaction beside a second; authority revoked while a router writes, then the scheduled pass; a limit of one with two intents waiting, and limits outside 1 to 50; a clock with no zone | One delivery per admitting destination, with the id `uuid5(finding, destination + "#finding")`, and the intent `ROUTED` with its own id; nothing more from the second, the concurrent or the third router, and the second passes the held intent by without waiting and writes nothing; the revoked router is refused `INVALID_ADMIN` and every row it wrote rolls back, and the scheduled pass then routes; one intent is routed and the other waits; `INVALID_LIMIT`; `INVALID_CLOCK`, with nothing written | `tests/test_findings_delivery.py::test_fd1_routing_writes_one_delivery_per_admitted_destination`, `::test_fd1_a_second_router_or_two_at_once_add_nothing`, `::test_fd1_a_router_passes_by_an_intent_another_router_holds`, `::test_fd1_the_scheduled_pass_routes_and_authority_lost_mid_route_writes_nothing`, `::test_the_router_routes_no_more_intents_than_its_limit`, `::test_the_router_refuses_a_limit_outside_one_to_fifty`, `::test_the_router_reads_its_clock_through_the_monitoring_check` |

## Trusted browsers (TB1 to TB10)

A person who verifies their second factor at sign-in may trust that browser for thirty days, so its later sign-ins, and its entries into the account's other workspaces, skip the code (design). The trust satisfies the sign-in factor and nothing more: a step-up still asks, a security change ends it, My account lists and removes it, and a workspace administrator may forbid it. The review step a notification's workspace-scoped address opens is the hosted service's row. Real PostgreSQL throughout; the browser rows run in Chromium.

| ID | Setup/action | Required result | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TB1 | A sign-in verified by an authenticator code, a recovery code and a passkey with "Trust this browser for 30 days" ticked; a password sign-in on the same browser next, and a Google stand-in's callback; the trust's label from a Chrome on Windows User-Agent; a session holding its own verification | The cookie is HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, `Path=/`, about thirty days long and `tb1.`-shaped; the next sign-in's `GET /mfa` answers 303 to the landing with new session and CSRF cookies and no account cookie; the session's assurance is `trusted` with the original `verified_at`, never now, and the workspace's chain records `account_security.trusted_sign_in` with the session, the trust and the original method and time, which a code sign-in and a tampered cookie never record; the provider's sign-in is trusted the same way; the trust is listed as "Chrome on Windows" | `tests/test_trusted_browser_sign_in.py::test_tb_ticking_the_box_sets_the_cookie_and_the_next_sign_in_skips_the_code`, `::test_tb_provider_sign_in_honours_trust`, `::test_tb_the_trust_lists_the_browser_that_was_trusted`; `tests/test_trusted_browsers.py::test_tb_grant_then_apply_skips_the_factor_with_the_original_time`, `::test_tb_a_session_holding_its_own_verification_keeps_it` |
| TB2 | The box left unticked; `trust=on` posted from Account security once signed in, and any other value; a tampered cookie; the code prompt of a workspace that forbids trust | No cookie is set and the next sign-in asks for the code; Account security shows no box, grants nothing for `on` and answers 403 for anything else; a tampered cookie shows the code prompt; a forbidding workspace's code, recovery and passkey forms offer no box | `tests/test_trusted_browser_sign_in.py::test_tb_an_unticked_box_sets_no_cookie`, `::test_tb_the_box_is_not_offered_once_signed_in`, `::test_tb_a_tampered_cookie_shows_the_code_prompt`, `::test_tb_a_workspace_that_forbids_trust_offers_no_box` |
| TB3 | A trusted sign-in whose trust was granted two days earlier, then a step-up-guarded change; the store's recent-factor check and the database's `fresh` read for a trusted assurance, on an account session and a legacy one | The change answers 401 `MFA_REQUIRED` until a real code is entered; `RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` from the store, and the definer functions refuse the trusted assurance as stale | `tests/test_trusted_browser_sign_in.py::test_tb_trusted_sign_in_still_asks_for_step_up`; `tests/test_trusted_browsers.py::test_tb_a_trusted_session_still_fails_the_recent_factor_check`, `::test_tb_a_trusted_assurance_is_not_fresh_for_a_legacy_session` |
| TB4 | Two trusted browsers, then "Sign out other devices" from one; the same from a session of that person with no account sign-in, a setup credential's; a replaced authenticator; another account's cookie, a password change, thirty days, removal and a workspace that forbids trust | Both browsers ask for the code again; from the session with no account sign-in, the account's version moves, the trust no longer applies, every account sign-in of the account ends and the acting session stays; the replaced factor's trust, still unrevoked and unexpired, no longer applies; each refusal case leaves the session pending and writes nothing | `tests/test_trusted_browser_sign_in.py::test_tb_sign_out_other_devices_ends_every_trust`, `::test_tb_a_replaced_factor_ends_trust`; `tests/test_trusted_browsers.py::test_tb_trust_is_refused_when`, `::test_tb_sign_out_other_devices_from_a_session_with_no_account_sign_in_ends_trust` |
| TB5 | Granting from a stale, trusted, wrong-method or earlier verification and in a forbidding workspace; an eleventh trust; two grants at once at ten; a sign-in whose grant is refused | Each grant is refused and nothing is stored; the oldest trust is revoked, leaving ten; ten live trusts after the race; the sign-in still completes, with no cookie | `tests/test_trusted_browsers.py::test_tb_grant_is_refused_when`, `::test_tb_an_eleventh_trust_revokes_the_oldest`, `::test_tb_two_grants_at_once_leave_ten_live_trusts`; `tests/test_trusted_browser_sign_in.py::test_tb_a_refused_grant_still_signs_in` |
| TB6 | The table read and written as the runtime role under another account's setting and its own; `0101_trusted_browsers` applied and rolled back | Another account's rows are invisible and the runtime writes none, only the definer functions do; the downgrade drops the table, the functions and the column, deletes every `trusted` assurance and restores the method check and the three patched definitions | `tests/test_trusted_browsers.py::test_tb_the_table_is_account_isolated_and_runtime_read_only`, `::test_tb_downgrade_drops_everything_and_restores_the_method_check` |
